Prologue  (source worksheet: logion-000-prologue.md)

> DRAFT — working translation from the Coptic (and Greek where it survives), prepared with AI assistance; not reviewed by a Coptic scholar; not finished. Corrections invited and credited.

# Prologue

- **Witness status:** Greek+Coptic (P.Oxy. 654)
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek (P.Oxy. 654, Grenfell & Hunt 1904, public domain)
Οὗτοι οἱ λόγοι οἱ ἀπόκρυφοι οὓς ἐλάλησεν Ἰησοῦς ὁ ζῶν καὶ ἔγραψεν Δίδυμος Ἰούδας Θωμᾶς.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0)
ⲛⲁⲉⲓ ⲛⲉ ⲛ̄ϣⲁϫⲉ ⲉⲑⲏⲡ ⲉⲛⲧⲁ ⲓⲥ ⲉⲧⲟⲛϩ ϫⲟⲟⲩ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲁϥⲥϩⲁⲓ ⲥⲟⲩ ⲛ̄ϭⲓ ⲇⲓⲇⲩⲙⲟⲥ ⲓⲟⲩⲇⲁⲥ ⲑⲱⲙⲁⲥ

## English draft
These are the hidden sayings that the living Jesus spoke, and Didymus Judas Thomas wrote them down.

## Notes
- "Hidden/secret" — Coptic ⲉⲑⲏⲡ, Greek ἀπόκρυφοι: kept "hidden" to match both witnesses plainly.
- G1: checked against Greek and Coptic token order; no divergence beyond word order.

# Logion 1

- **Witness status:** Greek+Coptic (P.Oxy. 654)
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek (P.Oxy. 654, Grenfell & Hunt 1904, public domain)
καὶ εἶπεν· ὃς ἂν εὕρῃ τὴν ἑρμηνείαν τῶν λόγων τούτων, θανάτου οὐ μὴ γεύσηται.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0)
ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲉϫⲁϥ ϫⲉ ⲡⲉⲧ ⲁ ϩⲉ ⲉ ⲑⲉⲣⲙⲏⲛⲉⲓⲁ ⲛ̄ⲛⲉⲉⲓϣⲁϫⲉ ϥⲛⲁ ϫⲓϯⲡⲉ ⲁⲛ ⲙ̄ ⲡⲙⲟⲩ

## English draft
And he said: Whoever finds the meaning of these sayings will not taste death.

## Notes
- "Meaning" for ἑρμηνεία / ⲑⲉⲣⲙⲏⲛⲉⲓⲁ (also "interpretation"); fork noted, "meaning" chosen as the plainer word.
- G1: Greek and Coptic agree closely here; no smoothing done.

Logion 2  (source worksheet: logion-002.md)

> DRAFT — working translation from the Coptic (and Greek where it survives), prepared with AI assistance; not reviewed by a Coptic scholar; not finished. Corrections invited and credited.

# Logion 2

- **Witness status:** Greek+Coptic (P.Oxy. 654)
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek (P.Oxy. 654, Grenfell & Hunt 1904, public domain)
λέγει Ἰησοῦς· μὴ παυσάσθω ὁ ζητῶν τοῦ ζητεῖν ἕως ἂν εὕρῃ, καὶ ὅταν εὕρῃ θαμβηθήσεται, καὶ θαμβηθεὶς βασιλεύσει, καὶ βασιλεύσας ἀναπαήσεται.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0)
ⲡⲉϫⲉ ⲓⲥ ⲙⲛ̄ⲧⲣⲉϥⲗⲟ ⲛ̄ϭⲓ ⲡⲉⲧϣⲓⲛⲉ ⲉϥϣⲓⲛⲉ ϣⲁⲛⲧⲉϥϭⲓⲛⲉ ⲁⲩⲱ ϩⲟⲧⲁⲛ ⲉϥϣⲁⲛϭⲓⲛⲉ ϥⲛⲁϣⲧⲣ̄ⲧⲣ̄ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲉϥϣⲁⲛϣⲧⲟⲣⲧⲣ̄ ϥⲛⲁ ⲣ̄ϣⲡⲏⲣⲉ ⲁⲩⲱ ϥⲛⲁ ⲣ̄ⲣ̄ⲣⲟ ⲉϫⲙ̄ ⲡⲧⲏⲣϥ

## English draft
Jesus said: Let the one who seeks keep on seeking until he finds. When he finds, he will be troubled. When he has been troubled, he will marvel, and he will rule over everything.

## Notes
- Divergence flagged, not smoothed: the Greek ends "...will rule, and having ruled, will rest"; the Coptic ends at "rule over everything" with no "rest" clause. Draft follows the Coptic ending; Greek ending recorded here.
- G1: fork named; both roads on the page.

Logion 3  (source worksheet: logion-003.md)
========================================================================

> DRAFT — working translation from the Coptic (and Greek where it survives), prepared with AI assistance; not reviewed by a Coptic scholar; not finished. Corrections invited and credited.

# Logion 3

- **Witness status:** Greek+Coptic (P.Oxy. 654)
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek (P.Oxy. 654, Grenfell & Hunt 1904, public domain; fragmentary)
λέγει Ἰησοῦς· ἐὰν οἱ ἕλκοντες ὑμᾶς εἴπωσιν ὑμῖν Ἰδοὺ ἡ βασιλεία ἐν οὐρανῷ, ὑμᾶς φθήσεται τὰ πετεινὰ τοῦ οὐρανοῦ· ἐὰν εἴπωσιν ὅτι ὑπὸ τὴν γῆν ἐστιν, ὑμᾶς φθήσεται εἰσελθεῖν οἱ ἰχθύες τῆς θαλάσσης, καὶ ἡ βασιλεία τοῦ θεοῦ ἐντὸς ὑμῶν ἐστι κἀκτός.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0)
ⲡⲉϫⲉ ⲓⲥ ϫⲉ ⲉⲩϣⲁϫⲟⲟⲥ ⲛⲏⲧⲛ̄ ⲛ̄ϭⲓ ⲛⲉⲧⲥⲱⲕ ϩⲏⲧⲧⲏⲩⲧⲛ̄ ϫⲉ ⲉⲓⲥϩⲏⲏⲧⲉ ⲉⲧⲙⲛⲧⲉⲣⲟ ϩⲛ̄ ⲧⲡⲉ ⲉⲉⲓⲉ ⲛ̄ϩⲁⲗⲏⲧ ⲛⲁ ⲣ̄ϣⲟⲣⲡ ⲉⲣⲱⲧⲛ̄ ⲛ̄ⲧⲉ ⲧⲡⲉ ⲉⲩϣⲁⲛϫⲟⲟⲥ ⲛⲏⲧⲛ ϫⲉ ⲥ ϩⲛ̄ ⲑⲁⲗⲁⲥⲥⲁ ⲉⲉⲓⲉ ⲛ̄ⲧⲃⲧ ⲛⲁ ⲣ̄ ϣⲟⲣⲡ ⲉⲣⲱⲧⲛ̄ ⲁⲗⲗⲁ ⲧⲙⲛ̄ⲧⲉⲣⲟ ⲥ ⲙ̄ⲡⲉⲧⲛ̄ϩⲟⲩⲛ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲥ ⲙ̄ ⲡⲉⲧⲛ̄ⲃⲁⲗ ϩⲟⲧⲁⲛ ⲉⲧⲉⲧⲛ̄ϣⲁⲛ ⲥⲟⲩⲱⲛⲧⲏⲩⲧⲛ̄ ⲧⲟⲧⲉ ⲥⲉⲛⲁⲥⲟⲩⲱⲛⲧⲏⲛⲉ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲧⲉⲧⲛ ⲁⲉⲓⲙⲉ ϫⲉ ⲛ̄ⲧⲱⲧⲛ̄ ⲡⲉ ⲛ̄ϣⲏⲣⲉ ⲙ̄ⲡⲉⲓⲱⲧ ⲉⲧⲟⲛϩ ⲉϣⲱⲡⲉ ⲇⲉ ⲧⲉⲧ ⲛⲁⲥⲟⲩⲱⲛⲧⲏⲩⲧⲛ̄ ⲁⲛ ⲉⲉⲓⲉ ⲧⲉⲧⲛ̄ϣⲟⲟⲡ ϩⲛ̄ ⲟⲩⲙⲛ̄ⲧϩⲏⲕⲉ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛ̄ⲧⲱⲧⲛ̄ ⲡⲉ ⲧⲙⲛ̄ⲧϩⲏⲕⲉ

## English draft
Jesus said: If those who lead you say to you, "Look, the kingdom is in heaven," then the birds of the sky will get there before you. If they say to you, "It is in the sea," then the fish will get there before you. But the kingdom is inside you and outside you. When you know yourselves, then you will be known, and you will understand that you are the children of the living Father. But if you do not know yourselves, then you live in poverty, and you yourselves are that poverty.

## Notes
- "Those who lead you" — Coptic ⲛⲉⲧⲥⲱⲕ ϩⲏⲧⲧⲏⲩⲧⲛ̄ ("those who draw/pull you"); Greek οἱ ἕλκοντες ὑμᾶς matches ("those who draw you"). Kept plain.
- Greek says "under the earth" where Coptic says "in the sea"; draft follows the Coptic, fork recorded here.
- Reader-lens note (not a rendering rule): this is one of Robert's resonant logia; the rendering above was made from the witnesses only.
- G1: forks named; poverty wordplay (ⲙⲛ̄ⲧϩⲏⲕⲉ ... ⲧⲙⲛ̄ⲧϩⲏⲕⲉ) kept in English.

> DRAFT — working translation from the Coptic (and Greek where it survives), prepared with AI assistance; not reviewed by a Coptic scholar; not finished. Corrections invited and credited.

# Logion 4

- **Witness status:** Greek+Coptic (P.Oxy. 654)
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek (P.Oxy. 654, Grenfell & Hunt 1904, public domain; fragmentary)
λέγει Ἰησοῦς· οὐ μὴ [...] ἄνθρωπος πρεσβύτης ἐν ταῖς ἡμέραις αὐτοῦ ἐρωτῆσαι παιδίον μικρὸν ἑπτὰ ἡμερῶν περὶ τοῦ τόπου τῆς ζωῆς, καὶ ζήσεται· ὅτι πολλοὶ ἔσονται πρῶτοι ἔσχατοι καὶ ἔσχατοι πρῶτοι, καὶ ἔσονται εἷς [...]

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0)
ⲡⲉϫⲉ ⲓⲥ ϥⲛⲁϫⲛⲁⲩ ⲁⲛ ⲛ̄ϭⲓ ⲡⲣⲱⲙⲉ ⲛ̄ϩⲗⲗⲟ ϩⲛ̄ ⲛⲉϥϩⲟⲟⲩ ⲉϫⲛⲉ ⲟⲩⲕⲟⲩⲉⲓ ⲛ̄ϣⲏⲣⲉ ϣⲏⲙ ⲉϥ ϩⲛ̄ ⲥⲁϣϥ̅ ⲛ̄ϩⲟⲟⲩ ⲉⲧⲃⲉ ⲡⲧⲟⲡⲟⲥ ⲙ̄ⲡⲱⲛϩ ⲁⲩⲱ ϥⲛⲁⲱⲛϩ ϫⲉ ⲟⲩⲛ ϩⲁϩ ⲛϣⲟⲣⲡ ⲛⲁ ⲣ̄ϩⲁⲉ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛ̄ⲥⲉϣⲱⲡⲉ ⲟⲩⲁ ⲟⲩⲱⲧ

## English draft
Jesus said: The old man in his days will not hold back from asking a little child of seven days about the place of life, and he will live. For many who are first will become last, and they will become a single one.

## Notes
- "Will not hold back" — Coptic ϥⲛⲁϫⲛⲁⲩ ⲁⲛ, "will not hesitate"; kept plain.
- Greek fragment breaks off at "they will be one..."; Coptic completes "a single one." Both recorded; draft follows Coptic.
- G1: lacuna in Greek marked; no silent completion presented as Greek.

# Logion 5

- **Witness status:** Greek+Coptic (P.Oxy. 654)
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek (P.Oxy. 654, Grenfell & Hunt 1904, public domain; fragmentary)
λέγει Ἰησοῦς· γνῶθι τὸ ὂν ἔμπροσθεν τοῦ προσώπου σου, καὶ τὸ κρυπτόν σοι ἀποκαλυφθήσεται· οὐ γάρ ἐστιν κρυπτὸν ὃ οὐ φανερὸν γενήσεται.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0)
ⲡⲉϫⲉ ⲓⲥ ⲥⲟⲩⲱⲛ ⲡⲉⲧ ⲙ̄ⲡⲙ̄ⲧⲟ ⲙ̄ⲡⲉⲕϩⲟ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲉⲑⲏⲡ ⲉⲣⲟⲕ ϥⲛⲁϭⲱⲗⲡ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲛⲁⲕ ⲙⲛ̄ ⲗⲁⲁⲩ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲉϥ ϩⲏⲡ ⲉϥ ⲛⲁⲟⲩⲱⲛϩ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲁⲛ

## English draft
Jesus said: Know what is in front of your face, and what is hidden from you will be shown to you. For there is nothing hidden that will not be made plain.

## Notes
- "Made plain" for ϥⲛⲁⲟⲩⲱⲛϩ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ / φανερὸν γενήσεται; "revealed" also honest — fork noted, plainer word kept.
- The base-file token stream for this logion ends at "will not be made plain"; draft follows the base file exactly, nothing imported from check-only translations.
- Reader-lens note (not a rendering rule): one of Robert's resonant logia; rendering made from the witnesses only.
- G1: checked token-by-token against the base file.

Logion 6  (source worksheet: logion-006.md)
========================================================================

> DRAFT — working translation from the Coptic (and Greek where it survives), prepared with AI assistance; not reviewed by a Coptic scholar; not finished. Corrections invited and credited.

# Logion 6

- **Witness status:** Greek+Coptic, fragmentary (P.Oxy. 654)
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek (P.Oxy. 654, Grenfell & Hunt 1904, public domain; fragmentary)

P.Oxy. 654 preserves only scraps of this logion; the papyrus is broken and most of the Greek is lost. The question about fasting and prayer survives in part; the rest does not. No continuous Greek is printed here, because none survives. The Coptic below carries the saying.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0)

ⲁⲩϫⲛⲟⲩϥ ⲛ̄ϭⲓ ⲛⲉϥⲙⲁⲑⲏⲧⲏⲥ ⲡⲉϫⲁⲩ ⲛⲁϥ ϫⲉ ⲕⲟⲩⲱϣ ⲉⲧⲣⲛ̄ⲣⲛⲏⲥⲧⲉⲩⲉ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲉϣ ⲧⲉ ⲑⲉ ⲉⲛⲁϣⲗⲏⲗ ⲉⲛⲁϯⲉⲗⲉⲏⲙⲟⲥⲩⲛⲏ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲉⲛⲁⲣⲡⲁⲣⲁⲧⲏⲣⲉⲓ ⲉⲟⲩ ⲛ̄ϭⲓ ⲟⲩⲱⲙ ⲡⲉϫⲉ ⲓⲥ ϫⲉ ⲙ̄ⲡⲣ̄ϫⲉϭⲟⲗ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲉⲧⲉⲧⲙ̄ⲙⲟⲥⲧⲉ ⲙ̄ⲙⲟϥ ⲙ̄ⲡⲣ̄ⲁⲁϥ ϫⲉ ⲥⲉϭⲟⲗⲡ ⲧⲏⲣⲟⲩ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲙ̄ⲡⲉⲙⲧⲟ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲛ̄ⲧⲡⲉ ⲙⲛ̄ⲗⲁⲁⲩ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲉϥϩⲏⲡ ⲉϥⲛⲁⲟⲩⲱⲛϩ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲁⲛ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲙⲛⲗⲁⲁⲩ ⲉϥϩⲟⲃⲥ ⲉⲩⲛⲁϭⲱ ⲟⲩⲉϣⲛ̄ ϭⲟⲗⲡϥ

## English draft

His disciples asked him and said to him: Do you want us to fast? And how shall we pray and give to the poor? And what rule for food shall we keep? Jesus said: Do not tell lies. And what you hate, do not do it. For everything is laid bare in front of heaven. For there is nothing hidden that will not be shown, and nothing covered that will stay covered without being shown.

## Notes

- Fasting word is the Greek loan ⲣⲛⲏⲥⲧⲉⲩⲉ (to fast); "give to the poor" renders ϯⲉⲗⲉⲏⲙⲟⲥⲩⲛⲏ (give alms) in plain words.
- "Rule for food" — Coptic ⲣⲡⲁⲣⲁⲧⲏⲣⲉⲓ ⲉⲟⲩ ⲛ̄ϭⲓⲛⲟⲩⲱⲙ, literally "observe what in eating"; kept as a food-rule question, fork noted.
- "Laid bare in front of heaven" — ⲥⲉϭⲟⲗⲡ ⲧⲏⲣⲟⲩ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲙ̄ⲡⲉⲙⲧⲟ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲛ̄ⲧⲡⲉ; the draft keeps the heaven phrase rather than smoothing it away.
- Greek is too broken to control this logion word by word; the draft follows the Coptic, and the loss is stated on the page rather than hidden.
- G1: checked against the base-file sentences for this logion; question-and-answer order kept; no canonical parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

> DRAFT — working translation from the Coptic (and Greek where it survives), prepared with AI assistance; not reviewed by a Coptic scholar; not finished. Corrections invited and credited.

# Logion 7

- **Witness status:** Greek+Coptic, fragmentary (P.Oxy. 654)
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek (P.Oxy. 654, Grenfell & Hunt 1904, public domain; fragmentary)

Only the opening beatitude word of this logion is legible in the Greek scrap; the rest of P.Oxy. 654 for this saying is lost. No continuous Greek is printed here, because none survives. The Coptic below carries the saying.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0)

ⲡⲉϫⲉ ⲓⲥ ⲟⲩⲙⲁⲕⲁⲣⲓⲟⲥ ⲡⲉ ⲡⲙⲟⲩⲉⲓ ⲡⲁⲉⲓ ⲉⲧⲉ ⲡⲣⲱⲙⲉ ⲛⲁⲟⲩⲟⲙϥ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛ̄ⲧⲉ ⲡⲙⲟⲩⲉⲓ ϣⲱⲡⲉ ⲣ̄ⲣⲱⲙⲉ ⲁⲩⲱ ϥⲃⲏⲧ ⲛ̄ϭⲓ ⲡⲣⲱⲙⲉ ⲡⲁⲉⲓ ⲉⲧⲉ ⲡⲙⲟⲩⲉⲓ ⲛⲁⲟⲩⲟⲙϥ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲙⲟⲩⲉⲓ ⲛⲁϣⲱⲡⲉ ⲣ̄ⲣⲱⲙⲉ

## English draft

Jesus said: Blessed is the lion that the man will eat, and the lion will become a man. And cursed is the man that the lion will eat, and the lion will become a man.

## Notes

- "Blessed" renders the Greek loan ⲙⲁⲕⲁⲣⲓⲟⲥ, kept plain.
- "Cursed" renders ϥⲃⲏⲧ (the man is held foul/cursed); "wretched" is also honest — fork noted, "cursed" kept to match the blessed/cursed pair.
- The Coptic ends both halves the same way — the lion becomes a man — and the draft keeps that sameness instead of varying it for style.
- Greek control is effectively absent here beyond the opening word; the draft follows the Coptic alone in substance and says so.
- G1: checked against the base-file sentences; the two eat/become clauses kept in Coptic order; no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

Logion 8  (source worksheet: logion-008.md)

> DRAFT — working translation from the Coptic (and Greek where it survives), prepared with AI assistance; not reviewed by a Coptic scholar; not finished. Corrections invited and credited.

# Logion 8

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0)

ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲉϫⲁϥ ϫⲉ ⲉⲡⲣⲱⲙⲉ ⲧⲛ̄ⲧⲱⲛ ⲁⲩⲟⲩⲱϩⲉ ⲣ̄ⲣⲙⲛ̄ϩⲏⲧ ⲡⲁⲉⲓ ⲛ̄ⲧⲁϩⲛⲟⲩϫⲉ ⲛ̄ⲧⲉϥⲁⲃⲱ ⲉⲑⲁⲗⲁⲥⲥⲁ ⲁϥⲥⲱⲕ ⲙ̄ⲙⲟⲥ ⲉϩⲣⲁⲓ̈ ϩⲛ̄ⲑⲁⲗⲁⲥⲥⲁ ⲉⲥⲙⲉϩ ⲛⲧⲃⲧ ⲛ̄ⲕⲟⲩⲉⲓ ⲛ̄ ϩⲣⲁⲓ̈ ⲛ̄ϩⲏⲧⲟⲩ ⲁϥϩⲉ ⲁⲩⲛⲟϭ ⲛ̄ⲧⲃⲧ ⲉⲛⲁⲛⲟⲩϥ ⲛ̄ϭⲓ ⲡⲟⲩⲱϩⲉ ⲣ̄ⲣⲙⲛϩⲏⲧ ⲁϥⲛⲟⲩϫⲉ ⲛ̄ⲛ̄ⲕⲟⲩⲉⲓ ⲧⲏⲣⲟⲩ ⲛⲧⲃⲧ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲉⲡⲉⲥⲏⲧ ⲉⲑⲁⲗⲁⲥⲥⲁ ⲁϥⲥⲱⲧⲡ ⲙ̄ⲡⲛⲟϭ ⲛ̄ⲧⲃⲧ ⲭⲱⲣⲓⲥ ϩⲓⲥⲉ ⲡⲉⲧⲉ ⲟⲩⲛⲙⲁⲁϫⲉ ⲙ̄ⲙⲟϥ ⲉⲥⲱⲧⲙ̄ ⲙⲁⲣⲉϥⲥⲱⲧⲙ

## English draft

And he said: The man is like a wise fisherman. He threw his net into the sea. He pulled it up from the sea, full of small fish among them. The wise fisherman found a large good fish. He threw all the small fish down into the sea. He chose the large fish without trouble. Whoever has ears to hear, let him hear.

## Notes

- "Wise fisherman" — ⲟⲩⲱϩⲉ ⲣ̄ⲣⲙⲛ̄ϩⲏⲧ, literally a fisherman who is a man of understanding; kept plain.
- "Net" is ⲁⲃⲱ; "pulled it up" renders ⲥⲱⲕ (drag/haul); "chose" renders ⲥⲱⲧⲡ.
- "Without trouble" — ⲭⲱⲣⲓⲥ ϩⲓⲥⲉ, "without labor/toil"; fork noted.
- Closing ear formula kept as its own sentence, as in the Coptic.
- G1: checked against the base-file sentences; small-fish/large-fish order kept; no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

# Logion 9

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0)

ⲡⲉϫⲉ ⲓⲥ ϫⲉ ⲉⲓⲥϩⲏⲏⲧⲉ ⲁϥⲉⲓ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲛ̄ϭⲓ ⲡⲉⲧⲥⲓⲧⲉ ⲁϥⲙⲉϩⲧⲟⲟⲧϥ ⲁϥⲛⲟⲩϫⲉ ⲁϩⲟⲉⲓⲛⲉ ⲙⲉⲛ ϩⲉ ⲉϫⲛⲧⲉϩⲓⲏ ⲁⲩⲉⲓ ⲛ̄ϭⲓ ⲛ̄ϩⲁⲗⲁⲧⲉ ⲁⲩⲕⲁⲧϥⲟⲩ ϩⲛ̄ⲕⲟⲟⲩⲉ ⲁⲩϩⲉ ⲉϫⲛ̄ⲧⲡⲉⲧⲣⲁ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲙ̄ⲡⲟⲩϫⲉⲛⲟⲩⲛⲉ ⲉⲡⲉⲥⲏⲧ ⲉⲡⲕⲁϩ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲙ̄ⲡⲟⲩⲧⲉⲩⲉϩⲙⲥ ⲉϩⲣⲁⲓ̈ ⲉⲧⲡⲉ ⲁⲩⲱ ϩⲛ̄ⲕⲟⲟⲩⲉ ⲁⲩϩⲉ ⲉϫⲛ̄ⲛ̄ϣⲟⲛⲧⲉ ⲁⲩⲱϭⲧ ⲙ̄ⲡⲉϭⲣⲟϭ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲁⲡϥⲛⲧ ⲟⲩⲟⲙⲟⲩ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲁϩⲛ̄ⲕⲟⲟⲩⲉ ϩⲉ ⲉϫⲛ̄ⲡⲕⲁϩ ⲉⲧⲛⲁⲛⲟⲩϥ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲁϥϯⲕⲁⲣⲡⲟⲥ ⲉϩⲣⲁⲓ̈ ⲉⲧⲡⲉ ⲉⲛⲁⲛⲟⲩϥ ⲁϥⲉⲓ ⲛ̄ⲥⲉ ⲉⲥⲟⲧⲉ ⲁⲩⲱ ϣⲉϫⲟⲩⲱⲧ ⲉⲥⲟⲧⲉ

## English draft

Jesus said: Look, the sower went out. He filled his hand. He threw. Some fell on the road; the birds came and gathered them. Others fell on the rock; they did not take root down into the earth and did not send up ears to heaven. And others fell on the thorns; they choked the seed and the worm ate them. And others fell on the good earth, and it gave good fruit up to heaven; it came sixty per measure and a hundred twenty per measure.

## Notes

- "Sower" is ⲡⲉⲧⲥⲓⲧⲉ (the one who sows); "filled his hand" renders ⲙⲉϩⲧⲟⲟⲧϥ plainly.
- "Gathered them" — ⲕⲁⲧϥⲟⲩ (birds picked/gathered the seed); kept neutral rather than importing a devouring image the Coptic does not state here.
- "Take root" renders ϫⲓⲛⲟⲩⲛⲉ; "send up ears" renders ⲧⲉⲩⲉϩⲙⲥ (put out an ear of grain); both kept close to the Coptic verbs.
- Closing yield kept in the Coptic's own measure words — sixty per measure, a hundred twenty per measure — rather than smoothed into a single -fold figure.
- G1: checked against the base-file sentences; four fallings kept in order (road / rock / thorns / good earth); no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

ⲡⲉϫⲉ ⲓⲥ ϫⲉ ⲁⲉⲓⲛⲟⲩϫⲉ ⲛ̄ⲟⲩⲕⲱϩⲧ ⲉϫⲛ̄ⲡⲕⲟⲥⲙⲟⲥ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲉⲓⲥϩⲏⲏⲧⲉ ϯⲁⲣⲉϩ ⲉⲣⲟϥ ϣⲁⲛⲧⲉϥϫⲉⲣⲟ

# Logion 11

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0037–s0043, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉⲧⲉⲓⲡⲉ ⲛⲁⲣⲡⲁⲣⲁⲅⲉ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲧⲉⲧⲛⲧⲡⲉ ⲙⲙⲟⲥ ⲛⲁⲣⲡⲁⲣⲁⲅⲉ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛⲉⲧⲙⲟⲟⲩⲧ ⲥⲉⲟⲛϩ ⲁⲛ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛⲉⲧⲟⲛϩ ⲥⲉⲛⲁⲙⲟⲩ ⲁⲛ ⲛϩⲟⲟⲩ ⲛⲉⲧⲉⲧⲛⲟⲩⲱⲙ ⲙⲡⲉⲧⲙⲟⲟⲩⲧ ⲛⲉⲧⲉⲧⲛⲉⲓⲣⲉ ⲙⲙⲟϥ ⲙⲡⲉⲧⲟⲛϩ ϩⲟⲧⲁⲛ ⲉⲧⲉⲧⲛϣⲁⲛϣⲱⲡⲉ ϩⲙⲡⲟⲩⲟⲉⲓⲛ ⲟⲩ ⲡⲉⲧⲉⲧⲛⲛⲁⲁⲁϥ ϩⲙⲡϩⲟⲟⲩ ⲉⲧⲧⲉⲧⲛⲟ ⲛⲟⲩⲁ ⲁⲧⲉⲧⲛⲉⲓⲣⲉ ⲙⲡⲥⲛⲁⲩ ϩⲟⲧⲁⲛ ⲇⲉ ⲉⲧⲉⲧⲛϣⲁⲛϣⲱⲡⲉ ⲛⲥⲛⲁⲩ · ⲟⲩ ⲡⲉⲧⲉⲧⲛⲛⲁⲁⲁϥ ·

## English draft

Jesus said: This heaven will pass away, and the one above it will pass away. And those who are dead are not alive, and those who are alive will not die. What you eat when it is dead, you make alive. When you come to be in the light, what will you do? On the day you were one, you made the two. But when you come to be two, what will you do?

## Notes

- "Pass away" renders the Greek loan ⲣⲡⲁⲣⲁⲅⲉ (do a passing-by); "pass" alone would also be honest — "pass away" kept as the plain English idiom for a heaven ending.
- "The one above it" — Coptic says "the one that is over it [the heaven]"; the draft does not add a second "heaven" noun the Coptic leaves understood. Fork noted.
- After "will not die" the Coptic adds ⲛϩⲟⲟⲩ, literally "in days"; read here as "at any time" and not carried into the draft wording. Fork noted rather than smoothed over silently.
- "What you eat when it is dead" — Coptic verb frame carries the preterit converter ⲛⲉ- ("used to / were"); draft reads it as a standing habit ("what you eat … you make"), fork noted. "Make alive" renders ⲉⲓⲣⲉ ⲙⲡⲉⲧⲟⲛϩ (make [it into] the living one).
- The one/two sayings at the end touch the same one-and-two wording that returns in other forms at Logia 48 and 106; each stands on its own worksheet, never merged.
- G1: checked sentence by sentence against base sentences s0037–s0043; question order kept (light / one-day / two); no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

# Logion 12

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0044–s0046, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲙⲙⲁⲑⲏⲧⲏⲥ ⲛⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉⲧⲛⲥⲟⲟⲩⲛ ϫⲉⲕⲛⲁⲃⲱⲕ ⲛⲧⲟⲟⲧⲛ ⲛⲓⲙ ⲡⲉ ⲉⲧⲛⲁⲣⲛⲟϭ ⲉϩⲣⲁⲓ ⲉϫⲱⲛ ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ⲛⲁⲩ ϫⲉⲡⲙⲁ ⲛⲧⲁⲧⲉⲧⲛⲉⲓ ⲙⲙⲁⲩ ⲉⲧⲉⲧⲛⲛⲁⲃⲱⲕ ϣⲁⲓⲁⲕⲱⲃⲟⲥ ⲡⲇⲓⲕⲁⲓⲟⲥ ⲡⲁⲓ ⲛⲧⲁⲧⲡⲉ ⲙⲛⲡⲕⲁϩ ϣⲱⲡⲉ ⲉⲧⲃⲏⲏⲧϥ

## English draft

The disciples said to Jesus: We know that you will go away from us. Who will become great over us? Jesus said to them: In the place you have come to, you will go to James the Just, the one for whose sake heaven and earth came to be.

## Notes

- "Go away from us" renders ⲃⲱⲕ ⲛⲧⲟⲟⲧⲛ (go from us); no word for death or leaving-for-heaven is in the Coptic, and none is added.
- "Become great over us" renders ⲣⲛⲟϭ ⲉϩⲣⲁⲓ ⲉϫⲱⲛ word for word; the sense is "lead us / be over us" — fork noted, the plainer great-over-us wording kept close to the Coptic.
- "James" — Coptic ⲓⲁⲕⲱⲃⲟⲥ writes the Greek name Iakōbos; English "James" and "Jacob" are the same name in two English forms. Draft uses James; fork noted so the choice is not hidden.
- "The Just" renders the Greek loan ⲇⲓⲕⲁⲓⲟⲥ (just/righteous); "the Righteous" is also honest — fork noted.
- "For whose sake … came to be" renders ϣⲱⲡⲉ ⲉⲧⲃⲏⲧϥ (came to be because of him / for his sake); kept as the Coptic's own large claim, neither explained nor softened.
- G1: checked against base sentences s0044–s0046; speakers kept in order (disciples ask, Jesus answers); no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

# Logion 13

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0047–s0054, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ⲛⲛⲉϥⲙⲁⲑⲏⲧⲏⲥ ϫⲉⲧⲛⲧⲱⲛⲧ ⲛⲧⲉⲧⲛϫⲟⲟⲥ ⲛⲁⲓ ϫⲉⲉⲓⲉⲓⲛⲉ ⲛⲛⲓⲙ ⲡⲉϫⲁϥ ⲛⲁϥ ⲛϭⲓⲥⲓⲙⲱⲛ ⲡⲉⲧⲣⲟⲥ ϫⲉⲉⲕⲉⲓⲛⲉ ⲛⲟⲩⲁⲅⲅⲉⲗⲟⲥ ⲛⲇⲓⲕⲁⲓⲟⲥ ⲡⲉϫⲁϥ ⲛⲁϥ ⲛϭⲓⲙⲁⲑⲑⲁⲓⲟⲥ ϫⲉⲉⲕⲉⲓⲛⲉ ⲛⲟⲩⲣⲱⲙⲉ ⲙⲫⲓⲗⲟⲥⲟⲫⲟⲥ ⲛⲣⲙⲛϩⲏⲧ ⲡⲉϫⲁϥ ⲛⲁϥ ⲛϭⲓⲑⲱⲙⲁⲥ ϫⲉⲡⲥⲁϩ ϩⲟⲗⲱⲥ ⲧⲁⲧⲁⲡⲣⲟ ⲛⲁϣϣⲁⲡϥ ⲁⲛ ⲉⲧⲣⲁϫⲟⲟⲥ ϫⲉⲉⲕⲉⲓⲛⲉ ⲛⲛⲓⲙ ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉⲁⲛⲟⲕ ⲡⲉⲕⲥⲁϩ ⲁⲛ ⲉⲡⲉⲓⲁⲕⲥⲱ ⲁⲕϯϩⲉ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϩⲛⲧⲡⲏⲅⲏ ⲉⲧⲃⲣⲃⲣⲉ ⲧⲁⲓ ⲁⲛⲟⲕ ⲛⲧⲁⲓϣⲓⲧⲥ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲁϥϫⲓⲧϥ ⲁϥⲁⲛⲁⲭⲱⲣⲉⲓ ⲁϥϫⲱ ⲛⲁϥ ⲛϣⲟⲙⲧ ⲛϣⲁϫⲉ ⲛⲧⲁⲣⲉⲑⲱⲙⲁⲥ ⲇⲉ ⲉⲓ ϣⲁⲛⲉϥϣⲃⲉⲉⲣ ⲁⲩϫⲛⲟⲩϥ ϫⲉⲛⲧⲁⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲟⲟⲥ ϫⲉⲟⲩ ⲛⲁⲕ ⲡⲉϫⲁϥ ⲛⲁⲩ ⲛϭⲓⲑⲱⲙⲁⲥ ϫⲉⲉⲓϣⲁⲛϫⲱ ⲛⲏⲧⲛ ⲟⲩⲁ ϩⲛⲛϣⲁϫⲉ ⲛⲧⲁϥϫⲟⲟⲩ ⲛⲁⲓ ⲧⲉⲧⲛⲛⲁϥⲓⲱⲛⲉ ⲛⲧⲉⲧⲛⲛⲟⲩϫⲉ ⲉⲣⲟⲓ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛⲧⲉⲟⲩⲕⲱϩⲧ ⲉⲓ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϩⲛⲛⲱⲛⲉ ⲛⲥⲣⲱⲕϩ ⲙⲙⲱⲧⲛ

## English draft

Jesus said to his disciples: Compare me, and tell me who I am like. Simon Peter said to him: You are like a just angel. Matthew said to him: You are like a wise philosopher. Thomas said to him: Teacher, my mouth will not take it at all, to say who you are like. Jesus said: I am not your teacher. Since you drank, you got drunk from the spring that bubbles, the one I measured out. And he took him and drew back, and said three sayings to him. When Thomas came to his companions, they asked him: What did Jesus say to you? Thomas said to them: If I tell you one of the sayings he told me, you will pick up stones and throw them at me, and fire will come out of the stones and burn you.

## Notes

- "Compare me" renders ⲧⲛⲧⲱⲛⲧ (liken/compare me); "tell me who I am like" keeps the Coptic's own question form. In the opening sentence the base file marks one letter of "I" as a supplied editorial fix; the sense is not in doubt, and the fix is flagged here rather than hidden.
- "Just angel" — Greek loans ⲁⲅⲅⲉⲗⲟⲥ (angel) and ⲇⲓⲕⲁⲓⲟⲥ (just/righteous); draft keeps "just" as in Logion 12. "Wise philosopher" — ⲫⲓⲗⲟⲥⲟⲫⲟⲥ plus ⲣⲙⲛϩⲏⲧ (man of understanding), the same "wise / understanding" wording used at Logion 8.
- "My mouth will not take it at all" renders ⲧⲁⲧⲁⲡⲣⲟ ⲛⲁϣϣⲁⲡϥ ⲁⲛ with ϩⲟⲗⲱⲥ (wholly, at all); the mouth picture is kept because "mouth" (ⲧⲁⲡⲣⲟ) returns as a term in Logion 14. The base file marks the first letter of ϣⲁⲡ (take/receive) as supplied; flagged, sense clear.
- "You got drunk" renders ⲁⲕϯϩⲉ (you became drunk) from the bubbling spring (ⲡⲏⲅⲏ … ⲃⲣⲃⲣⲉ); "measured out" renders ϣⲓ (measure) — fork noted: "which I myself measured" is the more word-for-word road.
- "Drew back" renders the Greek loan ⲁⲛⲁⲭⲱⲣⲉⲓ (withdrew); the draft does not add "aside" or "alone," which the Coptic does not say. "Companions" renders ϣⲃⲏⲣ (friend/companion); "friends" is also honest — fork noted.
- The three sayings told to Thomas are not written in the text, and the draft does not invent them; Thomas's warning keeps its two beats in order (stones thrown, then fire from the stones).
- G1: checked against base sentences s0047–s0054; the three answers (Peter / Matthew / Thomas) and the return-scene kept in Coptic order; no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

# Logion 14

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0055–s0061, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ⲛⲁⲩ ϫⲉⲉⲧⲉⲧⲛϣⲁⲛⲣⲛⲏⲥⲧⲉⲩⲉ ⲧⲉⲧⲛⲛⲁϫⲡⲟ ⲛⲏⲧⲛ ⲛⲟⲩⲛⲟⲃⲉ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲉⲧⲉⲧⲛϣⲁⲛϣⲗⲏⲗ ⲥⲉⲛⲁⲣⲕⲁⲧⲁⲕⲣⲓⲛⲉ ⲙⲙⲱⲧⲛ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲉⲧⲉⲧⲛϣⲁⲛϯⲉⲗⲉⲏⲙⲟⲥⲩⲛⲏ ⲉⲧⲉⲧⲛⲛⲁⲉⲓⲣⲉ ⲛⲟⲩⲕⲁⲕⲟⲛ ⲛⲛⲉⲧⲛⲡⲛⲉⲩⲙⲁ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲉⲧⲉⲧⲛϣⲁⲛⲃⲱⲕ ⲉϩⲟⲩⲛ ⲉⲕⲁϩ ⲛⲓⲙ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛⲧⲉⲧⲙⲙⲟⲟϣⲉ ϩⲛⲛⲭⲱⲣⲁ ⲉⲩϣⲁⲛⲣⲡⲁⲣⲁⲇⲉⲭⲉ ⲙⲙⲱⲧⲛ ⲡⲉⲧⲟⲩⲛⲁⲕⲁⲁϥ ϩⲁⲣⲱⲧⲛ ⲟⲩⲟⲙϥ ⲛⲉⲧϣⲱⲛⲉ ⲛϩⲏⲧⲟⲩ ⲁⲣⲓⲑⲉⲣⲁⲡⲉⲩⲉ ⲙⲙⲟⲟⲩ ⲡⲉⲧⲛⲁⲃⲱⲕ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲉϩⲟⲩⲛ ϩⲛⲧⲉⲧⲛⲧⲁⲡⲣⲟ ϥⲛⲁϫⲱϩⲙⲧⲏⲩⲧⲛ ⲁⲛ ⲁⲗⲗⲁ ⲡⲉⲧⲛⲏⲩ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϩⲛⲧⲉⲧⲛⲧⲁⲡⲣⲟ ⲛⲧⲟϥ ⲡⲉⲧⲛⲁϫⲁϩⲙⲧⲏⲩⲧⲛ

## English draft

Jesus said to them: If you fast, you will bring sin on yourselves. And if you pray, they will condemn you. And if you give to the poor, you will do harm to your spirits. And if you go into any land and walk in the country, and they take you in, eat what they set before you. Heal the sick among them. For what goes into your mouth will not make you unclean, but what comes out of your mouth — that will make you unclean.

## Notes

- "Bring sin on yourselves" renders ϫⲡⲟ ⲛⲟⲩⲛⲟⲃⲉ (beget/bring forth a sin); "beget" is the word-for-word road — fork noted, plainer "bring … on" kept.
- "They will condemn you" renders ⲥⲉⲛⲁⲣⲕⲁⲧⲁⲕⲣⲓⲛⲉ ⲙⲙⲱⲧⲛ with the unnamed "they" left standing, as in the Coptic; the verb is the Greek loan for passing judgment. In the prayer line the base file supplies one wrapped letter at a line break; flagged, sense clear.
- "Give to the poor" renders ϯⲉⲗⲉⲏⲙⲟⲥⲩⲛⲏ (give alms) in the same plain words as Logion 6, for term consistency. "Do harm" renders ⲉⲓⲣⲉ ⲛⲟⲩⲕⲁⲕⲟⲛ (do an evil/harm); "your spirits" renders ⲛⲉⲧⲛⲡⲛⲉⲩⲙⲁ.
- "Take you in" renders ⲣⲡⲁⲣⲁⲇⲉⲭⲉ (receive); "set before you" renders ⲕⲁⲁϥ ϩⲁⲣⲱⲧⲛ (place it before you). "Country" renders the Greek loan ⲭⲱⲣⲁ (land/district) against ⲕⲁϩ (land) in the first half — the Coptic uses two different land-words and the draft keeps two.
- "Heal" renders the imperative ⲁⲣⲓⲑⲉⲣⲁⲡⲉⲩⲉ (do healing). "Make you unclean" renders ϫⲱϩⲙ (defile); the closing lines repeat the same verb (spelled ϫⲁϩⲙ in the last sentence of the base file) and the draft repeats the same English words. "Mouth" (ⲧⲁⲡⲣⲟ) is the same term as Logion 13.
- This logion answers back to the fast/pray/give questions of Logion 6 across the gap; the link is noted, the two logia are not merged or harmonized.
- G1: checked against base sentences s0055–s0061; the three if-clauses and the go/eat/heal orders kept in Coptic order; no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

# Logion 15

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0062–s0063, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉϩⲟⲧⲁⲛ ⲉⲧⲉⲧⲛϣⲁⲛⲛⲁⲩ ⲉⲡⲉⲧⲉⲙⲡⲟⲩϫⲡⲟϥ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϩⲛⲧⲥϩⲓⲙⲉ ⲡⲉϩⲧⲧⲏⲩⲧⲛ ⲉϫⲙⲡⲉⲧⲛϩⲟ ⲛⲧⲉⲧⲛⲟⲩⲱϣⲧ ⲛⲁϥ ⲡⲉⲧⲙⲙⲁⲩ ⲡⲉ ⲡⲉⲧⲛⲉⲓⲱⲧ

## English draft

Jesus said: When you see the one who was not born of woman, fall on your faces and worship him. That one is your Father.

## Notes

- "Was not born of woman" renders ⲙⲡⲟⲩϫⲡⲟϥ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϩⲛⲧⲥϩⲓⲙⲉ word for word (they did not bear him from woman); ϫⲡⲟ is bear/give birth, the same verb as "bring forth" in Logion 14. The draft does not supply a name for "the one" — the Coptic leaves him unnamed until the last line.
- "Fall on your faces" renders ⲡⲉϩⲧⲧⲏⲩⲧⲛ ⲉϫⲙⲡⲉⲧⲛϩⲟ (throw yourselves down on your face); "bow down" is the softer road — fork noted, the full fall kept. "Worship" renders ⲟⲩⲱϣⲧ.
- Closing line kept as its own short sentence, matching the Coptic's separate beat: "That one is your Father" (ⲡⲉⲧⲙⲙⲁⲩ ⲡⲉ ⲡⲉⲧⲛⲉⲓⲱⲧ).
- G1: checked against base sentences s0062–s0063; see/fall/worship order kept; no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

# Logion 16

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0064–s0068, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉⲧⲁⲭⲁ ⲉⲩⲙⲉⲉⲩⲉ ⲛϭⲓⲛⲣⲱⲙⲉ ϫⲉⲛⲧⲁⲓⲉⲓ ⲉⲛⲟⲩϫⲉ ⲛⲟⲩⲉⲓⲣⲏⲛⲏ ⲉϫⲙⲡⲕⲟⲥⲙⲟⲥ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲥⲉⲥⲟⲟⲩⲛ ⲁⲛ ϫⲉⲛⲧⲁⲓⲉⲓ ⲁⲛⲟⲩϫⲉ ⲛϩⲉⲛⲡⲱⲣϫ ⲉϫⲛⲡⲕⲁϩ ⲟⲩⲕⲱϩⲧ ⲟⲩⲥⲏϥⲉ ⲟⲩⲡⲟⲗⲉⲙⲟⲥ ⲟⲩⲛϯⲟⲩ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲛⲁϣⲱⲡⲉ ϩⲛⲟⲩⲏⲓ ⲟⲩⲛϣⲟⲙⲧ ⲛⲁϣⲱⲡⲉ ⲉϫⲛⲥⲛⲁⲩ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲥⲛⲁⲩ ⲉϫⲛϣⲟⲙⲧ ⲡⲉⲓⲱⲧ ⲉϫⲙⲡϣⲏⲣⲉ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡϣⲏⲣⲉ ⲉϫⲙⲡⲉⲓⲱⲧ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲥⲉⲛⲁⲱϩⲉ ⲉⲣⲁⲧⲟⲩ ⲉⲩⲟ ⲙⲙⲟⲛⲁⲭⲟⲥ

## English draft

Jesus said: Maybe people think I came to throw peace on the world, and they do not know that I came to throw divisions on the earth: fire, sword, war. For there will be five in a house: three against two and two against three, the father against the son and the son against the father. And they will stand, being single ones.

## Notes

- "Throw" renders ⲛⲟⲩϫⲉ, the ordinary word for throwing or casting something down, used twice ("throw peace" / "throw divisions"); "bring" is the softer road — fork noted, the plain verb kept. ⲉⲓⲣⲏⲛⲏ (peace), ⲥⲏϥⲉ (sword), and ⲡⲟⲗⲉⲙⲟⲥ (war) are Greek loans; ⲡⲱⲣϫ is divisions/splittings.
- Numbers kept exactly as the Coptic counts them: five in the house, three against two, two against three; the draft does not turn "against" (ⲉϫⲛ, upon/over) into "divided from," which the Coptic does not say.
- "Single ones" renders the Greek loan ⲙⲟⲛⲁⲭⲟⲥ (monakhos) in its plain sense — one who is single/alone; later church English "monks" would import an institution the saying does not name. Fork noted; the word returns across Thomas and stays "single one" here for consistency.
- G1: checked sentence by sentence against base sentences s0064–s0068; order kept (peace thought / divisions list / five-in-house / standing); no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

# Logion 17

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentence s0069, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉϯⲛⲁϯ ⲛⲏⲧⲛ ⲙⲡⲉⲧⲉⲙⲡⲉⲃⲁⲗ ⲛⲁⲩ ⲉⲣⲟϥ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲉⲧⲉⲙⲡⲉⲙⲁⲁϫⲉ ⲥⲟⲧⲙⲉϥ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲉⲧⲉⲙⲡⲉϭⲓϫ ϭⲙϭⲱⲙϥ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲙⲡⲉϥⲉⲓ ⲉϩⲣⲁⲓ ϩⲓⲡϩⲏⲧ ⲛⲣⲱⲙⲉ

## English draft

Jesus said: I will give you what no eye has seen, and what no ear has heard, and what no hand has touched, and what has not come up into the heart of a person.

## Notes

- Four shut doors in a row, kept parallel as the Coptic keeps them: eye/seen (ⲃⲁⲗ/ⲛⲁⲩ), ear/heard (ⲙⲁⲁϫⲉ/ⲥⲟⲧⲙ), hand/touched (ϭⲓϫ/ϭⲙϭⲱⲙ), heart. ϭⲙϭⲱⲙ is to touch or feel with the hand; "handled" is the other honest road.
- "Has not come up into the heart" renders ⲙⲡⲉϥⲉⲓ ⲉϩⲣⲁⲓ ϩⲓⲡϩⲏⲧ word for word (it did not come up on the heart). The draft keeps the rising picture rather than smoothing it to "entered anyone's mind"; fork noted. ⲣⲱⲙⲉ is person/man in the general sense — "of a person" kept, not narrowed.
- The thing given is never named in the Coptic; the draft likewise leaves "what" unnamed four times over.
- G1: checked against base sentence s0069; all four members present in Coptic order; no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

# Logion 18

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0070–s0074, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲙⲙⲁⲑⲏⲧⲏⲥ ⲛⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉϫⲟⲟⲥ ⲉⲣⲟⲛ ϫⲉⲧⲉⲛϩⲁⲏ ⲉⲥⲛⲁϣⲱⲡⲉ ⲛⲁϣ ⲛϩⲉ ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ⲁⲧⲉⲧⲛϭⲱⲗⲡ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲛⲧⲁⲣⲭⲏ ϫⲉⲕⲁⲁⲥ ⲉⲧⲉⲧⲛⲛⲁϣⲓⲛⲉ ⲛⲥⲁⲧϩⲁⲏ ϫⲉϩⲙⲡⲙⲁ ⲉⲧⲉⲧⲁⲣⲭⲏ ⲙⲙⲁⲩ ⲉⲣⲉⲧϩⲁⲏ ⲛⲁϣⲱⲡⲉ ⲙⲙⲁⲩ ⲟⲩⲙⲁⲕⲁⲣⲓⲟⲥ ⲡⲉⲧⲛⲁⲱϩⲉ ⲉⲣⲁⲧϥ ϩⲛⲧⲁⲣⲭⲏ ⲁⲩⲱ ϥⲛⲁⲥⲟⲩⲱⲛⲧϩⲁⲏ ⲁⲩⲱ ϥⲛⲁϫⲓϯⲡⲉ ⲁⲛ ⲙⲙⲟⲩ

## English draft

The disciples said to Jesus: Tell us how our end will be. Jesus said: Have you uncovered the beginning, then, that you should look for the end? For where the beginning is, there the end will be. Blessed is the one who will stand in the beginning. He will know the end, and he will not taste death.

## Notes

- "Uncovered" renders ϭⲱⲗⲡ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ (uncover, reveal); "discovered" is the flatter road — the picture of lifting a cover kept. The question in the Coptic is a challenge ("have you … that you should …?"), and the draft keeps that edge rather than turning it into a statement.
- ⲁⲣⲭⲏ (Greek loan, beginning) and ϩⲁⲏ (end) carry the whole saying; both kept with the same plain English words at every return. "Where the beginning is, there the end will be" follows the Coptic place-word ⲙⲁ and the doubled ⲙⲙⲁⲩ (there … there).
- "Stand in the beginning" renders ⲱϩⲉ ⲉⲣⲁⲧϥ ϩⲛⲧⲁⲣⲭⲏ — the same stand-word as Logion 16's closing line; consistency kept. "Taste death" renders ϫⲓϯⲡⲉ ⲙⲙⲟⲩ, the phrase that also closes Logia 1, 17's neighbor sayings, and 19 — rendered identically each time.
- G1: checked sentence by sentence against base sentences s0070–s0074; question-and-answer order kept; no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

# Logion 19

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0075–s0079, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉⲟⲩⲙⲁⲕⲁⲣⲓⲟⲥ ⲡⲉⲛⲧⲁϥϣⲱⲡⲉ ϩⲁⲧⲉϩⲏ ⲉⲙⲡⲁⲧⲉϥϣⲱⲡⲉ ⲉⲧⲉⲧⲛϣⲁⲛϣⲱⲡⲉ ⲛⲁⲓ ⲙⲙⲁⲑⲏⲧⲏⲥ ⲛⲧⲉⲧⲛⲥⲱⲧⲙ ⲁⲛⲁϣⲁϫⲉ ⲛⲉⲓⲱⲛⲉ ⲛⲁⲣⲇⲓⲁⲕⲟⲛⲉⲓ ⲛⲏⲧⲛ ⲟⲩⲛⲧⲏⲧⲛ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲙⲙⲁⲩ ⲛϯⲟⲩ ⲛϣⲏⲛ ϩⲙⲡⲁⲣⲁⲇⲓⲥⲟⲥ ⲉⲥⲉⲕⲓⲙ ⲁⲛ ⲛϣⲱⲙ ⲙⲡⲣⲱ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲙⲉⲣⲉⲛⲉⲩϭⲱⲃⲉ ϩⲉ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲡⲉⲧⲛⲁⲥⲟⲩⲱⲛⲟⲩ ϥⲛⲁϫⲓϯⲡⲉ ⲁⲛ ⲙⲙⲟⲩ

## English draft

Jesus said: Blessed is the one who was, before he came to be. If you become my disciples and hear my words, these stones will serve you. For you have five trees in Paradise that do not stir in summer or in winter, and their leaves do not fall. Whoever knows them will not taste death.

## Notes

- "Was, before he came to be" renders ϣⲱⲡⲉ ϩⲁⲧⲉϩⲏ ⲉⲙⲡⲁⲧⲉϥϣⲱⲡⲉ with the same verb (ϣⲱⲡⲉ, come to be) on both sides of the fork; "existed before he was born" is the explaining road — rejected here, fork noted, since the Coptic says nothing about birth.
- "Serve" renders the Greek loan ⲣⲇⲓⲁⲕⲟⲛⲉⲓ (do a serving, diakonei); the stones keep their strangeness — the draft does not soften them into a metaphor or name what the serving is.
- "Do not stir" renders ⲥⲉⲕⲓⲙ ⲁⲛ (they do not move); ⲕⲓⲙ is stir/move/shake. The two seasons are named plainly: ϣⲱⲙ (summer) and ⲡⲣⲱ (winter). "Their leaves do not fall" renders ⲙⲉⲣⲉⲛⲉⲩϭⲱⲃⲉ ϩⲉ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ — the habitual negative (they are not in the habit of falling), kept as a standing fact.
- "Whoever knows them will not taste death" — ϫⲓϯⲡⲉ ⲙⲙⲟⲩ rendered as in Logia 1 and 18; the five trees are counted, not explained.
- G1: checked sentence by sentence against base sentences s0075–s0079; three beats kept in order (blessed-before-being / stones serve / five trees); no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0080–s0083, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲙⲙⲁⲑⲏⲧⲏⲥ ⲛⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉϫⲟⲟⲥ ⲉⲣⲟⲛ ϫⲉⲧⲙⲛⲧⲣⲣⲟ ⲛⲙⲡⲏⲩⲉ ⲉⲥⲧⲛⲧⲱⲛ ⲉⲛⲓⲙ ⲡⲉϫⲁϥ ⲛⲁⲩ ϫⲉⲉⲥⲧⲛⲧⲱⲛ ⲁⲩⲃⲗⲃⲓⲗⲉ ⲛϣⲗⲧⲁⲙ ⲥⲥⲟⲃⲕ ⲡⲁⲣⲁⲛϭⲣⲟϭ ⲧⲏⲣⲟⲩ ϩⲟⲧⲁⲛ ⲇⲉ ⲉⲥϣⲁⲛϩⲉ ⲉϫⲙⲡⲕⲁϩ ⲉⲧⲟⲩⲣϩⲱⲃ ⲉⲣⲟϥ ϣⲁϥⲧⲉⲩⲟ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲛⲟⲩⲛⲟϭ ⲛⲧⲁⲣ ⲛϥϣⲱⲡⲉ ⲛⲥⲕⲉⲡⲏ ⲛϩⲁⲗⲁⲧⲉ ⲛⲧⲡⲉ

## English draft

The disciples said to Jesus: Tell us what the kingdom of the heavens is like. He said to them: It is like a grain of mustard seed. It is smaller than all the seeds. But when it falls on ground that is worked, it puts out a big branch, and it becomes a shelter for the birds of the sky.

## Notes

- "Is like" renders ⲧⲛⲧⲱⲛ (be like, resemble), asked and answered with the same verb; ⲙⲛⲧⲣⲣⲟ ⲛⲙⲡⲏⲩⲉ kept in full as "kingdom of the heavens" (plural ⲡⲏⲩⲉ), not shortened to "heaven."
- "A grain of mustard seed" renders ⲃⲗⲃⲓⲗⲉ ⲛϣⲗⲧⲁⲙ (a grain of mustard); ϣⲗⲧⲁⲙ is the mustard plant/seed word. "Smaller than all the seeds" renders ⲥⲥⲟⲃⲕ ⲡⲁⲣⲁⲛϭⲣⲟϭ ⲧⲏⲣⲟⲩ (it is small beyond all the seeds); ϭⲣⲟϭ is seed/grain for sowing.
- "Ground that is worked" renders ⲡⲕⲁϩ ⲉⲧⲟⲩⲣϩⲱⲃ ⲉⲣⲟϥ (the earth that people work on); "tilled ground" is the farm-word road — fork noted. "Puts out" renders ⲧⲉⲩⲟ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ (send/bring forth).
- "A big branch" renders ⲟⲩⲛⲟϭ ⲛⲧⲁⲣ; ⲧⲁⲣ is branch/shoot — kept singular as the Coptic has it, not grown into a tree. "Shelter" renders the Greek loan ⲥⲕⲉⲡⲏ (skepē, covering/shelter); ϩⲁⲗⲁⲧⲉ is birds, ⲧⲡⲉ the sky/heaven.
- G1: checked sentence by sentence against base sentences s0080–s0083; question/answer/smallest-seed/shelter order kept; no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

# Logion 21

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0084–s0093, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲙⲁⲣⲓϩⲁⲙ ⲛⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉⲉⲣⲉⲛⲉⲕⲙⲁⲑⲏⲧⲏⲥ ⲉⲓⲛⲉ ⲛⲛⲓⲙ ⲡⲉϫⲁϥ ϫⲉⲉⲩⲉⲓⲛⲉ ⲛϩⲉⲛϣⲏⲣⲉ ϣⲏⲙ ⲉⲩϭⲉⲗⲓⲧ ⲉⲩⲥⲱϣⲉ ⲉⲧⲱⲟⲩ ⲁⲛ ⲧⲉ ϩⲟⲧⲁⲛ ⲉⲩϣⲁⲛⲉⲓ ⲛϭⲓⲛϫⲟⲉⲓⲥ ⲛⲧⲥⲱϣⲉ ⲥⲉⲛⲁϫⲟⲟⲥ ϫⲉⲕⲉⲧⲉⲛⲥⲱϣⲉ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲛⲁⲛ ⲛⲧⲟⲟⲩ ⲥⲉⲕⲁⲕⲁϩⲏⲩ ⲙⲡⲉⲩⲙⲧⲟ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲉⲧⲣⲉⲩⲕⲁⲁⲥ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲛⲁⲩ ⲛⲥⲉϯⲧⲉⲩⲥⲱϣⲉ ⲛⲁⲩ ⲇⲓⲁⲧⲟⲩⲧⲟ ϯϫⲱ ⲙⲙⲟⲥ ϫⲉⲉϥϣⲁⲛⲉⲓⲙⲉ ⲛϭⲓⲡϫⲉⲥϩⲛⲏⲉⲓ ϫⲉϥⲛⲏⲩ ⲛϭⲓⲡⲣⲉϥϫⲓⲟⲩⲉ ϥⲛⲁⲣⲟⲉⲓⲥ ⲉⲙⲡⲁⲧⲉϥⲉⲓ ⲛϥⲧⲙⲕⲁⲁϥ ⲉϣⲟϫⲧ ⲉϩⲟⲩⲛ ⲉⲡⲉϥⲏⲓ ⲛⲧⲉⲧⲉϥⲙⲛⲧⲣⲣⲟ ⲉⲧⲣⲉϥϥⲓ ⲛⲛⲉϥⲥⲕⲉⲩⲟⲥ ⲛⲧⲱⲧⲛ ⲇⲉ ⲣⲟⲉⲓⲥ ϩⲁⲧⲉϩⲏ ⲙⲡⲕⲟⲥⲙⲟⲥ ⲙⲟⲩⲣ ⲙⲙⲱⲧⲛ ⲉϫⲛⲛⲉⲧⲛϯⲡⲉ ϩⲛⲟⲩⲛⲟϭ ⲛⲇⲩⲛⲁⲙⲓⲥ ϩⲓⲛⲁ ϫⲉⲛⲉⲛⲗⲏⲥⲧⲏⲥ ϩⲉ ⲉϩⲓⲏ ⲉⲉⲓ ϣⲁⲣⲱⲧⲛ ⲉⲡⲉⲓⲧⲉⲭⲣⲉⲓⲁ ⲉⲧⲉⲧⲛϭⲱϣⲧ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϩⲏⲧⲥ ⲥⲉⲛⲁϩⲉ · ⲉⲣⲟⲥ · ⲙⲁⲣⲉϥϣⲱⲡⲉ ϩⲛⲧⲉⲧⲛⲙⲏⲧⲉ ⲛϭⲓⲟⲩⲣⲱⲙⲉ ⲛⲉⲡⲓⲥⲧⲏⲙⲱⲛ ⲛⲧⲁⲣⲉⲡⲕⲁⲣⲡⲟⲥ ⲡⲱϩ ⲁϥⲉⲓ ϩⲛⲟⲩϭⲉⲡⲏ ⲉⲣⲉⲡⲉϥⲁⲥϩ ϩⲛⲧⲉϥϭⲓϫ ⲁϥϩⲁⲥϥ ⲡⲉⲧⲉⲟⲩⲛⲙⲁⲁϫⲉ ⲙⲙⲟϥ ⲉⲥⲱⲧⲙ ⲙⲁⲣⲉϥⲥⲱⲧⲙ

## English draft

Mariham said to Jesus: Who are your disciples like? He said: They are like small children staying in a field that does not belong to them. When the owners of the field come, they will say: Let go of our field for us. They take off their clothes in front of them, so they can let it go for them, and they give them their field. Because of this I say: If the master of the house learns that the thief is coming, he will keep watch before he comes, and he will not let him dig into his house — the house of his kingdom — to carry away his goods. But you, keep watch in front of the world. Tie yourselves at your sides with great power, so that thieves will not find a way to come to you, because the very chance you are watching for is the one they will find. Let a person who knows be among you. When the fruit grew ripe, he came in haste with his sickle in his hand, and he cut it. Whoever has ears to hear, let him hear.

## Notes

- "Mariham" kept as the Coptic names her (ⲙⲁⲣⲓϩⲁⲙ); the draft does not expand the name.
- "Staying in" renders the stative verb (lemma ϭⲟⲓⲗⲉ) for children in a field "that is not theirs" (ⲉⲧⲱⲟⲩ ⲁⲛ ⲧⲉ, literally "whose [owner] they are not"); fork noted — "living in" would say the same in looser English.
- "Let go of our field for us" renders ⲕⲉⲧⲉⲛⲥⲱϣⲉ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲛⲁⲛ (leave/release our field to us); "take off their clothes" renders ⲥⲉⲕⲁⲕⲁϩⲏⲩ, kept plain and not explained.
- "Master of the house" renders ⲡϫⲉⲥϩⲛⲏⲉⲓ (the lord/master in the house); "thief" renders ⲣⲉϥϫⲓⲟⲩⲉ (one who steals). "Dig into" renders ⲉϣⲟϫⲧ (to dig/break through); "goods" renders the Greek loan ⲥⲕⲉⲩⲟⲥ (vessels/things).
- "House of his kingdom" is kept literal (ⲡⲉϥⲏⲓ ⲛⲧⲉⲧⲉϥⲙⲛⲧⲣⲣⲟ); fork noted — the phrase yokes house and kingdom without smoothing.
- "Tie yourselves at your sides" renders ⲙⲟⲩⲣ ⲙⲙⲱⲧⲛ ⲉϫⲛⲛⲉⲧⲛϯⲡⲉ (bind on your sides/loins) with the Greek loan ⲇⲩⲛⲁⲙⲓⲥ as "power"; "thieves" here is the Greek loan ⲗⲏⲥⲧⲏⲥ. "The very chance you are watching for" renders ⲧⲉⲭⲣⲉⲓⲁ (Greek chreia: need/use/chance) with ϭⲱϣⲧ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ (looking out); what "they will find" points back to (the way, ϩⲓⲏ, or the chance) is left open — fork noted, not decided.
- "A person who knows" renders ⲟⲩⲣⲱⲙⲉ ⲛⲉⲡⲓⲥⲧⲏⲙⲱⲛ (Greek epistemon, knowing/skilled); "sickle" renders ⲁⲥϩ, "cut/reaped" renders ϩⲁⲥ.
- G1: checked sentence by sentence against base sentences s0084–s0093; three beats kept in order (children in the field / householder and thief / watch and ripe fruit); no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

> DRAFT — working translation from the Coptic (and Greek where it survives), prepared with AI assistance; not reviewed by a Coptic scholar; not finished. Corrections invited and credited.

# Logion 22

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0094–s0097, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲁⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ⲛⲁⲩ ⲉϩⲉⲛⲕⲟⲩⲓ ⲉⲩϫⲓⲉⲣⲱⲧⲉ ⲡⲉϫⲁϥ ⲛⲛⲉϥⲙⲁⲑⲏⲧⲏⲥ ϫⲉⲛⲉⲓⲕⲟⲩⲓ ⲉⲧϫⲓⲉⲣⲱⲧⲉ ⲉⲩⲧⲛⲧⲱⲛ ⲉⲛⲉⲧⲃⲏⲕ ⲉϩⲟⲩⲛ ⲉⲧⲙⲛⲧⲣⲣⲟ ⲡⲉϫⲁⲩ ⲛⲁϥ ϫⲉⲉⲓⲉⲉⲛⲟ ⲛⲕⲟⲩⲓ ⲧⲛⲛⲁⲃⲱⲕ ⲉϩⲟⲩⲛ ⲉⲧⲙⲛⲧⲣⲣⲟ ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ⲛⲁⲩ ϫⲉϩⲟⲧⲁⲛ ⲉⲧⲉⲧⲛϣⲁⲛⲣⲡⲥⲛⲁⲩ ⲟⲩⲁ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲉⲧⲉⲧⲛϣⲁⲛⲣⲡⲥⲁ ⲛϩⲟⲩⲛ ⲛⲧϩⲉ ⲙⲡⲥⲁ ⲛⲃⲟⲗ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲥⲁ ⲛⲃⲟⲗ ⲛⲧϩⲉ ⲙⲡⲥⲁ ⲛϩⲟⲩⲛ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲥⲁⲛ ⲛⲧⲡⲉ ⲛⲧϩⲉ ⲙⲡⲥⲁ ⲙⲡⲓⲧⲛ ⲁⲩⲱ ϩⲓⲛⲁ ⲉⲧⲉⲧⲛⲛⲁⲉⲓⲣⲉ ⲙⲡϩⲟⲟⲩⲧ ⲙⲛⲧⲥϩⲓⲙⲉ ⲙⲡⲓⲟⲩⲁ ⲟⲩⲱⲧ · ϫⲉⲕⲁⲁⲥ ⲛⲛⲉⲡϩⲟⲟⲩⲧ · ⲣϩⲟⲟⲩⲧ · ⲛⲧⲉⲧⲥϩⲓⲙⲉ ⲣⲥϩⲓⲙⲉ ϩⲟⲧⲁⲛ ⲉⲧⲉⲧⲛϣⲁⲛⲉⲓⲣⲉ ⲛϩⲉⲛⲃⲁⲗ ⲉⲡⲙⲁ ⲛⲟⲩⲃⲁⲗ · ⲁⲩⲱ ⲟⲩϭⲓϫ ⲉⲡⲙⲁ ⲛⲟⲩϭⲓϫ · ⲁⲩⲱ ⲟⲩ ⲟⲩⲉⲣⲏⲧⲉ ⲉⲡⲙⲁ ⲛⲟⲩⲟⲩⲉⲣⲏⲧⲉ ⲟⲩϩⲓⲕⲱⲛ · ⲉⲡⲙⲁ ⲛⲟⲩϩⲓⲕⲱⲛ ⲧⲟⲧⲉ ⲧⲉⲧⲛⲛⲁⲃⲱⲕ ⲉϩⲟⲩⲛ ⲉⲧⲙⲛⲧⲣⲣⲟ

## English draft

Jesus saw little children nursing. He said to his disciples: These little ones who are nursing are like the ones who go into the kingdom. They said to him: Then, if we are little, will we go into the kingdom? Jesus said to them: When you make the two into one, and you make the inside like the outside and the outside like the inside, and the top like the bottom, and when you make the male and the female into a single one, so that the male is not male and the female is not female, when you make eyes where an eye was, and a hand where a hand was, and a foot where a foot was, and a likeness where a likeness was, then you will go into the kingdom.

## Notes

- "Nursing" renders ϫⲓⲉⲣⲱⲧⲉ (taking milk/sucking); "little children / little ones" renders ⲕⲟⲩⲓ, kept small in both question and answer.
- "Go into the kingdom" renders ⲃⲱⲕ ⲉϩⲟⲩⲛ ⲉⲧⲙⲛⲧⲣⲣⲟ throughout; the Coptic ⲙⲛⲧⲣⲣⲟ is rendered "kingdom" without adding "of heaven," which this logion does not say.
- "Make the two into one" renders ⲣⲡⲥⲛⲁⲩ ⲟⲩⲁ; ⲣ- is "make/do" (ⲉⲓⲣⲉ). The inside/outside pair renders ⲡⲥⲁ ⲛϩⲟⲩⲛ / ⲡⲥⲁ ⲛⲃⲟⲗ (the inner side / the outer side); "top / bottom" renders ⲡⲥⲁⲛ ⲛⲧⲡⲉ / ⲡⲥⲁ ⲙⲡⲓⲧⲛ (the upper part / the lower part) in plain words.
- "Single one" renders ⲡⲓⲟⲩⲁ ⲟⲩⲱⲧ; ϫⲉⲕⲁⲁⲥ (so that) opens the clause "the male is not male and the female is not female" — kept as a so-that clause, not turned into a definition. "Male / female" render ⲡϩⲟⲟⲩⲧ / ⲧⲥϩⲓⲙⲉ.
- "Eyes where an eye was" keeps the Coptic move from plural ϩⲉⲛⲃⲁⲗ (eyes) to ⲟⲩⲃⲁⲗ (an eye) in the place-phrase ⲉⲡⲙⲁ ⲛ- (in the place of); hand (ϭⲓϫ), foot (ⲟⲩⲉⲣⲏⲧⲉ), and likeness (ϩⲓⲕⲱⲛ, Greek eikon) follow the same pattern without variation for style.
- Doublet discipline: the one-and-two wording touches Logion 11's close and returns later (Logia 48, 106); each stands on its own worksheet, never merged.
- G1: checked sentence by sentence against base sentences s0094–s0097; question, likeness, and make-one sequence kept in Coptic order; no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

# Logion 23

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0098–s0099, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉϯⲛⲁⲥⲉⲧⲡⲧⲏⲛⲉ ⲟⲩⲁ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϩⲛϣⲟ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲥⲛⲁⲩ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϩⲛⲧⲃⲁ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲥⲉⲛⲁⲱϩⲉ ⲉⲣⲁⲧⲟⲩ ⲉⲩⲟ ⲟⲩⲁ ⲟⲩⲱⲧ ·

## English draft

Jesus said: I will pick you, one out of a thousand and two out of ten thousand, and they will stand, being a single one.

## Notes

- "Pick" renders ⲥⲉⲧⲡ (lemma ⲥⲱⲧⲡ, choose/select); "thousand" renders ϣⲟ, "ten thousand" renders ⲧⲃⲁ (a myriad), counted exactly as the Coptic counts them.
- "Being a single one" renders ⲉⲩⲟ ⲟⲩⲁ ⲟⲩⲱⲧ (being one single [one]); ⲟⲩⲱⲧ keeps its "single/only" sense as in Logion 22, not softened to "united."
- G1: checked against base sentences s0098–s0099; numbers and standing kept; no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

Logion 24  (source worksheet: logion-024.md)

> DRAFT — working translation from the Coptic (and Greek where it survives), prepared with AI assistance; not reviewed by a Coptic scholar; not finished. Corrections invited and credited.

# Logion 24

- **Witness status:** Greek+Coptic, fragmentary (P.Oxy. 655; trace only)
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek (P.Oxy. 655, Grenfell & Hunt 1904, public domain; fragmentary)

P.Oxy. 655 preserves only a broken trace touching this logion's closing world-clause; the opening of the logion does not survive in Greek. No continuous Greek is printed here, because none survives. The Coptic below carries the saying, and the Greek trace is used as a control point only, not as a base.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0100–s0103, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲛⲉϥⲙⲁⲑⲏⲧⲏⲥ ϫⲉⲙⲁⲧⲥⲉⲃⲟⲛ ⲉⲡⲧⲟⲡⲟⲥ ⲉⲧⲕⲙⲙⲁⲩ ⲉⲡⲉⲓⲧⲁⲛⲁⲅⲕⲏ ⲉⲣⲟⲛ ⲧⲉ ⲉⲧⲣⲉⲛϣⲓⲛⲉ ⲛⲥⲱϥ ⲡⲉϫⲁϥ ⲛⲁⲩ ϫⲉⲡⲉⲧⲉⲩⲛⲙⲁⲁϫⲉ ⲙⲙⲟϥ ⲙⲁⲣⲉϥ · ⲥⲱⲧⲙ ⲟⲩⲛⲟⲩⲟⲉⲓⲛ ϣⲟⲟⲡ · ⲙⲡϩⲟⲩⲛ ⲛⲟⲩⲣⲙⲟⲩⲟⲉⲓⲛ ⲁⲩⲱ ϥⲣⲟⲩⲟⲉⲓⲛ ⲉⲡⲕⲟⲥⲙⲟⲥ ⲧⲏⲣϥ ⲉϥⲧⲙⲣⲟⲩⲟⲉⲓⲛ ⲟⲩⲕⲁⲕⲉ ⲡⲉ

## English draft

His disciples said: Show us the place where you are, because we must look for you. He said to them: Whoever has ears, let him hear. There is light inside a person of light, and he gives light to the whole world. If he does not give light, it is darkness.

## Notes

- Greek control is a trace only (see Greek section); the draft follows the Coptic. Any fuller Greek wording for this logion would be invented, and none is printed.
- "Show us" renders ⲙⲁⲧⲥⲉⲃⲟⲛ (give-teaching to us: show/teach us); "place" renders the Greek loan ⲧⲟⲡⲟⲥ. "Because we must look for you" renders ⲉⲡⲉⲓⲧⲁⲛⲁⲅⲕⲏ ⲉⲣⲟⲛ ⲧⲉ ⲉⲧⲣⲉⲛϣⲓⲛⲉ ⲛⲥⲱϥ — literally "since the necessity (Greek anankē) upon us is to seek after you"; fork noted, the must-clause kept plain.
- "Person of light" renders ⲟⲩⲣⲙⲟⲩⲟⲉⲓⲛ (a man/person of light) with ⲟⲩⲟⲉⲓⲛ (light) inside him (ⲙⲡϩⲟⲩⲛ); the draft does not turn the person into the light itself.
- "Gives light to the whole world" renders ϥⲣⲟⲩⲟⲉⲓⲛ ⲉⲡⲕⲟⲥⲙⲟⲥ ⲧⲏⲣϥ (Greek kosmos kept as "world"); the closing "it is darkness" renders ⲟⲩⲕⲁⲕⲉ ⲡⲉ — whether the unlit one or the unlit state is meant, the Coptic leaves the pronoun (ϥ, he/it) open; fork noted, "it" kept as the plainer road. Resonance readings stay reader-lens only and do not bend this rendering (audit §4).
- G1: checked sentence by sentence against base sentences s0100–s0103; request, ear-saying, and light-clauses kept in order; no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

# Logion 25

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0104–s0105, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉⲙⲉⲣⲉⲡⲉⲕⲥⲟⲛ ⲛⲧϩⲉ ⲛⲧⲉⲕⲯⲩⲭⲏ ⲁⲣⲓⲧⲏⲣⲉⲓ ⲙⲙⲟϥ ⲛⲧϩⲉ ⲛⲧⲉⲗⲟⲩ ⲙⲡⲉⲕⲃⲁⲗ

## English draft

Jesus said: Love your brother like your own soul. Watch over him like the pupil of your eye.

## Notes

- "Love" renders ⲙⲉⲣⲉ (lemma ⲙⲉ, to love); "brother" renders ⲥⲟⲛ; "soul" renders the Greek loan ⲯⲩⲭⲏ (psychē) as "soul," not thinned to "self" — fork noted.
- "Watch over" renders ⲁⲣⲓⲧⲏⲣⲉⲓ (Greek tērei, keep/guard — do a guarding); "pupil of your eye" renders ⲧⲉⲗⲟⲩ ⲙⲡⲉⲕⲃⲁⲗ (the little one/pupil of your eye), the eye's own word kept instead of the smoother "apple of your eye" — fork noted.
- G1: checked against base sentences s0104–s0105; love-clause and guard-clause kept paired; no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

> DRAFT — working translation from the Coptic (and Greek where it survives), prepared with AI assistance; not reviewed by a Coptic scholar; not finished. Corrections invited and credited.

# Logion 26

- **Witness status:** Greek+Coptic, fragmentary (P.Oxy. 1.1–4; only the closing clause survives in Greek)
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek (P.Oxy. 1, Grenfell & Hunt 1898, public domain; fragmentary)

The leaf begins mid-saying: the opening of this logion is lost on the papyrus, and G&H print dots for the loss. Only the tail survives: ". . . καὶ τότε διαβλέψεις ἐκβαλεῖν τὸ κάρφος τὸ ἐν τῷ ὀφθαλμῷ τοῦ ἀδελφοῦ σου." The Coptic below carries the whole saying; the surviving Greek is a control on its ending only. No Greek opening is printed, because none survives.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0106–s0108, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉⲡϫⲏ ⲉⲧϩⲙⲡⲃⲁⲗ ⲙⲡⲉⲕⲥⲟⲛ ⲕⲛⲁⲩ ⲉⲣⲟϥ ⲡⲥⲟⲓ ⲇⲉ ⲉⲧϩⲙⲡⲉⲕⲃⲁⲗ ⲕⲛⲁⲩ ⲁⲛ ⲉⲣⲟϥ ϩⲟⲧⲁⲛ ⲉⲕϣⲁⲛⲛⲟⲩϫⲉ ⲙⲡⲥⲟⲓ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϩⲙⲡⲉⲕⲃⲁⲗ · ⲧⲟⲧⲉ ⲕⲛⲁⲛⲁⲩ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲉⲛⲟⲩϫⲉ ⲙⲡϫⲏ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϩⲙⲡⲃⲁⲗ ⲙⲡⲉⲕⲥⲟⲛ

## English draft

Jesus said: You see the speck in your brother's eye, but the beam in your own eye you do not see. When you throw the beam out of your own eye, then you will see clearly to throw the speck out of your brother's eye.

## Notes

- "Speck" renders ϫⲏ; the surviving Greek tail has κάρφος (a dry twig, a speck) for the same word — the two witnesses agree on the small object. "Mote" is also honest; "speck" kept as the plainer word.
- "Beam" renders ⲥⲟⲓ (a wooden beam, a log); "log" is the other honest road — fork noted, "beam" kept.
- "Throw out" renders ⲛⲟⲩϫⲉ (throw, cast) in both halves; the draft does not soften the second half into different wording.
- Greek control covers the ending only (see Greek section); the draft follows the Coptic throughout. Nothing is restored into the Greek column from the Coptic or from any parallel.
- G1: checked sentence by sentence against base sentences s0106–s0108; seeing / not-seeing / when-you-throw order kept; no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

> DRAFT — working translation from the Coptic (and Greek where it survives), prepared with AI assistance; not reviewed by a Coptic scholar; not finished. Corrections invited and credited.

# Logion 27

- **Witness status:** Greek+Coptic (P.Oxy. 1.4–11)
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek (P.Oxy. 1, Grenfell & Hunt 1898, public domain)

Λέγει Ἰησοῦς, ἐὰν μὴ νηστεύσητε τὸν κόσμον οὐ μὴ εὕρητε τὴν βασιλείαν τοῦ θεοῦ· καὶ ἐὰν μὴ σαββατίσητε τὸ σάββατον οὐκ ὄψεσθε τὸν πατέρα.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0109–s0110, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲉⲧⲉⲧⲛϣⲁⲛⲧⲙⲣⲛⲏⲥⲧⲉⲩⲉ ⲉⲡⲕⲟⲥⲙⲟⲥ ⲧⲉⲧⲛⲛⲁϩⲉ ⲁⲛ ⲉⲧⲙⲛⲧⲣⲣⲟ ⲉⲧⲉⲧⲛⲧⲙⲉⲓⲣⲉ ⲙⲡⲥⲁⲃⲃⲁⲧⲟⲛ ⲛⲥⲁⲃⲃⲁⲧⲟⲛ ⲛⲧⲉⲧⲛⲛⲁⲛⲁⲩ ⲁⲛ ⲉⲡⲉⲓⲱⲧ

## English draft

If you do not fast from the world, you will not find the kingdom. If you do not keep the Sabbath as a Sabbath, you will not see the Father.

## Notes

- No "Jesus said" stands in the Coptic here: the base text opens straight with the condition. The Greek carries Λέγει Ἰησοῦς. The draft follows the Coptic and does not import the Greek introduction.
- Divergence footnoted, not smoothed (audit §1.3 flags this logion): the Greek has "the kingdom of God" (τὴν βασιλείαν τοῦ θεοῦ); the Coptic has only ⲧⲙⲛⲧⲣⲣⲟ, "the kingdom." Draft follows the Coptic.
- Fasting fork: the Greek νηστεύσητε τὸν κόσμον puts "the world" in the accusative ("fast the world," G&H's "fast to the world"); the Coptic ⲣⲛⲏⲥⲧⲉⲩⲉ ⲉⲡⲕⲟⲥⲙⲟⲥ uses ⲉ-, "fast as regards the world." "Fast from the world" kept as the plain road; both constructions are on the page here.
- "Keep the Sabbath as a Sabbath" renders ⲉⲓⲣⲉ ⲙⲡⲥⲁⲃⲃⲁⲧⲟⲛ ⲛⲥⲁⲃⲃⲁⲧⲟⲛ (make the Sabbath a Sabbath); the Greek has the single verb σαββατίσητε. "Make the Sabbath a real Sabbath" is the other honest road — fork noted.
- G1: checked against base sentences s0109–s0110; both condition-and-result pairs kept in order; no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

> DRAFT — working translation from the Coptic (and Greek where it survives), prepared with AI assistance; not reviewed by a Coptic scholar; not finished. Corrections invited and credited.

# Logion 28

- **Witness status:** Greek+Coptic, fragmentary (P.Oxy. 1.11–21; the Greek breaks off mid-saying, the remainder is Coptic alone)
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek (P.Oxy. 1, Grenfell & Hunt 1898, public domain; fragmentary)

Λέγει Ἰησοῦς, ἔ[σ]την ἐν μέσῳ τοῦ κόσμου καὶ ἐν σαρκὶ ὤφθην αὐτοῖς, καὶ εὗρον πάντας μεθύοντας καὶ οὐδένα εὗρον διψῶντα ἐν αὐτοῖς, καὶ πονεῖ ἡ ψυχή μου ἐπὶ τοῖς υἱοῖς τῶν ἀνθρώπων, ὅτι τυφλοί εἰσιν τῇ καρδίᾳ αὐτῶ[ν] καὶ οὐ βλέ[πουσιν . . .

The papyrus text ends there, mid-saying (brackets and closing dots as printed by G&H). Everything in the Coptic after "and do not see" — the empty coming-and-going, the present drunkenness, the shaking off of the wine — has no surviving Greek. No Greek is printed for it, because none survives.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0111–s0116, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉⲁⲓⲱϩⲉ ⲉⲣⲁⲧ ϩⲛⲧⲙⲏⲧⲉ ⲙⲡⲕⲟⲥⲙⲟⲥ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲁⲓⲟⲩⲱⲛϩ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲛⲁⲩ ϩⲛⲥⲁⲣⲝ ⲁⲓϩⲉ ⲉⲣⲟⲟⲩ ⲧⲏⲣⲟⲩ ⲉⲩⲧⲁϩⲉ ⲙⲡⲓϩⲉ ⲉⲣⲉⲗⲁⲁⲩ ⲛϩⲏⲧⲟⲩ ⲉϥⲟⲃⲉ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲁⲧⲁⲯⲩⲭⲏ ϯⲧⲕⲁⲥ ⲉϫⲛⲛϣⲏⲣⲉ ⲛⲛⲣⲱⲙⲉ ϫⲉϩⲉⲛⲃⲗⲗⲉⲉⲩ ⲉ ⲛⲉ ϩⲙⲡⲉⲩϩⲏⲧ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲥⲉⲛⲁⲩ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲁⲛ ϫⲉⲛⲧⲁⲩⲉⲓ ⲉⲡⲕⲟⲥⲙⲟⲥ ⲉⲩϣⲟⲩⲉⲓⲧ · ⲉⲩϣⲓⲛⲉ ⲟⲛ ⲉⲧⲣⲉⲩⲉⲓ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϩⲙⲡⲕⲟⲥⲙⲟⲥ ⲉⲩϣⲟⲩⲉⲓⲧ ⲡⲗⲏⲛ ⲧⲉⲛⲟⲩ ⲥⲉⲧⲟϩⲉ ϩⲟⲧⲁⲛ ⲉⲩϣⲁⲛⲛⲉϩⲡⲉⲩⲏⲣⲡ ⲧⲟⲧⲉ ⲥⲉⲛⲁⲣⲙⲉⲧⲁⲛⲟⲉⲓ

## English draft

Jesus said: I stood in the middle of the world, and I showed myself to them in flesh. I found all of them drunk. I found none of them thirsty. And my soul ached for the children of men, because they are blind in their heart and do not see: empty they came into the world, and empty they seek to leave the world again. But for now they are drunk. When they shake off their wine, then they will change their minds.

## Notes

- "I showed myself to them" renders ⲁⲓⲟⲩⲱⲛϩ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲛⲁⲩ (I appeared / made myself seen to them). The Greek is passive: ὤφθην αὐτοῖς, "I was seen by them." Fork noted; the draft follows the Coptic's active form.
- "My soul ached" renders ⲁⲧⲁⲯⲩⲭⲏ ϯⲧⲕⲁⲥ (my soul gives pain / aches); the Greek has πονεῖ ἡ ψυχή μου, "my soul suffers" (G&H: "grieveth"). "Ached" kept as the plain body word; ⲯⲩⲭⲏ (psychē) rendered "soul," as at Logion 25.
- "Drunk" renders both ⲧⲁϩⲉ (in "I found them drunk") and ⲧⲟϩⲉ (in "for now they are drunk") — same sense, kept with one English word both times. "Thirsty" renders ⲟⲃⲉ (to thirst).
- "Blind in their heart" renders ϩⲉⲛⲃⲗⲗⲉⲉⲩ ⲉⲛⲉ ϩⲙⲡⲉⲩϩⲏⲧ (blind ones they are in their heart); the Greek agrees (τυφλοί εἰσιν τῇ καρδίᾳ αὐτῶν).
- "Change their minds" renders ⲣⲙⲉⲧⲁⲛⲟⲉⲓ (do-metanoia). "Repent" is the church word for the same verb — fork noted, the plainer road kept.
- Greek control ends at "and do not see" (see Greek section); the last three Coptic movements are drafted on the Coptic alone and marked as such here.
- G1: checked sentence by sentence against base sentences s0111–s0116; stood / showed / found-drunk / found-none-thirsty / soul-ached / blind / empty-in-empty-out / drunk-for-now / shake-off sequence kept in order; no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

> DRAFT — working translation from the Coptic (and Greek where it survives), prepared with AI assistance; not reviewed by a Coptic scholar; not finished. Corrections invited and credited.

# Logion 29

- **Witness status:** Greek+Coptic, fragmentary (P.Oxy. 1.22; only the closing words survive in Greek)
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek (P.Oxy. 1, Grenfell & Hunt 1898, public domain; fragmentary)

Of this saying the papyrus keeps only the end: ". . . τ]ὴν πτωχείαν" ("... poverty"). The rest of the leaf's line is lost. The Coptic below carries the saying; the surviving Greek confirms its closing word only. No fuller Greek is printed, because none survives.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0117–s0119, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ⲉϣϫⲉⲛⲧⲁⲧⲥⲁⲣⲝ ϣⲱⲡⲉ ⲉⲧⲃⲉⲡⲛⲉⲩⲙⲁ ⲟⲩϣⲡⲏⲣⲉ ⲧⲉ ⲉϣϫⲉ ⲡⲛⲉⲩⲙⲁ ⲇⲉ ⲉⲧⲃⲉⲡⲥⲱⲙⲁ ⲟⲩϣⲡⲏⲣⲉ ⲛϣⲡⲏⲣⲉ ⲡⲉ ⲁⲗⲗⲁ ⲁⲛⲟⲕ ϯⲣϣⲡⲏⲣⲉ ⲙⲡⲁⲓ ϫⲉⲡⲱⲥ ⲁⲧⲉⲓⲛⲟϭ ⲙⲙⲛⲧⲣⲙⲙⲁⲟ ⲁⲥⲟⲩⲱϩ ϩⲛⲧⲉⲓⲙⲛⲧϩⲏⲕⲉ

## English draft

Jesus said: If the flesh came to be because of the spirit, it is a wonder. But if the spirit came to be because of the body, it is a wonder of wonders. But I wonder at this: how this great wealth has come to live in this poverty.

## Notes

- Two different Greek-loan body words stand in this one saying and are kept distinct: ⲥⲁⲣⲝ (sarx) as "flesh" and ⲥⲱⲙⲁ (sōma) as "body." Flattening both into one English word would erase the saying's own contrast — not done.
- "Wonder" renders ϣⲡⲏⲣⲉ in both the noun uses and the verb ⲣϣⲡⲏⲣⲉ ("I wonder"); "marvel" is the other honest road — fork noted, one word kept through the saying.
- "Came to be" renders ϣⲱⲡⲉ with ⲉⲧⲃⲉ "because of"; "came into being" also honest.
- "Has come to live in" renders ⲁⲥⲟⲩⲱϩ ϩⲛ (it settled / came to dwell in). "Wealth" renders ⲙⲛⲧⲣⲙⲙⲁⲟ (rich-ness, the state of being rich); "poverty" renders ⲙⲛⲧϩⲏⲕⲉ — and the surviving Greek tail (πτωχείαν, poverty) lands on the same closing word.
- G1: checked against base sentences s0117–s0119; flesh/spirit, spirit/body, and the "but I wonder" turn kept in order; no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

Logion 30  (source worksheet: logion-030.md)

> DRAFT — working translation from the Coptic (and Greek where it survives), prepared with AI assistance; not reviewed by a Coptic scholar; not finished. Corrections invited and credited.

# Logion 30

- **Witness status:** Greek+Coptic, divergent (P.Oxy. 1.23–30; the stone-and-wood lines follow this logion in the Greek but stand as Logion 77 in the Coptic)
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek (P.Oxy. 1, Grenfell & Hunt 1898, public domain; restorations in brackets as printed by G&H)

[Λέγ]ει [Ἰησοῦς, ὅπ]ου ἐὰν ὦσιν [β̄ οὐκ] ε[ἰσι]ν ἄθεοι, καὶ [ὅ]που ε[ἷς] ἐστιν μόνος, [λέ]γω, ἐγώ εἰμι μετ' αὐτ[οῦ·] ἔγει[ρ]ον τὸν λίθον κἀκεῖ εὑρήσεις με, σχίσον τὸ ξύλον κἀγὼ ἐκεῖ εἰμι.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0120–s0121, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉⲡⲙⲁ ⲉⲟⲩⲛϣⲟⲙⲧ ⲛⲛⲟⲩⲧⲉ ⲙⲙⲁⲩ ϩⲉⲛⲛⲟⲩⲧⲉ ⲛⲉ ⲡⲙⲁ ⲉⲟⲩⲛⲥⲛⲁⲩ ⲏ ⲟⲩⲁ ⲁⲛⲟⲕ ϯϣⲟⲟⲡ ⲛⲙⲙⲁϥ ·

## English draft

Jesus said: Where there are three gods, they are gods. Where there are two or one, I am with him.

## Notes

- This is the slice's sharp divergence, recorded and never harmonized (audit §1.3). The Coptic reads ϣⲟⲙⲧ ⲛⲛⲟⲩⲧⲉ ... ϩⲉⲛⲛⲟⲩⲧⲉ ⲛⲉ, "three gods ... they are gods." The Greek, as G&H restored it, reads "they are not without God" (οὐκ εἰσιν ἄθεοι). The draft follows the Coptic; the Greek road stands in its own column above.
- The Greek numeral is itself restored: G&H print [β̄ οὐκ], "two," inside brackets — the "two" is their supplement, not letters read whole on the leaf. The Coptic's count word for the second clause is ⲥⲛⲁⲩ ⲏ ⲟⲩⲁ, "two or one," against the Greek's εἷς ... μόνος, "one alone." Both forks stay on the page.
- "I am with him" renders ⲁⲛⲟⲕ ϯϣⲟⲟⲡ ⲛⲙⲙⲁϥ, with the singular ϥ (him) covering the two-or-one case; "with them" would smooth the number over — not done. The Greek μετ' αὐτοῦ is likewise singular.
- Placement divergence, footnoted and not merged: in the Greek leaf the stone-and-wood lines ("Lift the stone and there you will find me; split the wood and there am I") follow this logion immediately. In the Coptic division those same lines stand at Logion 77. They are not imported into this draft; they will be drafted at Logion 77, and the join will be named there too (audit §1.3: the join is editorial history, not gospel fact).
- G1: checked against base sentences s0120–s0121; three-gods clause and two-or-one clause kept separate as the Coptic stands; no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

Logion 31  (source worksheet: logion-031.md)

> DRAFT — working translation from the Coptic (and Greek where it survives), prepared with AI assistance; not reviewed by a Coptic scholar; not finished. Corrections invited and credited.

# Logion 31

- **Witness status:** Greek+Coptic (P.Oxy. 1; this saying stands inside the P.Oxy. 1 range, logia 26–33)
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek (P.Oxy. 1, Grenfell & Hunt 1897, public domain)

Greek survives for this logion in P.Oxy. 1 and is used as a control witness. No Greek words are invented or restored in this worksheet; where the papyrus is broken, the loss is left as loss. The English draft below follows the Coptic, with the Greek checked as a control on the double prophet / physician clause.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0122–s0123, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ⲙⲛⲡⲣⲟⲫⲏⲧⲏⲥ ϣⲏⲡ · ϩⲙⲡⲉϥϯⲙⲉ ⲙⲉⲣⲉⲥⲟⲓⲛ ⲣⲑⲉⲣⲁⲡⲉⲩⲉ ⲛⲛⲉⲧⲥⲟⲟⲩⲛ ⲙⲙⲟϥ

## English draft

Jesus said: No prophet is welcome in his own village. No doctor heals the people who know him.

## Notes

- “Prophet” renders the Greek loan ⲡⲣⲟⲫⲏⲧⲏⲥ; “welcome” renders ϣⲏⲡ (from ϣⲱⲡ, receive / accept) — “accepted” is the other honest road; “welcome” kept as the plainer word.
- “Village” renders ⲡⲉϥϯⲙⲉ (his village); the draft does not widen it to “country” or “homeland.”
- “Doctor” renders ⲥⲟⲓⲛ (physician); “heals” renders ⲣⲑⲉⲣⲁⲡⲉⲩⲉ, a Greek loan verb (therapeuein, treat / heal). “Those who know him” renders ⲛⲉⲧⲥⲟⲟⲩⲛ ⲙⲙⲟϥ.
- The saying is a double clause in the Coptic — prophet, then physician — and the two halves are kept separate, not merged into one.
- G1: checked sentence by sentence against base sentences s0122–s0123; prophet / village and physician / know-him order kept; no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

# Logion 32

- **Witness status:** Greek+Coptic (P.Oxy. 1; this saying stands inside the P.Oxy. 1 range, logia 26–33)
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek (P.Oxy. 1, Grenfell & Hunt 1897, public domain)

Greek survives for this logion in P.Oxy. 1 and is used as a control witness. No Greek words are invented or restored in this worksheet; where the papyrus is broken, the loss is left as loss. The English draft below follows the Coptic, with the Greek checked as a control on the city / mountain / cannot-fall clause.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0124–s0125, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉⲟⲩⲡⲟⲗⲓⲥ ⲉⲩⲕⲱⲧ ⲙⲙⲟⲥ ϩⲓϫⲛⲟⲩⲧⲟⲟⲩ ⲉϥϫⲟⲥⲉ ⲉⲥⲧⲁϫⲣⲏⲩ ⲙⲛϭⲟⲙ ⲛⲥϩⲉ ⲟⲩⲇⲉ ⲥⲛⲁϣϩⲱⲡ ⲟⲛ

## English draft

Jesus said: A city built on a high mountain and made strong cannot fall, and it cannot be hidden.

## Notes

- “City” renders the Greek loan ⲡⲟⲗⲓⲥ; “built” renders ⲉⲩⲕⲱⲧ ⲙⲙⲟⲥ (which they build / being built); “high” renders ⲉϥϫⲟⲥⲉ (being high / lifted up).
- “Made strong” renders ⲉⲥⲧⲁϫⲣⲏⲩ (being made firm / fortified, from ⲧⲁϫⲣⲟ); “fortified” is the other honest road — fork noted, “made strong” kept as the plainer phrase.
- “Cannot fall” renders ⲙⲛϭⲟⲙ ⲛⲥϩⲉ (there is no power / possibility for it to fall); “it cannot be hidden” renders ⲥⲛⲁϣϩⲱⲡ ⲟⲛ, with ϩⲱⲡ (hide / be hidden).
- G1: checked sentence by sentence against base sentences s0124–s0125; built / high / made-strong order kept, then the two cannot-clauses; no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

# Logion 33

- **Witness status:** Greek+Coptic, fragmentary (P.Oxy. 1; Greek opening only — the rest of this logion is Coptic alone in the surviving record)
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek (P.Oxy. 1, Grenfell & Hunt 1897, public domain; fragmentary)

P.Oxy. 1 preserves only the opening of this logion in Greek; the lamp and lampstand clauses that follow do not survive in Greek. No continuous Greek is printed here, because none survives for the whole saying. The Coptic below carries the full logion, and the Greek opening is used as a control point only, not as a base for the remainder.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0126–s0129, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ⲡⲉⲧⲕⲛⲁⲥⲱⲧⲙ ⲉⲣⲟϥ ϩⲙⲡⲉⲕⲙⲁⲁϫⲉ ϩⲙⲡⲕⲉⲙⲁⲁϫⲉ ⲧⲁϣⲉⲟⲉⲓϣ ⲙⲙⲟϥ · ϩⲓϫⲛⲛⲉⲧⲛϫⲉⲛⲉⲡⲱⲣ ⲙⲉⲣⲉⲗⲁⲁⲩ · ⲅⲁⲣ ϫⲉⲣⲉϩⲏⲃⲥ ⲛϥⲕⲁⲁϥ · ϩⲁⲙⲁⲁϫⲉ ⲟⲩⲇⲉ ⲙⲉϥⲕⲁⲁϥ · ϩⲙⲙⲁ ⲉϥϩⲏⲡ · ⲁⲗⲗⲁ ⲉϣⲁⲣⲉϥⲕⲁⲁϥ ϩⲓϫⲛⲧⲗⲩⲭⲛⲓⲁ ϫⲉⲕⲁⲁⲥ ⲟⲩⲟⲛ ⲛⲓⲙ ⲉⲧⲃⲏⲕ ⲉϩⲟⲩⲛ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲉⲧⲛⲏⲩ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲉⲩⲛⲁⲛⲁⲩ ⲉⲡⲉϥⲟⲩⲟⲉⲓⲛ

## English draft

Jesus said: What you hear in your ear, in the other ear too, shout it out on your rooftops. No one lights a lamp and puts it under a measure, or in a hidden place. He puts it on the lampstand, so that everyone who comes in and everyone who goes out can see its light.

## Notes

- Greek control covers the opening only (see Greek section); the draft follows the Coptic throughout. Nothing is restored into the Greek column from the Coptic.
- “What you hear in your ear, in the other ear too” renders ⲡⲉⲧⲕⲛⲁⲥⲱⲧⲙ ⲉⲣⲟϥ ϩⲙⲡⲉⲕⲙⲁⲁϫⲉ ϩⲙⲡⲕⲉⲙⲁⲁϫⲉ, with ⲙⲁⲁϫⲉ (ear) twice; “shout it out” renders ⲧⲁϣⲉⲟⲉⲓϣ (proclaim / preach aloud); “rooftops” renders ⲛⲉⲧⲛϫⲉⲛⲉⲡⲱⲣ.
- Term fork flagged, not smoothed: the same written word ⲙⲁⲁϫⲉ appears again in the lamp clause (ϩⲁⲙⲁⲁϫⲉ). In the hearing clause it is plainly “ear”; in the lamp clause the sense required is a covering vessel / measure that a lamp is put under. The draft renders the second use “a measure” and leaves this fork on the page.
- “Lamp” renders ϩⲏⲃⲥ; “lights” renders ϫⲉⲣⲉ (kindles); “lampstand” renders the Greek loan ⲗⲩⲭⲛⲓⲁ; “light” renders ⲟⲩⲟⲉⲓⲛ. “Hidden place” renders ⲙⲁ ⲉϥϩⲏⲡ.
- G1: checked sentence by sentence against base sentences s0126–s0129; hear / proclaim, lamp-under, lamp-on-stand order kept; no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

# Logion 34

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentence s0130, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉⲟⲩⲃⲗⲗⲉ ⲉϥϣⲁⲛⲥⲱⲕ ϩⲏⲧϥ ⲛⲟⲩⲃⲗⲗⲉ ϣⲁⲩϩⲉ ⲙⲡⲉⲥⲛⲁⲩ · ⲉⲡⲉⲥⲏⲧ · ⲉⲩϩⲓⲉⲓⲧ ·

## English draft

Jesus said: If a blind person leads a blind person, the two of them will fall down into a pit.

## Notes

- “Leads” renders ⲥⲱⲕ ϩⲏⲧϥ (draws / leads before him); “the two of them” renders ⲡⲉⲥⲛⲁⲩ; “fall down” renders ϣⲁⲩϩⲉ … ⲉⲡⲉⲥⲏⲧ (they fall down); “pit” renders ϩⲓⲉⲓⲧ (a pit / ditch) — fork noted, “pit” kept as the plainer word.
- G1: checked against base sentence s0130; if-clause, two-of-them, down-into-pit order kept; no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

# Logion 35

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0131–s0132, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ⲙⲛϭⲟⲙ ⲛⲧⲉⲟⲩⲁ ⲃⲱⲕ ⲉϩⲟⲩⲛ ⲉⲡⲏⲓ ⲙⲡϫⲱⲱⲣⲉ ⲛϥϫⲓⲧϥ ⲛϫⲛⲁϩ ⲉⲓⲙⲏⲧⲓ ⲛϥⲙⲟⲩⲣ ⲛⲛⲉϥϭⲓϫ ⲧⲟⲧⲉ ϥⲛⲁⲡⲱⲱⲛⲉ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲙⲡⲉϥⲏⲓ

## English draft

Jesus said: No one can go into a strong man's house and take his things by force unless he first ties his hands. Then he can empty his house out.

## Notes

- “No one can” renders ⲙⲛϭⲟⲙ ⲛⲧⲉⲟⲩⲁ (there is no power for anyone to —); “strong man” renders ϫⲱⲱⲣⲉ; “house” renders ⲏⲓ, twice.
- “Take his things by force” renders ⲛϥϫⲓⲧϥ ⲛϫⲛⲁϩ — literally taking by force / seizure (ϫⲛⲁϩ); the draft does not soften this into “steal.”
- “Unless he first ties his hands” renders ⲉⲓⲙⲏⲧⲓ ⲛϥⲙⲟⲩⲣ ⲛⲛⲉϥϭⲓϫ, with ⲙⲟⲩⲣ (bind / tie) and ϭⲓϫ (hands, plural in the Coptic as written).
- Closing fork: “empty his house out” renders ϥⲛⲁⲡⲱⲱⲛⲉ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲙⲡⲉϥⲏⲓ (he will turn out / overturn his house); “plunder his house” is the other honest road — fork noted, the plainer turn-out wording kept in the draft.
- G1: checked sentence by sentence against base sentences s0131–s0132; cannot-go-in, unless-binds, then-empty-out order kept; no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).
- Fork note (G2): the Coptic ⲛϥϫⲓⲧϥ ⲛϫⲛⲁϩ reads literally "take him by force" — the draft renders "take his things by force" (the seizure of goods). Both readings noted; the draft follows the goods-reading.

# Logion 36

- **Witness status:** Greek+Coptic, fragmentary (P.Oxy. 655 preserves scraps touching this logion only)
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek (P.Oxy. 655, Grenfell & Hunt 1904, public domain; fragmentary)

P.Oxy. 655 preserves only broken scraps for this part of the book; no continuous Greek survives for the whole saying. No continuous Greek is printed here, because none survives. The Coptic below carries the saying, and the Greek scraps are used as a control point only, not as a base.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentence s0133, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ⲙⲡⲣϥⲓⲣⲟⲟⲩϣ ϫⲓⲛϩⲧⲟⲟⲩⲉ ϣⲁⲣⲟⲩϩⲉ ⲁⲩⲱ ϫⲓⲛϩⲓⲣⲟⲩϩⲉ ϣⲁϩⲧⲟⲟⲩⲉ ϫⲉⲟⲩ ⲡⲉⲧⲉⲧⲛⲛⲁⲧⲁⲁϥ ϩⲓⲱⲧⲧⲏⲩⲧⲛ

## English draft

Jesus said: Do not worry from morning to evening, or from evening to morning, about what you will put on.

## Notes

- Greek control is scraps only (see Greek section); the draft follows the Coptic. Any fuller Greek wording for this logion would be invented, and none is printed.
- "Do not worry" renders ⲙⲡⲣϥⲓⲣⲟⲟⲩϣ (do not take care / be anxious); "from morning to evening" renders ϫⲓⲛϩⲧⲟⲟⲩⲉ ϣⲁⲣⲟⲩϩⲉ, and the reversed pair ϫⲓⲛϩⲓⲣⲟⲩϩⲉ ϣⲁϩⲧⲟⲟⲩⲉ is kept in the same order — both directions of the day are named, not collapsed.
- "What you will put on" renders ϫⲉⲟⲩ ⲡⲉⲧⲉⲧⲛⲛⲁⲧⲁⲁϥ ϩⲓⲱⲧⲧⲏⲩⲧⲛ, literally "what it is that you will put upon yourselves"; "wear" is the other plain road — fork noted, "put on" kept as it stays closer to ϩⲓⲱⲧ-.
- G1: checked word by word against base sentence s0133; one-sentence logion, no clauses added or dropped; no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

# Logion 37

- **Witness status:** Greek+Coptic, fragmentary (P.Oxy. 655 preserves scraps touching this logion only)
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek (P.Oxy. 655, Grenfell & Hunt 1904, public domain; fragmentary)

P.Oxy. 655 preserves only broken scraps for this part of the book; no continuous Greek survives for the whole saying. No continuous Greek is printed here, because none survives. The Coptic below carries the saying, and the Greek scraps are used as a control point only, not as a base.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0134–s0137, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲛⲉϥⲙⲁⲑⲏⲧⲏⲥ ϫⲉⲁϣ ⲛϩⲟⲟⲩ ⲉⲕⲛⲁⲟⲩⲱⲛϩ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲛⲁⲛ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲁϣ ⲛϩⲟⲟⲩ ⲉⲛⲛⲁⲛⲁⲩ ⲉⲣⲟⲕ ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉϩⲟⲧⲁⲛ ⲉⲧⲉⲧⲛϣⲁⲛⲕⲉⲕⲧⲏⲩⲧⲛ ⲁϩⲏⲩ ⲙⲡⲉⲧⲛϣⲓⲡⲉ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛⲧⲉⲧⲛϥⲓ ⲛⲛⲉⲧⲛϣⲧⲏⲛ ⲛⲧⲉⲧⲛ̄ⲕⲁⲁⲩ ϩⲁⲡⲉⲥⲏⲧ · ⲛⲛⲉⲧⲛⲟⲩⲉⲣⲏⲧⲉ ⲛⲧϩⲉ ⲛⲛⲓⲕⲟⲩⲓ ⲛϣⲏⲣⲉ ϣⲏⲙ ⲛⲧⲉⲧⲛϩϫⲟⲡϫⲡ · ⲙⲙⲟⲟⲩ ⲧⲟⲧⲉ ⲧⲉⲧⲛⲛⲁⲛⲁⲩ ⲉⲡϣⲏⲣⲉ ⲙⲡⲉⲧⲟⲛϩ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲧⲉⲧⲛⲛⲁⲣϩⲟⲧⲉ ⲁⲛ

## English draft

His disciples said: On what day will you show yourself to us? And on what day will we see you? Jesus said: When you take off your clothes without being ashamed, and you take your clothes and put them under your feet like little children and trample them, then you will see the son of the Living One, and you will not be afraid.

## Notes

- Greek control is scraps only (see Greek section); the draft follows the Coptic. Any fuller Greek wording for this logion would be invented, and none is printed.
- The two questions are kept as two: "show yourself to us" renders ⲟⲩⲱⲛϩ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲛⲁⲛ (appear/reveal yourself to us); "we will see you" renders ⲉⲛⲛⲁⲛⲁⲩ ⲉⲣⲟⲕ. ⲁϣ ⲛϩⲟⲟⲩ (which day) is rendered "on what day" both times.
- "Take off your clothes without being ashamed" renders ⲉⲧⲉⲧⲛϣⲁⲛⲕⲉⲕⲧⲏⲩⲧⲛ ⲁϩⲏⲩ ⲙⲡⲉⲧⲛϣⲓⲡⲉ: ⲕⲱⲕ (strip/peel) with ⲁϩⲏⲩ (naked) and the negative ⲙⲡⲉⲧⲛϣⲓⲡⲉ (you have not been ashamed). The draft keeps "without being ashamed" as the plainer road; "and you are not ashamed" is the same fork stated flatly.
- "Put them under your feet" renders ⲕⲁⲁⲩ ϩⲁⲡⲉⲥⲏⲧ ⲛⲛⲉⲧⲛⲟⲩⲉⲣⲏⲧⲉ (place them under your feet), with ϣⲧⲏⲛ (garments/clothes) twice; "trample them" renders ϩϫⲟⲡϫⲡ ⲙⲙⲟⲟⲩ (tread them down). The like-little-children comparison (ⲛⲧϩⲉ ⲛⲛⲓⲕⲟⲩⲓ ⲛϣⲏⲣⲉ ϣⲏⲙ) is kept, not softened into a general "childlike."
- "The son of the Living One" renders ⲡϣⲏⲣⲉ ⲙⲡⲉⲧⲟⲛϩ, with ⲟⲛϩ (live) as a participle — "the one who lives"; "the Living One" kept as the plain road. Resonance readings stay reader-lens only and do not bend this rendering (audit §4).
- G1: checked sentence by sentence against base sentences s0134–s0137; question pair, strip/trample condition, and see/not-fear result kept in order; no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

# Logion 38

- **Witness status:** Greek+Coptic, fragmentary (P.Oxy. 655 preserves scraps touching this logion only)
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek (P.Oxy. 655, Grenfell & Hunt 1904, public domain; fragmentary)

P.Oxy. 655 preserves only broken scraps for this part of the book; no continuous Greek survives for the whole saying. No continuous Greek is printed here, because none survives. The Coptic below carries the saying, and the Greek scraps are used as a control point only, not as a base.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0138–s0141, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉϩⲁϩ ⲛⲥⲟⲡ · ⲁⲧⲉⲧⲛⲣⲉⲡⲓⲑⲩⲙⲉⲓ ⲉⲥⲱⲧⲙ ⲁⲛⲉⲓϣⲁϫⲉ ⲛⲁⲓ · ⲉⲧϯϫⲱ ⲙⲙⲟⲟⲩ ⲛⲏⲧⲛ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲙⲛⲧⲏⲧⲛ ⲕⲉⲟⲩⲁ ⲉⲥⲟⲧⲙⲟⲩ ⲛⲧⲟⲟⲧϥ ⲟⲩⲛϩⲉⲛϩⲟⲟⲩ ⲛⲁϣⲱⲡⲉ ⲛⲧⲉⲧⲛϣⲓⲛⲉ ⲛⲥⲱⲓ ⲧⲉⲧⲛⲛⲁϩⲉ ⲁⲛ ⲉⲣⲟⲓ

## English draft

Jesus said: Many times you have longed to hear these words that I am saying to you, and you have no one else to hear them from. There will be days when you will look for me and will not find me.

## Notes

- Greek control is scraps only (see Greek section); the draft follows the Coptic. Any fuller Greek wording for this logion would be invented, and none is printed.
- "Many times" renders ϩⲁϩ ⲛⲥⲟⲡ (many of times/occasions); "longed to hear" renders ⲁⲧⲉⲧⲛⲣⲉⲡⲓⲑⲩⲙⲉⲓ ⲉⲥⲱⲧⲙ (you did desire to hear), with the Greek loan ⲉⲡⲓⲑⲩⲙⲉⲓ (epithymein, desire/long for) kept as "longed" — "wanted" is the flatter road, fork noted here.
- "These words that I am saying to you" renders ⲁⲛⲉⲓϣⲁϫⲉ ⲛⲁⲓ ⲉⲧϯϫⲱ ⲙⲙⲟⲟⲩ ⲛⲏⲧⲛ, with ϣⲁϫⲉ (words/sayings) throughout; "you have no one else to hear them from" renders ⲙⲛⲧⲏⲧⲛ ⲕⲉⲟⲩⲁ ⲉⲥⲟⲧⲙⲟⲩ ⲛⲧⲟⲟⲧϥ (you do not have another one from whom to hear them) — the from-him (ⲛⲧⲟⲟⲧϥ) is kept, not generalized to "anywhere else."
- "There will be days" renders ⲟⲩⲛϩⲉⲛϩⲟⲟⲩ ⲛⲁϣⲱⲡⲉ; "you will look for me" renders ⲛⲧⲉⲧⲛϣⲓⲛⲉ ⲛⲥⲱⲓ (you will seek after me), and "will not find me" renders ⲧⲉⲧⲛⲛⲁϩⲉ ⲁⲛ ⲉⲣⲟⲓ. No ending is added — the saying stops at not-finding, and the draft stops there too.
- G1: checked sentence by sentence against base sentences s0138–s0141; longing, no-other-source, and seek/not-find clauses kept in order; no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

Logion 39  (source worksheet: logion-039.md)

> DRAFT — working translation from the Coptic (and Greek where it survives), prepared with AI assistance; not reviewed by a Coptic scholar; not finished. Corrections invited and credited.

# Logion 39

- **Witness status:** Greek+Coptic, fragmentary (P.Oxy. 655 preserves scraps touching this logion only)
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek (P.Oxy. 655, Grenfell & Hunt 1904, public domain; fragmentary)

P.Oxy. 655 preserves only broken scraps for this part of the book; no continuous Greek survives for the whole saying. No continuous Greek is printed here, because none survives. The Coptic below carries the saying, and the Greek scraps are used as a control point only, not as a base.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0142–s0146, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉⲙⲫⲁⲣⲓⲥⲁⲓⲟⲥ ⲙⲛⲛⲅⲣⲁⲙⲙⲁⲧⲉⲩⲥ ⲁⲩϫⲓ ⲛϣⲁϣⲧ · ⲛⲧⲅⲛⲱⲥⲓⲥ ⲁⲩϩⲟⲡⲟⲩ ⲟⲩⲧⲉ ⲙⲡⲟⲩⲃⲱⲕ ⲉϩⲟⲩⲛ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛⲉⲧⲟⲩⲱϣ ⲉⲃⲱⲕ ⲉϩⲟⲩⲛ ⲙⲡⲟⲩⲕⲁⲁⲩ ⲛⲧⲱⲧⲛ ⲇⲉ ϣⲱⲡⲉ ⲙⲫⲣⲟⲛⲓⲙⲟⲥ ⲛⲧϩⲉ ⲛⲛϩⲟϥ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛⲁⲕⲉⲣⲁⲓⲟⲥ ⲛⲧϩⲉ ⲛⲛϭⲣⲟⲙⲡⲉ

## English draft

Jesus said: The Pharisees and the scribes took the keys of knowledge and hid them. They did not go in, and those who wanted to go in they did not let in. But you, be shrewd like the snakes and innocent like the doves.

## Notes

- Greek control is scraps only (see Greek section); the draft follows the Coptic. Any fuller Greek wording for this logion would be invented, and none is printed.
- "Took the keys of knowledge and hid them" renders ⲁⲩϫⲓ ⲛϣⲁϣⲧ ⲛⲧⲅⲛⲱⲥⲓⲥ ⲁⲩϩⲟⲡⲟⲩ, with ϣⲁϣⲧ (keys) and the Greek loan ⲅⲛⲱⲥⲓⲥ (gnosis, knowledge) — "knowledge" kept plain rather than the technical "gnosis"; fork noted, the technical term is on the page here. ⲥⲕⲣⲓⲃⲉⲥ rendered via the base ⲅⲣⲁⲙⲙⲁⲧⲉⲩⲥ (grammateus) as "scribes."
- The two refusals are kept as two: "they did not go in" renders ⲟⲩⲧⲉ ⲙⲡⲟⲩⲃⲱⲕ ⲉϩⲟⲩⲛ (neither did they go in), and "they did not let in" renders ⲙⲡⲟⲩⲕⲁⲁⲩ with ⲕⲱ (let/allow) — the draft does not merge them into one "kept everyone out."
- Closing fork flagged: ⲫⲣⲟⲛⲓⲙⲟⲥ (Greek phronimos) and ⲁⲕⲉⲣⲁⲓⲟⲥ (Greek akeraios, unmixed/innocent) are both Greek loans. The draft renders "shrewd" and "innocent"; "wise" for phronimos and "pure" for akeraios are the other honest roads, named here not chosen silently. "Like the snakes" renders ⲛⲧϩⲉ ⲛⲛϩⲟϥ and "like the doves" renders ⲛⲧϩⲉ ⲛⲛϭⲣⲟⲙⲡⲉ, plurals kept.
- G1: checked sentence by sentence against base sentences s0142–s0146; took/hid, not-in/not-let-in, and snakes/doves clauses kept in order; no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

# Logion 40

- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0147–s0148, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ⲟⲩⲃⲁⲛⲉⲗⲟⲟⲗⲉ ⲁⲩⲧⲟϭⲥ ⲙⲡⲥⲁ ⲛⲃⲟⲗ ⲙⲡⲉⲓⲱⲧ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲉⲥⲧⲁϫⲣⲏⲩ ⲁⲛ ⲥⲉⲛⲁⲡⲟⲣⲕⲥ ϩⲁⲧⲉⲥⲛⲟⲩⲛⲉ ⲛⲥⲧⲁⲕⲟ

## English draft

Jesus said: A vine was planted outside the Father. And because it is not strong, it will be pulled up by its roots and destroyed.

## Notes

- "A vine was planted" renders ⲟⲩⲃⲁⲛⲉⲗⲟⲟⲗⲉ ⲁⲩⲧⲟϭⲥ (a vine-branch, they planted it), with ⲃⲁ (branch/shoot) and ⲉⲗⲟⲟⲗⲉ (grape/vine) kept together as "vine"; "outside the Father" renders ⲙⲡⲥⲁ ⲛⲃⲟⲗ ⲙⲡⲉⲓⲱⲧ (on the outside of the Father) — the draft does not soften this into "apart from" or "without."
- "Because it is not strong" renders ⲉⲥⲧⲁϫⲣⲏⲩ ⲁⲛ (it is not made strong/firm), with ⲧⲁϫⲣⲟ (make firm/strong); "it will be pulled up by its roots" renders ⲥⲉⲛⲁⲡⲟⲣⲕⲥ ϩⲁⲧⲉⲥⲛⲟⲩⲛⲉ (they will uproot it at its root), with ⲡⲱⲣⲕ (uproot/pull up) and ⲛⲟⲩⲛⲉ (root); "and destroyed" renders ⲛⲥⲧⲁⲕⲟ (and it will perish/be destroyed). Both results — uprooted and destroyed — are kept.
- Boundaries checked against the base's chapter divisions: this logion is the vine saying alone (base sentences s0147–s0148); the has/has-not saying that follows (s0149–s0150) is Logion 41 and is not pulled into this worksheet.
- G1: checked word by word against base sentences s0147–s0148; planted, not-strong, uprooted, destroyed order kept; no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

# Logion 41

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0149–s0150, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉⲡⲉⲧⲉⲩⲛⲧⲁϥ · ϩⲛⲧⲉϥϭⲓϫ ⲥⲉⲛⲁϯ ⲛⲁϥ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲉⲧⲉⲙⲛⲧⲁϥ ⲡⲕⲉϣⲏⲙ ⲉⲧⲉⲩⲛⲧⲁϥ ⲥⲉⲛⲁϥⲓⲧϥ ⲛⲧⲟⲟⲧϥ

Jesus said: Whoever has something in his hand, more will be given to him. And whoever does not have, even the little he has will be taken from him.

## Notes

- "Whoever has something in his hand" renders ⲡⲉⲧⲉⲩⲛⲧⲁϥ ϩⲛⲧⲉϥϭⲓϫ (the one who has in his hand), with ⲟⲩⲛⲧⲁ- "have" and ϭⲓϫ "hand" kept literal; the draft does not thin this to "whoever has."
- "More will be given to him" renders ⲥⲉⲛⲁϯ ⲛⲁϥ (they will give to him) with ϯ "give"; no object is named in the Coptic, so none is added.
- "Even the little he has" renders ⲡⲕⲉϣⲏⲙ ⲉⲧⲉⲩⲛⲧⲁϥ (even the small thing that he has), with ⲕⲉ "also/even" and ϣⲏⲙ "small/little"; "will be taken from him" renders ⲥⲉⲛⲁϥⲓⲧϥ ⲛⲧⲟⲟⲧϥ (they will take it from him), with ϥⲓ "take/lift" and ⲛⲧⲛ "from."
- Boundaries checked against the base's chapter divisions: this logion is the has/has-not saying alone (base sentences s0149–s0150); the passer-by saying that follows (s0151) is Logion 42 and is not pulled into this worksheet.
- G1: checked word by word against base sentences s0149–s0150; has-in-hand, will-be-given, little-taken order kept; no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

# Logion 43

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0152–s0154, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲁⲩ ⲛⲁϥ ⲛϭⲓⲛⲉϥⲙⲁⲑⲏⲧⲏⲥ ϫⲉⲛⲧⲁⲕⲛⲓⲙ ⲉⲕϫⲱ ⲛⲛⲁⲓ ⲛⲁⲛ ϩⲛⲛⲉϯϫⲱ ⲙⲙⲟⲟⲩ ⲛⲏⲧⲛ ⲛⲧⲉⲧⲛⲉⲓⲙⲉ ⲁⲛ ϫⲉⲁⲛⲟⲕⲛⲓⲙ ⲁⲗⲗⲁ ⲛⲧⲱⲧⲛ ⲁⲧⲉⲧⲛϣⲱⲡⲉ ⲛⲧϩⲉ ⲛⲛⲓⲓⲟⲩⲇⲁⲓⲟⲥ ϫⲉⲥⲉⲙⲉ ⲙⲡϣⲏⲛ ⲥⲉⲙⲟⲥⲧⲉ ⲙⲡⲉϥⲕⲁⲣⲡⲟⲥ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲥⲉⲙⲉ ⲙⲡⲕⲁⲣⲡⲟⲥ ⲥⲉⲙⲟⲥⲧⲉ ⲙⲡϣⲏⲛ

## English draft

His disciples said to him: Who are you, that you say these things to us? From the things I say to you, you do not know who I am. But you have become like the Jews: they love the tree, they hate its fruit; they love the fruit, they hate the tree.

## Notes

- "Who are you, that you say these things to us?" renders ϫⲉⲛⲧⲁⲕⲛⲓⲙ ⲉⲕϫⲱ ⲛⲛⲁⲓ ⲛⲁⲛ, with ⲛⲓⲙ "who" and ⲛⲁⲓ "these things"; the question is kept as a question about identity, not softened to "by what right."
- "From the things I say to you, you do not know who I am" renders ϩⲛⲛⲉϯϫⲱ ⲙⲙⲟⲟⲩ ⲛⲏⲧⲛ ⲛⲧⲉⲧⲛⲉⲓⲙⲉ ⲁⲛ ϫⲉⲁⲛⲟⲕⲛⲓⲙ, with ϩⲛ "from/in" and ⲉⲓⲙⲉ "know"; the "from" is kept — knowing is tested by the sayings themselves.
- "Like the Jews" renders ⲛⲧϩⲉ ⲛⲛⲓⲓⲟⲩⲇⲁⲓⲟⲥ (in the manner of the Jews); the name is left as the Coptic gives it, neither widened nor narrowed by explanation.
- The closing pairs are kept as two mirrored pairs, not collapsed: "they love the tree, they hate its fruit" renders ⲥⲉⲙⲉ ⲙⲡϣⲏⲛ ⲥⲉⲙⲟⲥⲧⲉ ⲙⲡⲉϥⲕⲁⲣⲡⲟⲥ, and "they love the fruit, they hate the tree" renders ⲥⲉⲙⲉ ⲙⲡⲕⲁⲣⲡⲟⲥ ⲥⲉⲙⲟⲥⲧⲉ ⲙⲡϣⲏⲛ, with ⲙⲉ "love," ⲙⲟⲥⲧⲉ "hate," ϣⲏⲛ "tree," and the Greek loan ⲕⲁⲣⲡⲟⲥ "fruit." The contradiction is the point and is not explained away.
- G1: checked sentence by sentence against base sentences s0152–s0154; question, reply, and tree/fruit mirror kept in order; no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).
