> DRAFT — working translation from the Coptic (and Greek where it survives), prepared with AI assistance; not reviewed by a Coptic scholar; not finished. Corrections invited and credited.

# Logion 74

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0284–s0285, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲁϥ ϫⲉ ⲡϫⲟⲉⲓⲥ ⲟⲩⲛ ϩⲁϩ ⲙⲡⲕⲱⲧⲉ ⲛⲧϫⲱⲧⲉ ⲙⲛ ⲗⲁⲁⲩ ⲇⲉ ϩⲛ ⲧϣⲱⲛⲉ.

## English draft

He said: Lord, there are many around the drinking trough, but there is nothing in the well.

## Notes

- The speaker is unnamed in the Coptic (ⲡⲉϫⲁϥ "he said") — the draft does not name him; a disciple is speaking to Jesus, who is addressed as ⲡϫⲟⲉⲓⲥ "Lord, master."
- "many" renders ⲟⲩⲛ ϩⲁϩ, the existential "there are many," kept plain as in Logia 65 and 73; "but there is nothing" renders ⲙⲛ ⲗⲁⲁⲩ ⲇⲉ, the matching negative existential.
- "the drinking trough" renders ⲧϫⲱⲧⲉ — fork: "the trough/manger" is the range; the standard reading is a drinking trough for animals, and the draft keeps it plain. "Around the trough" renders ⲙⲡⲕⲱⲧⲉ "in the circuit/turning of," the crowd pressed close.
- "the well" renders ⲧϣⲱⲛⲉ (well, cistern) — the contrast of the saying is trough vs. well: plenty gathered where the water should be drawn, and nothing where it should be stored.
- G1: checked sentence by sentence against base sentences s0284–s0285; two-clause balance kept in Coptic order; no Greek printed or invented; no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

# Logion 75

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0286–s0287, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲉ ⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ⲟⲩⲛ ϩⲁϩ ⲁϩⲉⲣⲁⲧⲟⲩ ϩⲓⲣⲙⲡⲣⲟ ⲁⲗⲗⲁ ⲙⲙⲟⲛⲁⲭⲟⲥ ⲛⲉⲧⲛⲁⲃⲱⲕ ⲉϩⲟⲩⲛ ⲉⲡⲙⲁ ⲛϣⲉⲗⲉⲉⲧ.

## English draft

Jesus said: There are many standing at the door, but it is the single ones who will enter the bridal chamber.

## Notes

- "the single ones" renders the Greek loan μοναχός (ⲙⲟⲛⲁⲭⲟⲥ) — kept uniform with Logia 16 and 49, where the same word was rendered identically. The contrast is many vs. single, kept sharp in Coptic order.
- "many" renders ⲟⲩⲛ ϩⲁϩ "there are many" — plain, consistent with Logia 65, 73, and 74.
- "the bridal chamber" renders ⲡⲙⲁ ⲛϣⲉⲗⲉⲉⲧ "the place of the wedding/bride" — fork: "the wedding place" is the other honest road; the draft keeps the chamber reading in plain register.
- "standing" renders ⲁϩⲉⲣⲁⲧⲟⲩ, the stative of ⲱϩⲉ "stand" — they are already standing at the door when the saying is spoken; the tense is kept.
- The ⲁⲗⲗⲁ (Greek loan ἀλλά) "but" marks the many/single pivot; the ⲛⲉⲧⲛⲁⲃⲱⲕ future relative "who will enter" puts the single ones' entry ahead of them, in the future. The draft does not move them inside early.
- G1: checked sentence by sentence against base sentences s0286–s0287; no Greek printed or invented; no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

# Logion 76

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0288–s0291, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉⲧⲙⲛⲧⲣⲣⲟ ⲙⲡⲉⲓⲱⲧ ⲉⲥⲧⲛⲧⲱⲛ ⲉⲩⲣⲱⲙⲉ ⲛⲉϣⲱⲧ ⲉⲟⲩⲛⲧⲁϥ ⲙⲙⲁⲩ ⲛⲟⲩⲫⲟⲣⲧⲓⲟⲛ ⲉⲁϥϩⲉ ⲉⲩⲙⲁⲣⲅⲁⲣⲓⲧⲏⲥ.
ⲡⲉϣⲱⲧ ⲉⲧⲙⲙⲁⲩ ⲟⲩⲥⲁⲃⲉ ⲡⲉ.
ⲁϥϯⲡⲉⲫⲟⲣⲧⲓⲟⲛ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲁϥⲧⲟⲟⲩ ⲛⲁϥ ⲙⲡⲓⲙⲁⲣⲅⲁⲣⲓⲧⲏⲥ ⲟⲩⲱⲧ.
ⲛⲧⲱⲧⲛ ϩⲱⲧⲧⲏⲩⲧⲛ ϣⲓⲛⲉ ⲛⲥⲁⲡⲉϥⲁϩⲟ ⲉⲙⲉϥⲱϫⲛ ⲉϥⲙⲏⲛ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲡⲙⲁ ⲉⲙⲉⲣⲉϫⲟⲟⲗⲉⲥ ⲧϩⲛⲟ ⲉϩⲟⲩⲛ ⲙⲙⲁⲩ ⲉⲟⲩⲱⲙ ⲟⲩⲇⲉ ⲙⲉⲣⲉⲡϥⲛⲧ ⲧⲁⲕⲟ.

## English draft

Jesus said: The kingdom of the Father is like a merchant who had a load of goods, and he found a pearl. That merchant was wise: he sold the goods and bought the one pearl for himself. You too — seek his treasure, the one that does not fail, that holds: the place where no thief gets near it to eat, and no worm destroys it.

## Notes

- "the kingdom of the Father" renders ⲧⲙⲛⲧⲣⲣⲟ ⲙⲡⲉⲓⲱⲧ (Greek loan ⲧⲙⲛⲧⲣⲣⲟ = ἡ βασιλεία) — plain, matching Logion 22's "the kingdom" usage; the Coptic explicitly names the Father here.
- "a load of goods" renders ⲟⲩⲫⲟⲣⲧⲓⲟⲛ (Greek loan φορτίον), a cargo or pack of merchandise — kept mercantile, not smoothed to "wares."
- "a pearl" renders ⲟⲩⲙⲁⲣⲅⲁⲣⲓⲧⲏⲥ (Greek loan μαργαρίτης), kept singular both times; the article shifts from indefinite at the finding (ⲉⲩⲙⲁⲣⲅⲁⲣⲓⲧⲏⲥ) to definite at the purchase (ⲡⲓⲙⲁⲣⲅⲁⲣⲓⲧⲏⲥ ⲟⲩⲱⲧ "the one pearl") — the draft tracks that shift.
- "wise" renders ⲥⲁⲃⲉ — plain, not raised to "shrewd" or "prudent."
- "sold ... and bought" keeps the Coptic clause order (ⲁϥϯ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ "he gave away/sold" then ⲁϥⲧⲟⲟⲩ "he bought") — the sale precedes the purchase in the sentence, and the draft does not reorder it.
- "his treasure, the one that does not fail, that holds" renders ⲡⲉϥⲁϩⲟ ⲉⲙⲉϥⲱϫⲛ ⲉϥⲙⲏⲛ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ — fork: ⲱϫⲛ (fail) and ⲙⲏⲛ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ (abide, endure) are two distinct verbs, both kept in the draft; "holds" carries the enduring force in plain register.
- "the place where no thief gets near it to eat, and no worm destroys it" — the ⲉⲟⲩⲱⲙ "to eat" is kept in the thief clause where the syntax puts it (ⲙⲙⲁⲩ ⲉⲟⲩⲱⲙ); fork: it could be read as a general statement that neither thief nor worm consumes it — the draft follows the clause division as written.
- No doublets in this slice; no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).
- G1: checked sentence by sentence against base sentences s0288–s0291; no Greek printed or invented; no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

# Logion 77

- **Witness status:** Greek+Coptic fragmentary — Greek survives only for the stone-and-wood tail (P.Oxy. 1 tail, Grenfell & Hunt 1897, public domain); the opening sentences are Coptic alone
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

P.Oxy. 1 preserves only the closing "lift up the stone / split the wood" portion of this saying. In the Greek fragments that portion stands attached to logion 30's material; in the Coptic it is its own numbered logion. No continuous or restored Greek is printed here; the Coptic carries the whole saying and the Greek's placement is footnoted, not imported.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0292–s0295, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉⲁⲛⲟⲕ ⲡⲉ ⲡⲟⲩⲟⲉⲓⲛ ⲡⲁⲓ ⲉⲧϩⲓϫⲱⲟⲩ ⲧⲏⲣⲟⲩ.
ⲁⲛⲟⲕ ⲡⲉ ⲡⲧⲏⲣϥ ⲛⲧⲁⲡⲧⲏⲣϥ ⲉⲓ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲛϩⲏⲧ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛⲧⲁⲡⲧⲏⲣϥ ⲡⲱϩ ϣⲁⲣⲟⲓ.
ⲡⲱϩ ⲛⲟⲩϣⲉ ⲁⲛⲟⲕ ϯⲙⲙⲁⲩ.
ϥⲓ ⲙⲡⲱⲛⲉ ⲉϩⲣⲁⲓ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲧⲉⲧⲛⲛⲁϩⲉ ⲉⲣⲟⲓ ⲙⲙⲁⲩ.

## English draft

Jesus said: I am the light over all things. I am the all: out of me the all came, and the all reached to me. Split a piece of wood — I am there. Lift up the stone, and you will find me there.

## Notes

- "I am the light over all things" renders ⲁⲛⲟⲕ ⲡⲉ ⲡⲟⲩⲟⲉⲓⲛ ⲡⲁⲓ ⲉⲧϩⲓϫⲱⲟⲩ ⲧⲏⲣⲟⲩ — "the light, the one over them all"; no article is dropped, none added.
- "the all" renders ⲡⲧⲏⲣϥ three times, kept uniform — fork: "everything" or "the whole" are the other honest roads; the draft keeps the abstract noun in plain register.
- "out of me the all came, and the all reached to me" renders ⲛⲧⲁⲡⲧⲏⲣϥ ⲉⲓ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲛϩⲏⲧ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛⲧⲁⲡⲧⲏⲣϥ ⲡⲱϩ ϣⲁⲣⲟⲓ — "the all came out of me and the all stretched/reached to me"; the draft keeps the double "the all" and the two opposite movements (out of / toward) as written, not resolved into one smooth motion.
- "Split a piece of wood — I am there" renders ⲡⲱϩ ⲛⲟⲩϣⲉ ⲁⲛⲟⲕ ϯⲙⲙⲁⲩ — ⲡⲱϩ "split" is the same verb used at Logion 47's "the wineskins are not patched"; the imperative stands bare, kept bare.
- "Lift up the stone, and you will find me there" renders ϥⲓ ⲙⲡⲱⲛⲉ ⲉϩⲣⲁⲓ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲧⲉⲧⲛⲛⲁϩⲉ ⲉⲣⲟⲓ ⲙⲙⲁⲩ — ϥⲓ "raise/lift up" in imperative, the finding future (ⲧⲉⲧⲛⲛⲁϩⲉ); ⲉϩⲣⲁⲓ keeps the upward direction, consistent with Logion 33's "set a lamp."
- Divergence (audit §1.3): the stone-and-wood lines that close this saying in the Coptic stand attached to logion 30's material in P.Oxy. 1 — footnoted at Logion 30 (slice 6) and here; the worksheets take the Coptic numbering for the Coptic column and do not import the Greek's placement.
- Robert's resonance lens (the light within, Logion 77 as "God inside us") is recorded as reader-lens only — footnoted here, never a rendering rule; the fork where the Coptic is ambiguous (Logion 77 is not one) would name both roads.
- No parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).
- G1: checked sentence by sentence against base sentences s0292–s0295; no Greek printed or invented beyond the attested-tail description; no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

Logion 78  (source worksheet: logion-078.md)

> DRAFT — working translation from the Coptic (and Greek where it survives), prepared with AI assistance; not reviewed by a Coptic scholar; not finished. Corrections invited and credited.

# Logion 78

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentence s0296, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉⲉⲧⲃⲉⲟⲩ ⲁⲧⲉⲧⲛⲉⲓ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲉⲧⲥⲱϣⲉ ⲉⲛⲁⲩ ⲉⲩⲕⲁϣ ⲉϥⲕⲓⲙ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϩⲓⲧⲙⲡⲧⲏⲩ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲉⲛⲁⲩ ⲉⲩⲣⲱⲙⲉ ⲉⲩⲛϣⲧⲏⲛ ⲉⲩϭⲏⲛ ϩⲓⲱⲱϥ ⲛⲧϩⲉ ⲛⲛⲉⲧⲛⲣⲣⲱⲟⲩ ⲙⲛⲛⲉⲧⲛⲙⲉⲅⲓⲥⲧⲁⲛⲟⲥ ⲛⲁⲓ ⲉⲛⲉϣⲧⲏⲛ ⲉⲧϭⲏⲛ ϩⲓⲱⲟⲩ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲥⲉⲛⲁϣⲥⲟⲩⲛⲧⲙⲉ ⲁⲛ.

## English draft

Jesus said: Why did you come out to the field? To see a reed shaken by the wind, and to see a person dressed in soft clothes like your kings and your great men? Those who dress in soft clothes will not know the truth.

## Notes

- The whole saying is one Coptic sentence (s0296) — kept as one unit; the questions are not split into separate logia.
- "to the field" renders ⲉⲧⲥⲱϣⲉ "to the country/field/wilderness" — plain; fork: "out to the wilderness" is the other honest road; the draft keeps the field reading.
- "a reed shaken by the wind" renders ⲟⲩⲕⲁϣ ⲉϥⲕⲓⲙ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϩⲓⲧⲙⲡⲧⲏⲩ — ⲕⲁϣ reed/cane, ⲕⲓⲙ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ moved/shaken, ⲡⲧⲏⲩ the wind; the relative ⲉϥ- keeps the shaking ongoing in the draft ("shaken," passive in English, matches the Coptic relative construction).
- "dressed in soft clothes" renders ⲉⲩⲛϣⲧⲏⲛ ⲉⲩϭⲏⲛ ϩⲓⲱⲱϥ — ⲛϣⲧⲏⲛ stative "dressed/clothed," ⲉⲩϭⲏⲛ soft clothing/garments; fork: "fine clothing" or "delicate robes" are the other honest roads; the draft keeps "soft" close to the Coptic.
- "like your kings and your great men" renders ⲛⲧϩⲉ ⲛⲛⲉⲧⲛⲣⲣⲱⲟⲩ ⲙⲛⲛⲉⲧⲛⲙⲉⲅⲓⲥⲧⲁⲛⲟⲥ — ⲣⲣⲱⲟⲩ kings, ⲙⲉⲅⲓⲥⲧⲁⲛⲟⲥ (Greek loan μεγιστᾶνος) great men/magnates; both possessives kept ("your kings and your great men") — the contrast is pointed at the hearers' own rulers, and the draft does not blunt it.
- "will not know the truth" renders ⲥⲉⲛⲁϣⲥⲟⲩⲛⲧⲙⲉ ⲁⲛ — ⲥⲟⲩⲛ the same knowing-word kept uniform at Logia 67 and 69; future negative kept future.
- The clause ⲛⲁⲓ ⲉⲛⲉϣⲧⲏⲛ ⲉⲧϭⲏⲛ ϩⲓⲱⲟⲩ ("those who are dressed in soft clothing") restates the subject with the full phrase rather than a pronoun — the draft repeats it in full rather than smoothing to "they."
- No doublets in this slice; no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).
- G1: checked sentence by sentence against base sentence s0296; no Greek printed or invented; no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

Logion 79  (source worksheet: logion-079.md)
========================================================================

> DRAFT — working translation from the Coptic (and Greek where it survives), prepared with AI assistance; not reviewed by a Coptic scholar; not finished. Corrections invited and credited.

# Logion 79

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0297–s0299, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲟⲩⲥϩⲓⲙⲉ ⲛⲁϥ ϩⲙⲡⲙⲏⲏϣⲉ ϫⲉⲛⲉⲉⲓⲁⲧⲥ ⲛⲧϩⲏ ⲛⲧⲁⲥϥⲓ ϩⲁⲣⲟⲕ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛⲕⲓⲃⲉ ⲉⲛⲧⲁⲩⲥⲁⲛⲟⲩϣⲕ.
ⲡⲉϫⲁϥ ⲛⲁⲥ ϫⲉⲛⲉⲉⲓⲁⲧⲟⲩ ⲛⲛⲉⲛⲧⲁⲩⲥⲱⲧⲙ ⲉⲡⲗⲟⲅⲟⲥ ⲙⲡⲉⲓⲱⲧ ⲁⲩϩⲁⲣⲉϩ ⲉⲣⲟϥ ϩⲛⲟⲩⲙⲉ.
ⲟⲩⲛϩⲉⲛϩⲟⲟⲩ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲛⲁϣⲱⲡⲉ ⲛⲧⲉⲧⲛϫⲟⲟⲥ ϫⲉⲛⲉⲉⲓⲁⲧϥ ⲛⲧϩⲏ ⲧⲁⲓ ⲉⲧⲉⲙⲡⲥⲱⲱ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛⲕⲓⲃⲉ ⲛⲁⲓ ⲉⲙⲡⲟⲩϯⲉⲣⲱⲧⲉ.

## English draft

A woman in the crowd said to him: Blessed is the womb that bore you, and the breasts that nursed you. He said to her: Blessed are those who heard the word of the Father and kept it in truth. For there will be days when you will say: Blessed is the womb that did not conceive, and the breasts that did not nurse.

## Notes

- The speaker in s0297 is unnamed ("a woman in the crowd," ⲟⲩⲥϩⲓⲙⲉ ϩⲙⲡⲙⲏⲏϣⲉ) — kept unnamed, consistent with Logion 74's unnamed speaker.
- "Blessed" renders the Greek loan ⲙⲁⲕⲁⲣⲓⲟⲥ — kept uniform with Logia 49, 54, 58, 68, 69. The adjective is plural in s0298 (ⲛⲉⲉⲓⲁⲧⲟⲩ "blessed [plural]") and the draft tracks the number.
- "the breasts that nursed you" renders ⲛⲕⲓⲃⲉ ⲉⲛⲧⲁⲩⲥⲁⲛⲟⲩϣⲕ — ⲕⲓⲃⲉ (plurale tantum in effect) breasts, ⲥⲁⲛⲟⲩϣ nursed/suckled; the draft keeps "breasts" in plain register.
- "who heard the word of the Father and kept it in truth" renders ⲛⲛⲉⲛⲧⲁⲩⲥⲱⲧⲙ ⲉⲡⲗⲟⲅⲟⲥ ⲙⲡⲉⲓⲱⲧ ⲁⲩϩⲁⲣⲉϩ ⲉⲣⲟϥ ϩⲛⲟⲩⲙⲉ — ⲥⲱⲧⲙ heard/listened, ϩⲁⲣⲉϩ guarded/kept, ⲡⲗⲟⲅⲟⲥ (Greek loan λόγος) kept as "word" in plain register; the aorists kept past in English.
- "For there will be days when you will say" renders ⲟⲩⲛϩⲉⲛϩⲟⲟⲩ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲛⲁϣⲱⲡⲉ ⲛⲧⲉⲧⲛϫⲟⲟⲥ — the ⲅⲁⲣ "for" kept (not dropped as a mere filler), the future II ⲛⲧⲉⲧⲛϫⲟⲟⲥ kept future; the sentence is addressed to the hearers ("you will say"), not reported as someone else's words.
- "did not conceive" / "did not nurse" renders ⲧⲁⲓ ⲉⲧⲉⲙⲡⲥⲱⲱ and ⲛⲁⲓ ⲉⲙⲡⲟⲩϯⲉⲣⲱⲧⲉ — the negated relatives kept negative; the womb (ⲛⲉⲉⲓⲁⲧ) and the breasts (ⲛⲕⲓⲃⲉ) mirror the woman's blessing exactly, and the draft keeps the mirror word for word.
- No doublets in this slice; no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).
- G1: checked sentence by sentence against base sentences s0297–s0299; no Greek printed or invented; no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

> DRAFT — working translation from the Coptic (and Greek where it survives), prepared with AI assistance; not reviewed by a Coptic scholar; not finished. Corrections invited and credited.

# Logion 80

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0300–s0301, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉⲡⲉⲛⲧⲁϥⲥⲟⲩⲱⲛⲡⲕⲟⲥⲙⲟⲥ ⲁϥϩⲉ ⲉⲡⲥⲱⲙⲁ.
ⲡⲉⲛⲧⲁϥϩⲉ ⲇⲉ ⲉⲡⲥⲱⲙⲁ ⲡⲕⲟⲥⲙⲟⲥ ⲙⲡϣⲁ ⲙⲙⲟϥ ⲁⲛ.

## English draft

Jesus said: Whoever has known the world has found the body. But whoever has found the body — the world is not worthy of him.

## Notes

- DOUBLET: this is the counterpart of Logion 56 ("Whoever has known the world has found a corpse; but the one who has found a corpse is not worthy of the world"). The two sayings are variants, not one saying — each stays on its own worksheet; nothing is merged here.
- "the body" renders ⲡⲥⲱⲙⲁ (Greek loan σῶμα) — kept distinct from Logion 56's ⲡⲧⲱⲙⲁ (corpse); the two words are not the same and the draft does not collapse them. Consistent with the slice-6 discipline that kept σάρξ (flesh) and σῶμα (body) distinct at Logion 29.
- "known" renders ⲥⲟⲩⲱⲛ — kept uniform with Logia 67 and 69; aorist kept past.
- "found" renders ⲡⲉⲛⲧⲁϥϩⲉ — the same finding verb used at Logion 76's pearl; both occurrences in s0300–s0301 rendered identically, the draft does not vary them for style.
- "the world is not worthy of him" renders ⲡⲕⲟⲥⲙⲟⲥ ⲙⲡϣⲁ ⲙⲙⲟϥ ⲁⲛ — ⲕⲟⲥⲙⲟⲥ (Greek loan κόσμος) kept as "the world," uniform with Logion 56; the ⲇⲉ "but" opens the second sentence and is kept.
- No parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).
- G1: checked sentence by sentence against base sentences s0300–s0301; no Greek printed or invented; no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

# Logion 81

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0302–s0303, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉⲡⲉⲛⲧⲁϥⲣⲣⲙⲙⲁⲟ ⲙⲁⲣⲉϥⲣⲣⲣⲟ.
ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲉⲧⲉⲟⲩⲛⲧⲁϥ ⲛⲟⲩⲇⲩⲛⲁⲙⲓⲥ ⲙⲁⲣⲉϥⲁⲣⲛⲁ.

## English draft

Jesus said: Whoever has become rich, let him rule as king. And whoever has power, let him deny it.

## Notes

- "has become rich" renders ⲡⲉⲛⲧⲁϥⲣⲣⲙⲙⲁⲟ (aorist of ⲣⲣⲙⲙⲁⲟ "become rich"); the draft keeps the "become" sense rather than the bare state "is rich," because the same root ⲣⲣⲙⲙⲁⲟ returns at Logion 85 as the noun ⲙⲛⲧⲣⲙⲙⲁⲟ "wealth" — footnoted there, never merged.
- "let him rule as king" renders ⲙⲁⲣⲉϥⲣⲣⲣⲟ (hortative of ⲣⲣⲣⲟ "be king/reign"); "rule as king" keeps it a verb rather than promoting him to a title.
- "whoever has power" renders ⲡⲉⲧⲉⲟⲩⲛⲧⲁϥ ⲛⲟⲩⲇⲩⲛⲁⲙⲓⲥ — the Greek loan δύναμις "power"; base lemma ⲇⲩⲛⲁⲙⲓⲥ, NOUN.
- "let him deny it" renders ⲙⲁⲣⲉϥⲁⲣⲛⲁ — the verb ⲁⲣⲛⲁ "deny, renounce" with no object written in the Coptic; the draft supplies "it" in plain English to carry the implied "the power." Fork noted: "let him renounce" would carry the same sense; nothing is resolved against the Coptic here.
- No parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).
- G1: checked sentence by sentence against base sentences s0302–s0303 (chapter division ch.81); no Greek printed or invented; no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

# Logion 82

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0304–s0305, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉⲡⲉⲧϩⲏⲛ ⲉⲣⲟⲓ ⲉϥϩⲏⲛ ⲉⲧⲥⲁⲧⲉ.
ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲉⲧⲟⲩⲏⲩ ⲙⲙⲟⲓ ϥⲟⲩⲏⲩ ⲛⲧⲙⲛⲧⲣⲣⲟ.

## English draft

Jesus said: Whoever is near me is near the fire. And whoever is far from me is far from the kingdom.

## Notes

- "is near" renders ϩⲏⲛ "near, close to" twice (ⲡⲉⲧϩⲏⲛ ⲉⲣⲟⲓ "the one who is near me"; ⲉϥϩⲏⲛ ⲉⲧⲥⲁⲧⲉ "he is near the fire"); both occurrences rendered identically, the draft does not vary them for style.
- "the fire" renders ⲧⲥⲁⲧⲉ (lemma ⲥⲁⲧⲉ); "the kingdom" renders ⲧⲙⲛⲧⲣⲣⲟ; the pair is kept in plain parallel — near/fire, far/kingdom.
- "is far" renders ⲟⲩⲏⲩ "far away, distant" twice (ⲡⲉⲧⲟⲩⲏⲩ ⲙⲙⲟⲓ "the one who is far from me"; ϥⲟⲩⲏⲩ ⲛⲧⲙⲛⲧⲣⲣⲟ "he is far from the kingdom"); both occurrences rendered identically.
- The clause is a balanced mirror (near-near / far-far); the draft keeps the mirror rather than smoothing it.
- No parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).
- G1: checked sentence by sentence against base sentences s0304–s0305 (chapter division ch.82); no Greek printed or invented; no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

# Logion 83

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0306–s0309, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉⲛϩⲓⲕⲱⲛ ⲥⲉⲟⲩⲟⲛϩ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲙⲡⲣⲱⲙⲉ.
ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲟⲩⲟⲉⲓⲛ ⲉⲧⲛϩⲏⲧⲟⲩ ϥϩⲏⲡ ϩⲛⲧϩⲓⲕⲱⲛ ⲙⲡⲟⲩⲟⲉⲓⲛ ⲙⲡⲉⲓⲱⲧ.
ϥⲛⲁϭⲱⲗⲡ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ.
ⲁⲩⲱ ⲧⲉϥϩⲓⲕⲱⲛ ϩⲏⲡ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϩⲓⲧⲛⲡⲉϥⲟⲩⲟⲉⲓⲛ.

## English draft

Jesus said: Images are shown to the person. And the light that is in them is hidden in the image of the Father's light. It will be revealed. And his image is hidden by his light.

## Notes

- "Images" renders ⲛϩⲓⲕⲱⲛ (Greek loan εἰκών); kept distinct from Logion 84's ⲉⲓⲛⲉ "likeness" — the two words are not the same and the draft does not collapse them. Consistent with Logion 50's "their image."
- "are shown" renders ⲥⲉⲟⲩⲟⲛϩ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ (ⲟⲩⲟⲛϩ "manifest, reveal"); kept distinct from s0308's ϭⲱⲗⲡ "reveal" — the saying uses two different reveal-verbs and the draft does not smooth them into one.
- "the light that is in them" renders ⲡⲟⲩⲟⲉⲓⲛ ⲉⲧⲛϩⲏⲧⲟⲩ — ⲛϩⲏⲧⲟⲩ "in them" refers to the images (ⲛϩⲓⲕⲱⲛ).
- "the image of the Father's light" renders ⲧϩⲓⲕⲱⲛ ⲙⲡⲟⲩⲟⲉⲓⲛ ⲙⲡⲉⲓⲱⲧ; "hidden" renders ϩⲏⲡ (stative), kept as "is hidden" in both s0307 and s0309.
- "It will be revealed" renders ϥⲛⲁϭⲱⲗⲡ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ (future) — subject is the light of s0307; the draft does not supply a new noun.
- "his image is hidden by his light" renders ⲧⲉϥϩⲓⲕⲱⲛ ϩⲏⲡ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϩⲓⲧⲛⲡⲉϥⲟⲩⲟⲉⲓⲛ — whose image and whose light is not named in the clause; the draft does not name them.
- No parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).
- G1: checked sentence by sentence against base sentences s0306–s0309 (chapter division ch.83, four sentences); no Greek printed or invented; no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

# Logion 84

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0310–s0311, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ⲛϩⲟⲟⲩ ⲉⲧⲉⲧⲛⲛⲁⲩ ⲉⲡⲉⲧⲛⲉⲓⲛⲉ ϣⲁⲣⲉⲧⲛⲣⲁϣⲉ.
ϩⲟⲧⲁⲛ ⲇⲉ ⲉⲧⲉⲧⲛϣⲁⲛⲛⲁⲩ · ⲉⲛⲉⲧⲛϩⲓⲕⲱⲛ ⲛⲧⲁⲩϣⲱⲡⲉ ϩⲓⲧⲉⲧⲛⲉϩⲏ ⲟⲩⲧⲉ ⲙⲉⲩⲙⲟⲩ ⲟⲩⲧⲉ ⲙⲉⲩⲟⲩⲱⲛϩ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲧⲉⲧⲛⲛⲁϥⲓ ϩⲁⲟⲩⲏⲣ.

## English draft

Jesus said: When you see your likeness, rejoice. But when you see your images which came to be before you — neither dying nor being revealed — you will carry a burden.

## Notes

- "When" renders ⲛϩⲟⲟⲩ "in the days" (temporal); the literal would be "In the days when you see your likeness" — the draft keeps the plain plain-English sense and records the literal here.
- "likeness" renders ⲡⲉⲧⲛⲉⲓⲛⲉ — kept distinct from Logion 83's (and Logion 50's) ϩⲓⲕⲱⲛ "image"; the saying itself uses both words and the draft does not collapse them. Fork noted: "form" or "appearance" would be honest alternatives for ⲉⲓⲛⲉ; "likeness" is chosen and held.
- "which came to be before you" renders ⲛⲧⲁⲩϣⲱⲡⲉ ϩⲓⲧⲉⲧⲛⲉϩⲏ — aorist relative ⲛⲧⲁⲩ- "which came to be," with ϩⲓⲧⲉⲧⲛⲉϩⲏ "before you."
- "neither dying nor being revealed" renders ⲟⲩⲧⲉ ⲙⲉⲩⲙⲟⲩ ⲟⲩⲧⲉ ⲙⲉⲩⲟⲩⲱⲛϩ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ — the plural subjects are the images (ⲛⲉⲧⲛϩⲓⲕⲱⲛ); ⲟⲩⲱⲛϩ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ kept as "being revealed," uniform with Logion 83.
- "you will carry a burden" renders ⲧⲉⲧⲛⲛⲁϥⲓ ϩⲁⲟⲩⲏⲣ — ϥⲓ "carry, lift" with ⲟⲩⲏⲣ "burden, load"; the draft keeps the bare word rather than smoothing to "trouble."
- No parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).
- G1: checked sentence by sentence against base sentences s0310–s0311 (chapter division ch.84); no Greek printed or invented; no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

> DRAFT — working translation from the Coptic (and Greek where it survives), prepared with AI assistance; not reviewed by a Coptic scholar; not finished. Corrections invited and credited.

# Logion 85

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0312–s0313, normalized text as in the base file; manuscript letters partially damaged in the second sentence — see notes)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉⲛⲧⲁⲁⲇⲁⲙ ϣⲱⲡⲉ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϩⲛⲟⲩⲛⲟϭ ⲛⲇⲩⲛⲁⲙⲓⲥ ⲙⲛⲟⲩⲛⲟϭ ⲙⲙⲛⲧⲣⲙⲙⲁⲟ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲙⲡⲉϥϣⲱⲡⲉ.
ⲉϥⲙⲡϣⲁ ⲙⲙⲱⲧⲛ ⲛⲉⲩⲁⲝⲓⲟⲥ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲡⲉ ⲛⲉϥⲛⲁϫⲓ ⲧⲉⲓⲡⲉ ⲁⲛ ⲙⲡⲙⲟⲩ.

## English draft

Jesus said: Adam came to be from a great power and great wealth, and yet he did not become worthy of you. For had he been worthy, he would not have tasted death.

## Notes

- MANUSCRIPT DAMAGE: the second sentence's letters are partially damaged in the manuscript (dots and brackets in the base file's orig readings, e.g. at ⲉϥⲙⲡϣⲁ, ⲛⲉϥⲛⲁϫⲓ, and ⲧⲉⲓⲡⲉ); the normalized text supplies the letters as printed above and the draft follows the edition — nothing damaged has been invented beyond what the edition supplies, and this is stated on the page.
- "came to be" renders ⲛⲧⲁⲁⲇⲁⲙ ϣⲱⲡⲉ (ⲛⲧ + aorist) — kept parallel with ⲙⲡⲉϥϣⲱⲡⲉ "he did not become" in the same sentence; both keep the "become" sense of ϣⲱⲡⲉ.
- "a great power and great wealth" renders ⲟⲩⲛⲟϭ ⲛⲇⲩⲛⲁⲙⲓⲥ ⲙⲛⲟⲩⲛⲟϭ ⲙⲙⲛⲧⲣⲙⲙⲁⲟ — δύναμις "power" (Greek loan) and ⲙⲛⲧⲣⲙⲙⲁⲟ "wealth" built on the same ⲣⲙⲙⲁⲟ root as Logion 81's "become rich" — footnoted as an echo, never merged.
- "did not become worthy of you" renders ⲙⲡⲉϥϣⲱⲡⲉ ⲉϥⲙⲡϣⲁ ⲙⲙⲱⲧⲛ — the negative ⲙⲡϣⲁ "unworthy" (ⲡϣⲁ from Greek ἄξιος with negative) carried by both clauses; the draft keeps both, not collapsed: "he did not become worthy of you … for he was unworthy." Honesty note: "worthy of you" bridges the base's sentence division (s0312 ends at ⲙⲡⲉϥϣⲱⲡⲉ; ⲉϥⲙⲡϣⲁ ⲙⲙⲱⲧⲛ opens s0313) — the clauses belong together as elliptical main verb plus its circumstantial complement, and the draft is read as one thought.
- "For he was unworthy" renders ⲛⲉⲩⲁⲝⲓⲟⲥ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲡⲉ — the ⲅⲁⲣ "for" makes this the reason-clause for what precedes.
- "he would not have tasted death" renders ⲛⲉϥⲛⲁϫⲓ ⲧⲉⲓⲡⲉ ⲁⲛ ⲙⲡⲙⲟⲩ — counterfactual ⲛⲉϥⲛⲁ- "would have"; "taste death" (ϫⲓⲡⲉ ⲙⲙⲟⲩ) rendered identically at Logia 1, 18, and 19 per the standing discipline.
- No parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).
- G1: checked sentence by sentence against base sentences s0312–s0313 (chapter division ch.85); no Greek printed or invented; no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).
- RULING 2026-10-12 (Gemini audit Flag 1, verified): the main line now carries the counterfactual explicitly ('had he been worthy'), because the literal clause order reversed the sense in plain English; the diplomatic Coptic breakdown above stands as the record of the fork.

# Logion 86

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0314–s0315, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉⲛⲃⲁϣⲟⲣ ⲟⲩⲛⲧⲁⲩ ⲛⲉⲩⲃⲏⲃ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛϩⲁⲗⲁⲧⲉ ⲟⲩⲛⲧⲁⲩ ⲙⲙⲁⲩ ⲙⲡⲉⲩⲙⲁϩ
ⲡϣⲏⲣⲉ ⲇⲉ ⲙⲡⲣⲱⲙⲉ ⲙⲛⲧⲁϥ ⲛⲟⲩⲙⲁ ⲉⲣⲓⲕⲉ ⲛⲧⲉϥⲁⲡⲉ ⲛϥⲙⲧⲟⲛ · ⲙⲙⲟϥ

## English draft

Jesus said: The foxes have their holes, and the birds have their nests — but the son of man has no place to lay his head and rest.

## Notes

- MANUSCRIPT DAMAGE: both sentences carry supplied-letter flags in the base file (e.g. ⲛ̄ in ⲛⲃⲁϣⲟⲣ, the ⲟⲩⲛ̄ⲧ]ⲁ̣ read at "they have," and ⲙ̄[ⲙⲟ] at the closing reflexive of s0315); the normalized text above follows the edition — nothing damaged has been invented beyond what the edition supplies, and this is stated on the page.
- "holes" renders ⲃⲏⲃ "hole, den" (not a Greek loan); "nests" renders ⲙⲁϩ "nest." Both possessives kept: "their holes," "their nests."
- "the son of man" renders ⲡϣⲏⲣⲉ ⲙⲡⲣⲱⲙⲉ — kept as the plain title, not glossed.
- "has no place to lay his head" renders ⲙⲛⲧⲁϥ ⲛⲟⲩⲙⲁ ⲉⲣⲓⲕⲉ ⲛⲧⲉϥⲁⲡⲉ — negative existential ⲙⲛⲧⲁϥ "he has not," ⲙⲁ "place," ⲉⲣⲓⲕⲉ "incline, bend" with ⲧⲉϥⲁⲡⲉ "his head" as its object.
- "and rest" renders the finalis ⲛϥⲙⲧⲟⲛ ⲙⲙⲟϥ "so that he may rest (himself)" — read as the natural continuation of the same thought, not a separate command.
- No parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).
- G1: checked sentence by sentence against base sentences s0314–s0315 (chapter division ch.86); no Greek printed or invented; foxes/birds clause and son-of-man clause in Coptic order.

# Logion 87

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0316–s0317, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲁϥ ⲛϭⲓⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉⲟⲩⲧⲁⲗⲁⲓⲡⲱⲣⲟⲛ ⲡⲉ ⲡⲥⲱⲙⲁ ⲉⲧⲁϣⲉ ⲛⲟⲩⲥⲱⲙⲁ
ⲁⲩⲱ ⲟⲩⲧⲁⲗⲁⲓⲡⲱⲣⲟⲥ ⲧⲉ ⲧⲯⲩⲭⲏ ⲉⲧⲁϣⲉ ⲛⲛⲁⲓ ⲙⲡⲥⲛⲁⲩ

## English draft

Jesus said: Wretched is the body that depends on a body, and wretched is the soul that depends on these two.

## Notes

- "Wretched" renders ⲧⲁⲗⲁⲓⲡⲱⲣⲟⲛ / ⲟⲩⲧⲁⲗⲁⲓⲡⲱⲣⲟⲥ — the Greek loan ταλαίπωρος (the base file tags it OrigLang=grc in both sentences), masculine in the first clause agreeing with ⲡⲥⲱⲙⲁ, feminine in the second agreeing with ⲧⲯⲩⲭⲏ. Both kept as "wretched," not softened and not varied away.
- "body" renders ⲥⲱⲙⲁ (the σῶμα loan) in all three occurrences — deliberately kept distinct from ⲡⲧⲱⲙⲁ "corpse," the native corpse-word at Logia 56, 60, and 80, per the standing discipline.
- "depends on" renders ⲁϣⲉ "depend on, hang from" — the same verb in both clauses, kept the same way.
- "these two" renders ⲛⲁⲓ ⲙⲡⲥⲛⲁⲩ — left as the Coptic leaves it. Fork named here, not smoothed: "these two" most plainly reads as the two bodies of the first clause, but the Coptic does not spell it out; the draft does not supply "bodies" or "body and soul."
- "the soul" renders ⲧⲯⲩⲭⲏ (Greek loan, OrigLang=grc) — kept uniform with its earlier use at Logion 25.
- No doublets in this slice to merge; no parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).
- G1: checked sentence by sentence against base sentences s0316–s0317 (chapter division ch.87); no Greek printed or invented; no modern translation consulted or quoted.

# Logion 88

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0318–s0320, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉⲛⲁⲅⲅⲉⲗⲟⲥ ⲛⲏⲩ ϣⲁⲣⲱⲧⲛ ⲙⲛⲛⲡⲣⲟⲫⲏⲧⲏⲥ
ⲁⲩⲱ ⲥⲉⲛⲁϯ ⲛⲏⲧⲛ ⲛⲛⲉⲧⲉⲩⲛⲧⲏⲧⲛⲥⲉ
ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛⲧⲱⲧⲛ ϩⲱⲧⲧⲏⲩⲧⲛ ⲛⲉⲧⲛⲧⲟⲧⲧⲏⲛⲉ ⲧⲁⲁⲩ ⲛⲁⲩ ⲛⲧⲉⲧⲛϫⲟⲟⲥ ⲛⲏⲧⲛ ϫⲉⲁϣ ⲛϩⲟⲟⲩ ⲡⲉⲧⲟⲩⲛⲏⲩ ⲛⲥⲉϫⲓⲡⲉⲧⲉⲡⲱⲟⲩ

## English draft

Jesus said: The angels and the prophets will come to you, and they will give you what is yours. And you yourselves — give them what is in your hands, and say to yourselves: On what day will they come and take what is theirs?

## Notes

- "The angels and the prophets" renders ⲛⲁⲅⲅⲉⲗⲟⲥ … ⲛⲡⲣⲟⲫⲏⲧⲏⲥ — both Greek loans (ἄγγελος, προφήτης; base file tags both OrigLang=grc), plural with the definite article, future ⲛⲏⲩ "they will come."
- "they will give you what is yours" renders ⲥⲉⲛⲁϯ ⲛⲏⲧⲛ ⲛⲛⲉⲧⲉⲩⲛⲧⲏⲧⲛⲥⲉ — with ϯ "give," future ⲥⲉⲛⲁ-, and the relative phrase ⲛⲉⲧⲉⲩⲛⲧⲏⲧⲛ "that which is yours." Fork named here, not smoothed: the base file's parse carries a trailing ⲥⲉ "they" as subject of the existential (ⲛⲉⲧⲉ-ⲩⲛⲧⲏ-ⲧⲛ-ⲥⲉ, "that which they have of yours / that which is yours in their keeping"); the draft keeps the plain floor "what is yours" and leaves the fork in this note.
- "you yourselves" renders the emphatic ⲛⲧⲱⲧⲛ ϩⲱⲧⲧⲏⲩⲧⲛ — kept identical to its rendering at Logion 60 per the standing consistency discipline.
- "what is in your hands" renders ⲛⲉⲧⲛⲧⲟⲧⲧⲏⲛⲉ — the relative ⲛⲉⲧⲉ- plus ⲛ-ⲧⲟⲟⲧ "in the hand" with suffix "of you (pl.)." Kept plain, not expanded into "your possessions."
- "give them" renders the imperative ⲧⲁⲁⲩ ⲛⲁⲩ — kept as a command in Coptic order.
- "and say to yourselves" renders ⲛⲧⲉⲧⲛϫⲟⲟⲥ ⲛⲏⲧⲛ — circumstantial "saying to yourselves," kept as the continuing instruction.
- "On what day" renders ⲁϣ ⲛϩⲟⲟⲩ — left as the plain question; the draft does not answer it.
- "they will come and take what is theirs" renders ⲡⲉⲧⲟⲩⲛⲏⲩ ⲛⲥⲉϫⲓⲡⲉⲧⲉⲡⲱⲟⲩ — future ⲡⲉⲧ-ⲟⲩⲛⲏⲩ "they will come," conjunctive ⲛⲥⲉϫⲓ "and take," and ⲡⲉⲧⲉⲡⲱⲟⲩ "that which is theirs." The "theirs" stands unglossed — whose, and what kind of taking, the Coptic does not say.
- No parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).
- G1: checked sentence by sentence against base sentences s0318–s0320 (chapter division ch.88); no Greek printed or invented; no modern translation consulted or quoted.

# Logion 89

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0321–s0322, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉⲉⲧⲃⲉⲟⲩ ⲧⲉⲧⲛⲉⲓⲱⲉ ⲙⲡⲥⲁ ⲛⲃⲟⲗ ⲙⲡⲡⲟⲧⲏⲣⲓⲟⲛ
ⲧⲉⲧⲛⲣⲛⲟⲉⲓ ⲁⲛ ϫⲉⲡⲉⲛⲧⲁϥⲧⲁⲙⲓⲟ ⲙⲡⲥⲁ ⲛϩⲟⲩⲛ ⲛⲧⲟϥ ⲟⲛ ⲡⲉⲛⲧⲁϥⲧⲁⲙⲓⲟ ⲙⲡⲥⲁ ⲛⲃⲟⲗ ·

## English draft

Jesus said: Why do you wash the outside of the cup? Do you not understand that the one who made the inside also made the outside?

## Notes

- "Why do you wash" renders ⲉⲧⲃⲉⲟⲩ ⲧⲉⲧⲛⲉⲓⲱⲉ — the plain question "for what reason / why" with ⲉⲓⲱⲉ "wash" and the second-person-plural subject. Kept as a question, not an accusation.
- "the outside" / "the inside" render ⲡⲥⲁ ⲛⲃⲟⲗ / ⲙⲡⲥⲁ ⲛϩⲟⲩⲛ — ⲡⲥⲁ "side" with ⲛⲃⲟⲗ "outward" and ⲛϩⲟⲩⲛ "inward." The draft keeps the mirror of the Coptic: same side-word both times.
- "the cup" renders ⲡⲡⲟⲧⲏⲣⲓⲟⲛ — the ποτήριον loan (base file tags it OrigLang=grc). Kept as "cup," not "drinking vessel."
- "Do you not understand" renders ⲧⲉⲧⲛⲣⲛⲟⲉⲓ ⲁⲛ — ⲣⲛⲟⲉⲓ (νοεῖν) "perceive, understand," negated. Kept as the plain question.
- "the one who made" renders ⲡⲉⲛⲧⲁϥⲧⲁⲙⲓⲟ — ⲧⲁⲙⲓⲟ "make, create" in the relative past. Kept identical in both halves ("made the inside … made the outside"), not varied away.
- "also" renders ⲛⲧⲟϥ ⲟⲛ "he too / he also" — the emphatic pronoun carries the point of the second clause, kept as "also."
- No parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).
- G1: checked sentence by sentence against base sentences s0321–s0322 (chapter division ch.89); no Greek printed or invented; no modern translation consulted or quoted.

# Logion 90

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0323–s0324, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉⲁⲙⲏⲉⲓⲧⲛ ϣⲁⲣⲟⲓ ϫⲉⲟⲩⲭⲣⲏⲥⲧⲟⲥ ⲡⲉ ⲡⲁⲛⲁϩⲃ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲧⲁⲙⲛⲧϫⲟⲉⲓⲥ ⲟⲩⲣⲙⲣⲁϣ
ⲧⲉ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲧⲉⲧⲛⲛⲁϩⲉ ⲉⲩⲁⲛⲁⲡⲁⲩⲥⲓⲥ ⲛⲏⲧⲛ

Jesus said: Come to me, for my yoke is good and my lordship is gentle — and you will find rest for yourselves.

- "Come to me" renders the imperative ⲁⲙⲏⲉⲓⲧⲛ ϣⲁⲣⲟⲓ — kept as the plain invitation.
- "is good" renders ⲟⲩⲭⲣⲏⲥⲧⲟⲥ ⲡⲉ — the Greek loan χρηστός (base file tags it OrigLang=grc), "good, kind, useful." Fork named honestly here and not smoothed: χρηστός sits one letter from χριστός, and the manuscript reads χρηστός — the draft follows the manuscript as printed and does not import "Christ" into the word.
- "my yoke" renders ⲡⲁⲛⲁϩⲃ — kept plain; "my lordship" renders ⲧⲁⲙⲛⲧϫⲟⲉⲓⲥ "my dominion, my being-lord." The draft does not soften it to "burden" — the Coptic pairs yoke with lordship, kept as written.
- "is gentle" renders ⲟⲩⲣⲙⲣⲁϣ — stative "gentle, mild, easy."
- SENTENCE-SPLIT HONESTY NOTE: the base file attaches the stray particle ⲧⲉ as the first token of s0324; the draft reads it as the close of the preceding clause ("…is gentle") before the "and you will find" turn, and says so here rather than inventing a translation for the particle.
- "you will find rest for yourselves" renders ⲧⲉⲧⲛⲛⲁϩⲉ ⲉⲩⲁⲛⲁⲡⲁⲩⲥⲓⲥ ⲛⲏⲧⲛ — with ϩⲉ "find" in the future, and ⲁⲛⲁⲡⲁⲩⲥⲓⲥ (ἀνάπαυσις loan) "rest" kept uniform with Logia 50, 51, and 60. Scribal-spelling note from the base file: the manuscript's ⲁⲛⲁ{ⲩ}ⲡⲁ⟨ⲩ⟩ⲥⲓⲥ carries a deleted and a supplied letter; the normalized text above follows the edition.
- No parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

> DRAFT — working translation from the Coptic (and Greek where it survives), prepared with AI assistance; not reviewed by a Coptic scholar; not finished. Corrections invited and credited.

# Logion 91

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0325–s0328, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲁⲩ ⲛⲁϥ ϫⲉ ϫⲟⲟⲥ ⲉⲣⲟⲛ ϫⲉ ⲛⲧⲕ ⲛⲓⲙ ϩⲓⲛⲁ ⲉⲛⲛⲁⲣⲡⲓⲥⲧⲉⲩⲉ ⲉⲣⲟⲕ

ⲡⲉϫⲁϥ ⲛⲁⲩ ϫⲉ ⲧⲉⲧⲛⲣⲡⲉⲓⲣⲁⲍⲉ ⲙⲡϩⲟ ⲛⲧⲡⲉ ⲙⲛⲡⲕⲁϩ

ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲉⲧⲛⲡⲉⲧⲛⲙⲧⲟ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲙⲡⲉⲧⲛⲥⲟⲩⲱⲛϥ

ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲉⲓⲕⲁⲓⲣⲟⲥ ⲧⲉⲧⲛⲥⲟⲟⲩⲛ ⲁⲛ ⲛⲣⲡⲉⲓⲣⲁⲍⲉ ⲙⲙⲟϥ

## English draft

They said to him: "Tell us who you are, so that we may believe in you."

He said to them: "You examine the face of heaven and earth, and the one standing before you, you do not know — and as for this moment, you do not know how to test it."

## Notes

- "Tell us who you are" renders ϫⲟⲟⲥ ⲉⲣⲟⲛ ϫⲉ ⲛⲧⲕ ⲛⲓⲙ — imperative of ϫⲱ "say" with the ⲥ object ("tell it"), ⲉⲣⲟⲛ "to us," and the lit. "you are who" question. Kept plain.
- "so that we may believe in you" renders ϩⲓⲛⲁ ⲉⲛⲛⲁⲣⲡⲓⲥⲧⲉⲩⲉ ⲉⲣⲟⲕ — the base file marks the ⲉ…ⲛⲁ construction "Optative / 'Second Future'"; πιστεύω is a Greek loan (base file tags it OrigLang=grc). "Believe" kept plain, not "have faith" glossed.
- "examine" renders ⲣⲡⲉⲓⲣⲁⲍⲉ (πειράζω loan, base file tags it OrigLang=grc) in "you examine the face of heaven and earth" (ⲙⲡϩⲟ ⲛⲧⲡⲉ ⲙⲛⲡⲕⲁϩ). Fork named honestly and not smoothed: πειράζω spans test / examine / try / tempt; "examine" fits the sky-reading half of the saying, "test" the closing half — the draft keeps both English words rather than forcing one onto both clauses.
- "the one standing before you" renders ⲡⲉⲧⲛⲡⲉⲧⲛⲙⲧⲟ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ — lit. "the one who is in your presence"; ⲙⲧⲟ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ = "presence, before the face." The base file tags the phrase entity="person." No identity supplied beyond what is written.
- "you do not know" renders ⲙⲡⲉⲧⲛⲥⲟⲩⲱⲛϥ — aorist negative ("you have not known him"). Fork named: the draft uses the plain present; "you have not come to know him" is the harder floor.
- "this moment" renders ⲡⲉⲓⲕⲁⲓⲣⲟⲥ (καιρός loan, base file tags it OrigLang=grc, entity="time") — "time, season, the right moment" kept plain as "this moment."
- "how to test it" renders ⲛⲣⲡⲉⲓⲣⲁⲍⲉ ⲙⲙⲟϥ — lit. "for the testing of it" (nominalized verb with ⲛⲧⲉ). Fork named honestly: the suffix ϥ is "him" or "it" — the one before them, or this moment. The draft reads "it" (the moment) and names the fork rather than deciding it silently.
- ⲥⲟⲟⲩⲛ "know" kept uniform across the two clauses; ⲡⲉϫⲁⲩ "they said" and ⲡⲉϫⲁϥ "he said" kept as the plain dialogue frame.
- No parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).
- G1: checked sentence by sentence against base sentences s0325–s0328 (chapter division ch.91); no Greek printed or invented; no modern translation consulted or quoted.

Logion 92  (source worksheet: logion-092.md)

> DRAFT — working translation from the Coptic (and Greek where it survives), prepared with AI assistance; not reviewed by a Coptic scholar; not finished. Corrections invited and credited.

# Logion 92

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0329–s0330, normalized text as in the base file)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉ ϣⲓⲛⲉ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲧⲉⲧⲛⲛⲁϭⲓⲛⲉ

ⲁⲗⲗⲁ ⲛⲉⲧⲁⲧⲉⲧⲛϫⲛⲟⲩⲓ ⲉⲣⲟⲟⲩ ⲛⲛⲓϩⲟⲟⲩ ⲉⲙⲡⲓϫⲟⲟⲩ ⲛⲏⲧⲛ ⲙⲡϩⲟⲟⲩ ⲉⲧⲙⲙⲁⲩ ⲧⲉⲛⲟⲩ ⲉϩⲛⲁⲓ ⲉϫⲟⲟⲩ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲧⲉⲧⲛϣⲓⲛⲉ ⲁⲛ ⲛⲥⲱⲟⲩ

## English draft

Jesus said: "Seek, and you will find. But the things you asked me about in those days, I did not tell you then. Now I want to tell them — and you do not ask about them."

## Notes

- "Seek … find" keeps ϣⲓⲛⲉ / ϭⲓⲛⲉ uniform with Logion 2 ("seeks … finds") and later Logion 94. The two verbs are never swapped or smoothed into one.
- "the things you asked me about" renders ⲛⲉⲧⲁⲧⲉⲧⲛϫⲛⲟⲩⲓ ⲉⲣⲟⲟⲩ — ϫⲛⲟⲩ = "ask, question"; ⲉⲓ = "me"; ⲉⲣⲟⲟⲩ = "about them." Kept plain: asked, not "inquired" elevated.
- "in those days" renders ⲛⲛⲓϩⲟⲟⲩ (base file tags it entity="time"); "then" renders ⲙⲡϩⲟⲟⲩ ⲉⲧⲙⲙⲁⲩ — lit. "on that day." The draft does not invent "back then"; "then" is the plain floor.
- "I did not tell you" renders ⲉⲙⲡⲓϫⲟⲟⲩ ⲛⲏⲧⲛ — circumstantial negative past: "I did not say them to you." ϫⲱ "say" kept plain as "tell" in the giving-information sense.
- "I want to tell them" renders ⲉϩⲛⲁⲓ ⲉϫⲟⲟⲩ — aorist of ϩⲛⲁⲓ "wish, want; be about to." Fork named honestly: "I want to tell them" vs. "I am about to tell them." The draft takes "want" and names the fork.
- "you do not ask about them" renders ⲧⲉⲧⲛϣⲓⲛⲉ ⲁⲛ ⲛⲥⲱⲟⲩ — ϣⲓⲛⲉ ⲛⲥⲱ- = "seek after, inquire about." The ϣⲓⲛⲉ root echoes the opening "Seek" on purpose — the saying turns on that echo, and the draft keeps it rather than varying the word away.
- The ⲓ︤ⲥ︥ in s0329 is the nomen sacrum for ⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ, as the base file's normalized text expands it; nothing is invented beyond the edition's own expansion.
- Doublet discipline: the "seek and you will find" family (Logia 2, 92, 94) is footnoted, never merged — each occurrence stands on its own worksheet.
- No parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).
- G1: checked sentence by sentence against base sentences s0329–s0330 (chapter division ch.92); no Greek printed or invented; no modern translation consulted or quoted.

# Logion 93

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0331–s0332, normalized text as in the base file; bracketed letters as in the base file's manuscript layer — see notes)

ϫⲉⲙⲡⲣϯⲡⲉⲧⲟⲩⲁⲁⲃ ⲛⲛⲟⲩϩⲟⲣ ϫⲉⲕⲁⲥ ⲛⲟⲩⲛⲟϫⲟⲩ ⲉⲧⲕⲟⲡⲣⲓⲁ

ⲙⲡⲣⲛⲟⲩϫⲉ ⲛⲙⲙⲁⲣⲅⲁⲣⲓⲧⲏ[ⲥ ⲛ]ⲛ̣ⲉϣⲁⲩ ϩⲓⲛⲁ ϫⲉⲛⲟⲩⲁⲁϥ · ⲛⲗⲁ...

## English draft

Do not give what is holy to the dogs, lest they throw it to the dung-heap.

Do not throw pearls to the swine, lest they make them …

[The manuscript is damaged here; the sentence breaks off. Nothing is supplied.]

## Notes

- SPEAKER ATTRIBUTION HONESTY: no "Jesus said" opens this logion — unlike Logia 92, 94, and 95, the saying begins directly with the command. The leading ϫⲉ is the edition's clause-linker; the draft carries the command without inventing a speaker line.
- "Do not give" renders ⲙⲡⲣϯ — the prohibitive "do not give." "Do not throw" renders ⲙⲡⲣⲛⲟⲩϫⲉ — prohibitive of ⲛⲟⲩϫⲉ "throw, cast." The two verbs kept distinct, not collapsed.
- "what is holy" renders ⲡⲉⲧⲟⲩⲁⲁⲃ — lit. "the holy [thing]." Kept plain.
- "the dogs" renders ⲛⲟⲩϩⲟⲟⲣ — the base file notes this as a special plural form; kept plain as "the dogs," not softened.
- "lest they throw it to the dung-heap" renders ϫⲉⲕⲁⲥ ⲛⲟⲩⲛⲟϫⲟⲩ ⲉⲧⲕⲟⲡⲣⲓⲁ — ϫⲉⲕⲁⲥ + negative optative ("lest"); ⲛⲟϫⲟⲩ = "throw" (ⲛⲟⲩϫⲉ), object "it" = the holy thing; ⲧⲕⲟⲡⲣⲓⲁ = κοπρία loan (base file tags it OrigLang=grc), "dung-heap, manure-pile." Kept hard and plain.
- "pearls" renders ⲙⲙⲁⲣⲅⲁⲣⲓⲧⲏⲥ — μαργαρίτης loan (base file tags it OrigLang=grc). Kept as the loan's plain floor.
- "the swine" renders ⲛⲉϣⲁⲩ — "pigs, swine"; kept as "swine."
- "lest they make them" renders ϩⲓⲛⲁ ϫⲉⲛⲟⲩⲁⲁϥ — ⲁⲁ = "make, do" (ⲉⲓⲣⲉ), object "them" = the pearls. What "make them" completes into is lost with the manuscript.
- MANUSCRIPT DAMAGE — recorded on the page, nothing invented: ⲙⲁⲣⲅⲁⲣⲓⲧⲏ̣[ⲥ carries a supplied final ⲥ; ⲛ̄]ⲛ̣ⲉϣⲁⲩ carries a supplied ⲛ̄ and a dotted (uncertain) ⲛ̣; the sentence ends in a lacuna that the base file prints as ⲛ̣ⲗ̣ⲁ̣[...] (gap_reason="lac…" in the manuscript layer). The draft stops where the manuscript stops.
- The middle-dot · before the lacuna is as printed in the base file's normalized text, not added by this draft.
- No parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).
- G1: checked sentence by sentence against base sentences s0331–s0332 (chapter division ch.93); no Greek printed or invented; no modern translation consulted or quoted; no completion of the damaged sentence attempted.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0335–s0336; brackets and dotted letters as in the base file's manuscript layer — see notes)

[ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉ] ⲉ̣ϣⲱⲡⲉ ⲟⲩⲛⲧⲏⲧⲛ ϩⲟⲙⲧ ⲙⲡⲣϯ ⲉⲧⲙⲏⲥⲉ

ⲁⲗⲗⲁ ϯ̣ ⲙ̣[ⲙⲟϥ] ⲙ̣ⲡ̣ⲉ̣ⲧ̣[ⲉ]ⲧⲛⲛⲁϫⲓⲧⲟⲩ ⲁⲛ ⲛⲧⲟⲟⲧϥ

## English draft

Jesus [said]: "If you have money, do not lend it at interest — but give it to the one from whom you will not take it."

## Notes

- CONJECTURAL RESTORATION — read before using this worksheet: the whole opening [ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉ] ("Jesus said:") is missing in the manuscript; the base file records it as "conjectural restoration; cf. - "If you have money" renders ⲉ̣ϣⲱⲡⲉ ⲟⲩⲛⲧⲏⲧⲛ ϩⲟⲙⲧ — conditional ⲉϣⲱⲡⲉ with the existential ⲟⲩⲛⲧⲏⲧⲛ "there is to you"; ϩⲟⲙⲧ = "money" (lit. copper/bronze; the base file tags it entity="object"). The ⲉ̣ is dotted (uncertain) in the manuscript layer; recorded, not smoothed.
- "do not lend it at interest" renders ⲙⲡⲣϯ ⲉⲧⲙⲏⲥⲉ — prohibitive ⲙⲡⲣ + ϯ ⲉ ("give to") + ⲧⲙⲏⲥⲉ "interest, usury." Fork named honestly: the literal floor is "do not give [it] to interest"; "do not lend at interest" is the plain-English carrying of it. The draft takes the latter and names the fork.
- "but give it to the one from whom you will not take it" renders ⲁⲗⲗⲁ ϯ ⲙⲙⲟϥ ⲙⲡⲉⲧⲉⲧⲛⲛⲁϫⲓⲧⲟⲩ ⲁⲛ ⲛⲧⲟⲟⲧϥ — ⲁⲗⲗⲁ (ἀλλά loan) "but"; ϯ ⲙⲙⲟϥ "give it"; the recipient clause: "the one from whom (ⲛⲧⲟⲟⲧϥ) you will not take (ϫⲓ) it." "Back" is not in the Coptic — the draft does not add "back," and says so.
- MANUSCRIPT DAMAGE — recorded on the page: ⲙ̣[ⲙⲟϥ] is supplied conjecturally (base file cites Layton 1989, p. 86); ⲙ̣ⲡ̣ⲉ̣ⲧ̣[ⲉ]ⲧⲛ carries dotted (uncertain) letters and a supplied ⲉ; ϯ̣ is dotted. The normalized text is quoted as the edition prints it; the damage is the manuscript's, not this draft's invention.
- No parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0337–s0338; dotted letters and brackets as in the base file's manuscript layer — see notes)

ⲡ̣[ⲉϫ]ⲉ̣ⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉ ⲧⲙⲛⲧⲣⲣⲟ ⲙⲡⲉⲓⲱⲧ ⲉⲥⲧⲛⲧⲱ[ⲛ ⲉⲩⲥϩⲓⲙⲉ ⲁⲥϫⲓ ⲛⲟⲩⲕⲟⲩⲓ ⲛⲥⲁⲓⲣ ⲁ[ⲥϩ]ⲟ̣ⲡϥ ϩⲛⲟⲩϣⲱⲧⲉ ⲁⲥⲁⲁϥ ⲛϩⲉⲛⲛⲟ[ϭ ⲛⲛⲟⲉⲓⲕ

ⲡⲉⲧⲉⲩⲙⲙⲁⲁϫⲉ ⲙⲙⲟϥ ⲙⲁ[ⲣⲉ]ϥ̣ⲥⲱⲧⲙ

Jesus said: "The kingdom of the Father is like a woman who took a little yeast, hid it in the dough, and made it into big loaves. The one who has ears to hear, let him hear."

The speaker line is present (only damaged), so the draft prints "Jesus said:" without brackets and records the damage here.
- "yeast" renders ⲥⲁⲓⲣ. Fork named honestly: the floor is leaven/yeast; "yeast" is the peasant-plain carrying, "leaven" the technical one. The draft takes "yeast" and names the fork.

- The closing ears formula is rendered identically to Logia 8, 21, 63, and 65 ("The one who has ears to hear, let him hear") — each on its own worksheet, never merged.

# Logion 97

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0339–s0342; dotted letters and brackets as in the base file's manuscript layer — see notes)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉ ⲧⲙⲛⲧⲣⲣⲟ ⲙⲡⲉ̣[ⲓⲱⲧ] ⲉⲥⲧⲛⲧⲱⲛ ⲉⲩⲥϩⲓⲙⲉ ⲉⲥϥⲓ ϩⲁⲟⲩϭ̣[ⲉⲗⲙⲁⲓ] ⲉϥⲙⲉϩ ⲛⲛⲟⲉⲓⲧ

ⲉⲥⲙⲟⲟϣⲉ ϩ̣[ⲓⲧⲉ]ϩⲓⲏ · ⲉⲥⲟⲩⲏⲩ ⲁⲡⲙⲁⲁϫⲉ ⲙⲡϭⲉⲗⲙⲁⲓ ⲟⲩⲱϭⲡ · ⲁⲡⲛⲟⲉⲓⲧ ϣⲟⲩⲟ ⲛⲥⲱⲥ̣ [ϩ]ⲓ̣ⲧⲉϩⲓⲏ

ⲛⲉⲥⲥⲟⲟⲩⲛ ⲁⲛ ⲡⲉ ⲛⲉⲙⲡⲉⲥⲉⲓⲙⲉ ⲉϩⲓⲥⲉ

ⲛⲧⲉⲣⲉⲥⲡⲱϩ ⲉϩⲟⲩⲛ ⲉⲡⲉⲥⲏⲓ ⲁⲥⲕⲁⲡϭⲉⲗⲙⲁⲓ ⲉⲡⲉⲥⲏⲧ · ⲁⲥϩⲉ ⲉⲣⲟϥ ⲉϥϣⲟⲩⲉⲓⲧ ·

## English draft

Jesus said: "The kingdom of the Father is like a woman carrying a jar full of flour. While she was walking on the road, still far off, the handle of the jar broke, and the flour spilled out behind her on the road. She did not know it; she had felt no weight. When she got inside her house, she set the jar down and found it empty."

## Notes

- MANUSCRIPT DAMAGE — recorded on the page: ⲙⲡⲉ̣[ⲓⲱⲧ] (dotted ⲉ, supplied ⲓⲱⲧ); the jar word is conjecturally restored — the manuscript reads ϭⲗ̣[ⲙⲉⲉⲓ] (dotted ϭ, supplied ⲙⲉⲉⲓ) and the base normalizes to ϭⲉⲗⲙⲁⲓ, citing Layton 1989, p. 88; ϩ̣[ⲓⲧⲉ] and [ϩ]ⲓ̣ⲧⲉ are conjectural restorations (same citation); ⲛⲥⲱⲥ̣ carries a dotted (uncertain) ⲥ. The · marks are the base edition's punctuation, carried over. Nothing is invented beyond the edition; the damage is the manuscript's, not this draft's.
- "a jar" renders ⲟⲩϭⲉⲗⲙⲁⲓ (restored; see above). "full of flour": ⲉϥⲙⲉϩ stative of ⲙⲟⲩϩ "be full," and ⲛⲟⲉⲓⲧ = meal/flour — the draft takes "flour" as the peasant-plain floor and names the meal/flour fork. ⲛⲟⲉⲓⲧ is kept distinct from ⲛⲟⲉⲓⲕ "loaf/bread" at Logion 96.
- "the handle" renders ⲡⲙⲁⲁϫⲉ. Noted honestly: ⲙⲁⲁϫⲉ is the same word as "ear" in the Logia 8/21/63/65/96 ears formula — the wordplay is on the page, not smoothed, but the two senses are not conflated in the draft.
- "still far off" renders ⲉⲥⲟⲩⲏⲩ — circumstantial, stative of ⲟⲩⲉ "be far/distant." Fork named: the Coptic does not say far from *what* (the house is implied by the next sentence, not stated); the draft does not supply "[from home]" and says so.
- "she had felt no weight" renders ⲛⲉⲙⲡⲉⲥⲉⲓⲙⲉ ⲉϩⲓⲥⲉ — preterite negative of ⲉⲓⲙⲉ "perceive/know" with object ϩⲓⲥⲉ. Fork named: ϩⲓⲥⲉ spans trouble, toil, and weight; the draft takes the literal floor "no weight" and does not gloss the saying's point into the text. Reader-lens readings (Robert's or anyone's) do not bend this line.
- "spilled out" (ϣⲟⲩⲟ) and "empty" (ϣⲟⲩⲉⲓⲧ, stative of the same root) keep the shared root visible across the two sentences — "the flour spilled out … she found it empty."
- "set the jar down" renders ⲁⲥⲕⲁⲡϭⲉⲗⲙⲁⲓ ⲉⲡⲉⲥⲏⲧ — ⲕⲱ "set/put" + ⲉⲡⲉⲥⲏⲧ "downward/down."
- No parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).
- G1: checked sentence by sentence against base sentences s0339–s0342 (chapter division ch.97); no Greek printed or invented; no modern translation consulted or quoted; restorations flagged as the edition's (Layton 1989, p. 88), not this draft's.

Logion 98  (source worksheet: logion-098.md)
========================================================================

> DRAFT — working translation from the Coptic (and Greek where it survives), prepared with AI assistance; not reviewed by a Coptic scholar; not finished. Corrections invited and credited.

# Logion 98

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0343–s0345; no manuscript damage flags on these sentences)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ⲧⲙⲛⲧⲣⲣⲟ ⲙⲡⲉⲓⲱⲧ ⲉⲥⲧⲛⲧⲱⲛ ⲉⲩⲣⲱⲙⲉ ⲉϥⲟⲩⲱϣ ⲉⲙⲟⲩⲧⲟⲩⲣⲱⲙⲉ ⲙⲙⲉⲅⲓⲥⲧⲁⲛⲟⲥ

ⲁϥϣⲱⲗⲙ ⲛⲧⲥⲏϥⲉ ϩⲙⲡⲉϥⲏⲓ ⲁϥϫⲟⲧⲥ ⲛⲧϫⲟ ϫⲉⲕⲁⲁⲥ ⲉϥⲛⲁⲉⲓⲙⲉ ϫⲉⲧⲉϥϭⲓϫ ⲛⲁⲧⲱⲕ ⲉϩⲟⲩⲛ

ⲧⲟⲧⲉ ⲁϥϩⲱⲧⲙ ⲙⲡⲙⲉⲅⲓⲥⲧⲁⲛⲟⲥ

## English draft

Jesus said: "The kingdom of the Father is like a man who wanted to kill one of the great men. He drew the sword in his house and drove it into the wall, to find out whether his hand could drive it all the way through. Then he killed the great man."

## Notes

- No ϫⲉ clause-linker follows ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ here (unlike Logia 96–97) — the saying opens directly. The draft carries the saying without inventing a linker, and records the difference here.
- "one of the great men" renders ⲟⲩⲣⲱⲙⲉ ⲙⲙⲉⲅⲓⲥⲧⲁⲛⲟⲥ. Fork named honestly: ⲙⲉⲅⲓⲥⲧⲁⲛⲟⲥ is the Greek loan μεγιστᾶνες (nobles/magnates/great men); "one of the great men" is the plain-English carrying. The draft takes it and names the fork.
- "He drew the sword" renders ⲁϥϣⲱⲗⲙ ⲛⲧⲥⲏϥⲉ — first occurrence of ϣⲱⲗⲙ "draw" with ⲥⲏϥⲉ "sword" as object; "in his house" is ϩⲙⲡⲉϥⲏⲓ.
- "drove it into the wall" renders ⲁϥϫⲟⲧⲥ ⲛⲧϫⲟ — ϫⲟⲧⲥ "pierced/stabbed it" + ⲛⲧϫⲟ "into the wall" (ϫⲟ = wall). "Drove" is the plain carrying of the stabbing action; the fork (stab/pierce/drive) is named.
- The wall-test clause: ⲉϥⲛⲁⲉⲓⲙⲉ ϫⲉⲧⲉϥϭⲓϫ ⲛⲁⲧⲱⲕ ⲉϩⲟⲩⲛ — literally "so that he would know that his hand would be strong, inward." Fork named: ⲧⲱⲕ = be strong/firm, ⲉϩⲟⲩⲛ = inward/inside — the draft's "whether his hand could drive it all the way through" is the plain-English carrying of the test's point (stabbing the wall to prove his hand for the killing), and the literal floor is recorded here, not smoothed.
- "Then he killed the great man" renders ⲧⲟⲧⲉ ⲁϥϩⲱⲧⲙ ⲙⲡⲙⲉⲅⲓⲥⲧⲁⲛⲟⲥ — ⲧⲟⲧⲉ is the Greek loan τότε "then." Spelling flag: the base prints ϩⲱⲧⲙ̄ where Sahidic expects ϩⲱⲧⲃ "kill"; the sense "killed" is carried by the saying's context (the assassination succeeds after the hand-test), and the spelling is recorded here, not normalized away.
- No parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).
- G1: checked sentence by sentence against base sentences s0343–s0345 (chapter division ch.98); no Greek printed or invented; no modern translation consulted or quoted.

# Logion 99

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0346–s0348; the · mark is the base edition's punctuation — see notes)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲙⲙⲁⲑⲏⲧⲏⲥ ⲛⲁϥ ϫⲉⲛⲉⲕ·ⲥⲛⲏⲩ ⲙⲛⲧⲉⲕⲙⲁⲁⲩ ⲥⲉⲁϩⲉⲣⲁⲧⲟⲩ ϩⲓⲡⲥⲁⲛⲃⲟⲗ

ⲡⲉϫⲁϥ ⲛⲁⲩ ϫⲉⲛⲉⲧⲛⲛⲉⲓⲙⲁ ⲉⲧⲉⲓⲣⲉ ⲙⲡⲟⲩⲱϣ ⲙⲡⲁⲉⲓⲱⲧ ⲛⲁⲓ ⲛⲉ ⲛⲁⲥⲛⲏⲩ ⲙⲛⲧⲁⲙⲁⲁⲩ

ⲛⲧⲟⲟⲩ ⲡⲉ ⲉⲧⲛⲁⲃⲱⲕ ⲉϩⲟⲩⲛ ⲉⲧⲙⲛⲧⲣⲣⲟ ⲙⲡⲁⲉⲓⲱⲧ

## English draft

The disciples said to him: "Your brothers and your mother are standing outside."

He said to them: "Those here who do the will of my Father — these are my brothers and my mother. They are the ones who will enter the kingdom of my Father."

## Notes

- "are standing" renders ⲥⲉⲁϩⲉ ⲣⲁⲧⲟⲩ — the idiom ⲁϩⲉ ⲣⲁⲧ "stand (on) feet" = "stand." The idiom is noted and rendered plainly, not literally.
- The manuscript punctuation ⲛⲉⲕ·ⲥⲛⲏⲩ (· between the possessive "your" and "brothers") is carried in the quote as the base prints it; it is the edition's punctuation mark, not a word.
- "Those here" renders ⲛ ⲉⲧ ⲛ ⲛⲉⲓ ⲙⲁ — "the ones who are in these places." Fork named: the literal floor is "those in these places"; "those here" is the plain carrying. The draft takes it and names the fork.
- "the will of my Father": ⲟⲩⲱϣ here is the noun "will" — the same root as the verb ⲟⲩⲱϣ "want" at Logion 98 ("who wanted to kill"); the noun/verb kinship is noted, not collapsed.
- ⲉⲧⲙⲛⲧⲣⲣⲟ: the base normalizes the kingdom word as ⲙⲛⲧⲣⲣⲟ here (manuscript ⲙⲛ̄ⲧⲉⲣⲟ), versus ⲙⲛⲧⲣⲣⲟ at Logia 96–98. Quoted as the base prints each logion; spelling variation is recorded, not silently normalized.
- "outside" renders ϩⲓⲡⲥⲁⲛⲃⲟⲗ — ⲥⲁ ⲛⲃⲟⲗ "the outer side."
- No parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).
- G1: checked sentence by sentence against base sentences s0346–s0348 (chapter division ch.99); no Greek printed or invented; no modern translation consulted or quoted.

> DRAFT — working translation from the Coptic (and Greek where it survives), prepared with AI assistance; not reviewed by a Coptic scholar; not finished. Corrections invited and credited.

# Logion 100

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0349–s0350; the · mark is the base edition's punctuation — see notes)

ⲁⲩⲧⲥⲁⲃⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ⲉⲩⲛⲟⲩⲃ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲉϫⲁⲩ ⲛⲁϥ ϫⲉⲛⲉⲧⲏⲡ · ⲁⲕⲁⲓⲥⲁⲣ ⲥⲉϣⲓⲧⲉ ⲙⲙⲟⲛ ⲛⲛϣⲱⲙ

ⲡⲉϫⲁϥ ⲛⲁⲩ ϫⲉϯⲛⲁⲕⲁⲓⲥⲁⲣ ⲛⲕⲁⲓⲥⲁⲣ ϯⲛⲁⲡⲛⲟⲩⲧⲉ ⲙⲡⲛⲟⲩⲧⲉ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲉⲧⲉⲡⲱⲓ ⲡⲉ ⲙⲁⲧⲛⲛⲁⲓϥ

## English draft

They showed Jesus a gold coin and said to him: "Caesar's men demand the taxes from us."

He said to them: "Give to Caesar what is Caesar's, give to God what is God's — and what is mine, give to me."

## Notes

- "a gold coin" renders ⲉⲩⲛⲟⲩⲃ. Fork named honestly: the Coptic says "gold" (the base tags it as substance); "coin" is supplied by the scene's context (a tax question put to Jesus). The draft takes "a gold coin" as the plain carrying and names the fork.
- "Caesar's men" renders ⲛⲉⲧⲏⲡ ⲁⲕⲁⲓⲥⲁⲣ — literally "those counted/reckoned, Caesar's" (ⲏⲡ = stative of ⲱⲡ "count/reckon"). Fork named: the literal floor is "those who are counted"; "Caesar's men" is the plain-English carrying of the tax-collectors. The base's entity annotation identifies ⲕⲁⲓⲥⲁⲣ with Tiberius — that is the annotator's identification, noted here, not a rendering rule.
- "demand" renders ϣⲓⲧⲉ. Fork named: the verb spans ask, demand, require; "demand" fits the tax scene and the draft takes it, naming the fork.
- "the taxes" renders ⲛⲛϣⲱⲙ (plural). Fork named: ϣⲱⲙ spans taxes, dues, tribute; "taxes" is the plain floor.
- "what is Caesar's" renders ⲛⲁⲕⲁⲓⲥⲁⲣ — the possessive ⲛⲁ + proper noun construction (the base flags it as an NPN construction): "the things of Caesar." "Give to Caesar what is Caesar's" is the plain carrying; the construction is noted, not flattened.
- The third clause ⲡⲉⲧⲉⲡⲱⲓ ⲡⲉ ⲙⲁⲧⲛⲛⲁⲓϥ — "that which is mine (ⲡⲱ = 'mine'), give it to me (imperative ⲙⲁ + ⲧⲛ + ⲛⲁⲓ + ⲥⲩ)" — is rendered exactly as it stands. It has no canonical counterpart in the saying as Matthew/Mark/Luke carry it; noted as a Thomas-only clause on this worksheet. No parallel citations are added anywhere (PENDING ROBERT).
- No parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).
- G1: checked sentence by sentence against base sentences s0349–s0350 (chapter division ch.100); no Greek printed or invented; no modern translation consulted or quoted.

Logion 101  (source worksheet: logion-101.md)

> DRAFT — working translation from the Coptic (and Greek where it survives), prepared with AI assistance; not reviewed by a Coptic scholar; not finished. Corrections invited and credited.

# Logion 101

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0351–s0353; manuscript damage flagged in the notes — normalized edition followed)

ⲡⲉⲧⲛⲁⲙⲉⲥⲧⲉⲡⲉϥⲉⲓⲱⲧ ⲁⲛ ⲙⲛⲧⲉϥⲙⲁⲁⲩ ⲛⲧⲁϩⲉ ϥⲛⲁϣⲣⲙⲁⲑⲏⲧⲏⲥ ⲛⲁⲓ ⲁⲛ

ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲉⲧⲛⲁⲙⲣⲣⲉⲡⲉϥⲉⲓⲱⲧ ⲁⲛ ⲙⲛⲧⲉϥⲙⲁⲁⲩ ⲛⲧⲁϩⲉ ϥⲛⲁϣⲣⲙⲁⲑⲏⲧⲏⲥ ⲛⲁⲓ ⲁⲛ

ⲧⲁⲙⲁⲁⲩ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲛⲧⲁⲥ......... ..ⲟⲗ ⲧⲁⲙⲁⲁⲩ ⲇⲉ ⲙⲙⲉ ⲁⲥϯ ⲛⲁⲓ ⲙⲡⲱⲛϩ

## English draft

Whoever hates his father and his mother the way I do will not be my disciple.

And whoever loves his father and his mother the way I do will not be my disciple.

For my mother […], but my true mother gave me life.

## Notes

- "hates" renders ⲙⲉⲥⲧⲉ. Fork named: the verb spans hate and detest; the draft keeps it hard, consistent with Logion 55's ⲙⲟⲥⲧⲉ ("hate") — but Logion 55 is a separate saying on its own worksheet, footnoted here, never merged.
- "the way I do" renders ⲛⲧⲁϩⲉ. Fork named honestly: the phrase is "in this manner / in the way" — the draft takes it as pointing to the speaker's own example ("the way I do"); the bare floor would be "in the [same] manner." The draft takes the first and names the second.
- The hate clause and the love clause are kept as two parallel sentences, exactly as the Coptic lays them down. Nothing is combined, softened, or read as one saying wearing two masks.
- "my true mother" renders ⲧⲁⲙⲁⲁⲩ ⲇⲉ ⲙⲙⲉ. Fork named: ⲙⲙⲉ spans true, real, genuine; "true" is the plain carrying. The unnamed first mother and the named true mother are kept distinct.
- The lacuna in the third sentence is carried into the English as a marked gap, not filled. The manuscript layer (see orig-group attributes) shows heavy loss: ⲛ̄ⲧⲁⲥ̣[.........] with only [..]ⲟ̣ⲗ̣ legible at the clause's end, plus dotted letters elsewhere (ⲉⲓ̣[ⲱ]ⲧ, ⲙⲡⲱⲛϩ̣). The normalized edition is followed; no word is supplied, and the gap is not guessed at.
- "life" renders ⲡⲱⲛϩ — the noun of living/aliveness, kept plain.
- Doublet discipline: the father-and-mother hate wording echoes Logion 55; each stands on its own worksheet, never merged. Logion 101's twist (hate *and* love both barring discipleship) is this saying's own shape and is not harmonized with anything.
- No parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).
- G1: checked sentence by sentence against base sentences s0351–s0353 (chapter division ch.101, 3 sentences); no Greek printed or invented; no modern translation consulted or quoted.

# Logion 102

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentence s0354; supplied letters flagged in the notes — normalized edition followed)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉⲟⲩⲟⲉⲓ ⲛⲁⲩ ⲙⲫⲁⲣⲓⲥⲁⲓⲟⲥ ϫⲉⲉⲩⲉⲓⲛⲉ ⲛⲟⲩⲟⲩϩⲟⲣ ⲉϥⲛⲛⲕⲟⲧⲕ ϩⲓϫⲛⲡⲟⲩⲟⲙϥ ⲛϩⲛⲉϩⲟⲟⲩ ϫⲉⲟⲩⲧⲉ ϥⲟⲩⲱⲙ ⲁⲛ ⲟⲩⲧⲉ ϥⲕⲱ ⲁⲛ ⲛⲛⲉϩⲟⲟⲩ ⲉⲟⲩⲱⲙ

## English draft

Jesus said: "Woe to them, the Pharisees — they are like a dog asleep on the oxen's feed: it neither eats nor lets the oxen eat."

## Notes

- "Woe to them" renders ⲟⲩⲟⲉⲓ ⲛⲁⲩ. Fork named: ⲟⲩⲟⲉⲓ is the Coptic woe-cry (cf. the ⲟⲩⲟⲓ form elsewhere); "woe" is the plain floor. The dative ⲛⲁⲩ ("to them") is kept before naming the Pharisees, as the Coptic orders it.
- "like a dog asleep on the oxen's feed" renders ⲉⲩⲉⲓⲛⲉ ⲛⲟⲩⲟⲩϩⲟⲣ ⲉϥⲛⲛⲕⲟⲧⲕ ϩⲓϫⲛⲡⲟⲩⲟⲙϥ ⲛϩⲛⲉϩⲟⲟⲩ. ⲛⲕⲟⲧⲕ = sleep; ⲡⲟⲩⲟⲙϥ = the food/eating-place (orig layer reads ⲡⲟⲩⲟⲛⲉϥ "the food of the oxen"); ⲉϩⲟⲟⲩ = oxen/cattle. Fork named: the plain carrying "the oxen's feed" keeps the feed literal; "manger" would be a smoothing and is not taken.
- "it neither eats nor lets the oxen eat" renders ϫⲉⲟⲩⲧⲉ ϥⲟⲩⲱⲙ ⲁⲛ ⲟⲩⲧⲉ ϥⲕⲱ ⲁⲛ ⲛⲛⲉϩⲟⲟⲩ ⲉⲟⲩⲱⲙ. Fork named: ⲕⲱ here is the permissive "let/allow" — "lets the oxen eat"; the literal floor would be "does not give the oxen to eat." The draft takes the plain permissive carrying and names the floor.
- Supplied letters in the manuscript layer are recorded, not printed as restored text: [ϫⲉⲟ]ⲩⲟⲉⲓ, [ⲛ̄ⲛ]ⲟ̣ⲩⲟⲩϩⲟⲣ, ⲛ̄ϩ̣[ⲛ̄]ⲛ̣ⲉϩⲟⲟⲩ, ϥⲕ̣[ⲱ], plus the nomina-sacra abbreviation ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓ︤ⲥ︥ expanded in the normalized layer. The normalized edition is followed throughout; nothing is invented.
- No doublets in this slice to merge; the dog saying stands alone.
- No parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).
- G1: checked sentence by sentence against base sentence s0354 (chapter division ch.102, single sentence); no Greek printed or invented; no modern translation consulted or quoted.

> DRAFT — working translation from the Coptic (and Greek where it survives), prepared with AI assistance; not reviewed by a Coptic scholar; not finished. Corrections invited and credited.

# Logion 103

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentence s0355; the / mark is the base edition's manuscript punctuation — see notes)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉⲟⲩⲙⲁⲕⲁⲣⲓⲟⲥ ⲡⲉ ⲡⲣⲱⲙⲉ ⲡⲁⲓ ⲉⲧⲥⲟⲟⲩⲛ ϫⲉϩⲉⲛⲁϣ ⲙⲙⲉⲣⲟⲥ ⲉⲛⲗⲏⲥⲧⲏⲥ ⲛⲏⲩ ⲉϩⲟⲩⲛ ϩⲓⲛⲁ ⲉϥⲛⲁⲧⲱⲟⲩⲛ/ ⲛϥⲥⲱⲟⲩϩ ⲛⲧⲉϥⲙⲛⲧⲣⲣⲟ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛϥⲙⲟⲩⲣ ⲙⲙⲟϥ ⲉϫⲛⲧⲉϥϯⲡⲉ ϩⲁⲧⲉϩⲏ ⲉⲙⲡⲁⲧⲟⲩⲉⲓ ⲉϩⲟⲩⲛ

## English draft

Jesus said: "Blessed is the man who knows at what hour, in which part [of the night], the thieves will come in — so that he will get up, gather his kingdom, and gird himself before they come in."

## Notes

- "Blessed" renders ⲙⲁⲕⲁⲣⲓⲟⲥ, kept uniform with Logia 49/54/68/69/79/103 across the lane. "the man" renders ⲡⲣⲱⲙⲉ with the demonstrative ⲡⲁⲓ folded in ("this man who knows" would be stiffer; the relative ⲉⲧⲥⲟⲟⲩⲛ carries the clause).
- "at what hour, in which part [of the night]" renders ϫⲉϩⲉⲛⲁϣ ⲙⲙⲉⲣⲟⲥ. ⲁϣ = hour/time; ⲙⲉⲣⲟⲥ = part/portion. Fork named honestly: the Coptic says "at what hour, at which part" with no night-word written; "[of the night]" is supplied by the draft as the plain carrying of the night-watch sense, bracketed as supplied, not smuggled in. The other fork — leaving it as bare "at which part" — is the literal floor and is named here.
- "the thieves" renders ⲉⲛⲗⲏⲥⲧⲏⲥ (Greek λῃστής loan, plural); "will come in" renders ⲛⲏⲩ ⲉϩⲟⲩⲛ (future relative).
- "get up" renders ⲧⲱⲟⲩⲛ — rise/stand up, kept plain; the manuscript layer carries a / mark after it (ⲉϥⲛⲁⲧⲱⲟⲩⲛ/), which the base edition prints as manuscript punctuation, noted here, not interpreted.
- "gather his kingdom" renders ⲛϥⲥⲱⲟⲩϩ ⲛⲧⲉϥⲙⲛⲧⲣⲣⲟ. Fork named: ⲥⲱⲟⲩϩ = gather/collect (orig layer ⲛϥⲥⲱϩⲟⲩϩ); ⲙⲛⲧⲣⲣⲟ = "his kingdom" literally. The draft keeps the literal "his kingdom" rather than smoothing to "his household" or "his goods"; the smoothing is named as the fork not taken.
- "gird himself" renders ⲛϥⲙⲟⲩⲣ ⲙⲙⲟϥ — ⲙⲟⲩⲣ = bind/gird, with the reflexive ⲙⲙⲟϥ ("himself"); ⲉϫⲛⲧⲉϥϯⲡⲉ = "over his loins" (ϯⲡⲉ = loins). Plain: "gird himself" before "they come in" (ⲉⲙⲡⲁⲧⲟⲩⲉⲓ ⲉϩⲟⲩⲛ, "before they come").
- Manuscript layer notes: nomina-sacra ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓ︤ⲥ︥ expanded in normalized; supplied [ⲕⲁ] in ⲙⲁ̣[ⲕⲁ]ⲣⲓⲟⲥ, [ⲛ̄ⲁϣ] in ϫⲉϩ[ⲛ̄ⲁϣ], [ⲉϥ] in [ⲉϥ]ⲛ̣ⲁⲧⲱⲟⲩⲛ, [ⲁ] in ϩ̣[ⲁ]ⲧ̣ⲉϩⲏ, [ⲣⲟ] in ⲙⲛ︤ⲧ︥ⲉ̣[ⲣⲟ]. The normalized edition is followed; nothing restored beyond it.
- No doublets in this slice to merge.
- No parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).
- G1: checked sentence by sentence against base sentence s0355 (chapter division ch.103, single sentence); no Greek printed or invented; no modern translation consulted or quoted.

> DRAFT — working translation from the Coptic (and Greek where it survives), prepared with AI assistance; not reviewed by a Coptic scholar; not finished. Corrections invited and credited.

# Logion 104

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0356–s0359; the · mark is the base edition's manuscript punctuation — see notes)

ⲡⲉϫⲁⲩ ⲛⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉⲁⲙⲟⲩ ⲛⲧⲛϣⲗⲏⲗ ⲙⲡϩⲟⲟⲩ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛⲧⲛⲣⲛⲏⲥⲧⲉⲩⲉ

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉⲟⲩ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲡⲉ ⲡⲛⲟⲃⲉ ⲛⲧⲁⲉⲓⲁⲁϥ

ⲏ ⲛⲧⲁⲩϫⲣⲟ ⲉⲣⲟⲓ ϩⲛⲟⲩ

ⲁⲗⲗⲁ ϩⲟⲧⲁⲛ ⲉⲣϣⲁⲛⲡⲛⲩⲙⲫⲓⲟⲥ ⲉⲓ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϩⲙⲡⲛⲩⲙⲫⲱⲛ ⲧⲟⲧⲉ ⲙⲁⲣⲟⲩⲛⲏⲥⲧⲉⲩⲉ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲙⲁⲣⲟⲩϣⲗⲏⲗ ·

## English draft

They said to Jesus: "Come, let us pray today and let us fast."

Jesus said: "What then is the sin that I have committed?

Or in what have they defeated me?

But when the bridegroom comes out of the bridal chamber, then let them fast and let them pray."

## Notes

- "Come" renders ⲁⲙⲟⲩ (imperative of ⲉⲓ "come"); "let us pray" renders ⲛⲧⲛϣⲗⲏⲗ and "let us fast" renders ⲛⲧⲛⲣⲛⲏⲥⲧⲉⲩⲉ (ⲛⲧⲛ- optative, "let us"). ⲛⲏⲥⲧⲉⲩⲉ = fast (Greek νηστεύω loan); the same verb appears in the closing sentence (ⲙⲁⲣⲟⲩⲛⲏⲥⲧⲉⲩⲉ, "let them fast") and is rendered identically both times.
- "What then is the sin that I have committed?" renders ⲟⲩ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲡⲉ ⲡⲛⲟⲃⲉ ⲛⲧⲁⲉⲓⲁⲁϥ. ⲟⲩ = the "what?" question word; ⲅⲁⲣ is kept as "then"; ⲛⲧⲁⲉⲓⲁⲁϥ = the relative "which I did" (ⲁⲁϥ = do/commit). Fork named: the bare floor would be "What is the sin I have done?" — the draft takes the slightly fuller plain carrying and names the floor.
- "in what have they defeated me" renders ⲏ ⲛⲧⲁⲩϫⲣⲟ ⲉⲣⲟⲓ ϩⲛⲟⲩ. Fork named honestly: ϫⲣⲟ spans be strong, prevail, overcome; the draft takes "defeated" as the plain carrying of "got the better of" — the literal floor "in what have they prevailed against me" is named. ⲏ = "or," kept, so the second question stands as its own question.
- "the bridegroom" renders ⲡⲛⲩⲙⲫⲓⲟⲥ (Greek νυμφίος loan); "the bridal chamber" renders ⲡⲛⲩⲙⲫⲱⲛ (Greek νυμφών loan). The bridal-chamber word echoes Logion 75's ⲡⲁⲥⲧⲟⲥ ("bridal chamber") and Logion 104 uses the Greek loan instead — the echo is footnoted, never merged; each saying stands on its own worksheet.
- The closing ⲙⲁⲣⲟⲩ- optatives are third person ("let them fast and let them pray") — the draft keeps the pronoun shift exactly as written: the askers are "us," the answer turns them into "them." Nothing is smoothed.
- Manuscript layer notes: ⲛ̣[ⲓ︤]ⲥ︥ expanded to ⲛⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ; ⲙⲡⲟⲟⲩ normalized to ⲙⲡϩⲟⲟⲩ ("today"); the closing · mark is the base edition's manuscript punctuation, carried into the worksheet. Normalized edition followed; nothing invented.
- Fasting echo: the fasting language answers the fasting question of Logia 6/14 and Logion 27's fast-the-world construction across the gap — noted as an echo, not harmonized.
- No parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).
- G1: checked sentence by sentence against base sentences s0356–s0359 (chapter division ch.104, 4 sentences); no Greek printed or invented; no modern translation consulted or quoted.

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉⲡⲉⲧⲛⲁⲥⲟⲩⲱⲛⲡⲉⲓⲱⲧ ⲙⲛⲧⲙⲁⲁⲩ ⲥⲉⲛⲁⲙⲟⲩⲧⲉ ⲉⲣⲟϥ ϫⲉⲡϣⲏⲣⲉ ⲙⲡⲟⲣⲛⲏ

- "will be called" renders ⲥⲉⲛⲁⲙⲟⲩⲧⲉ ⲉⲣⲟϥ — the impersonal third-person plural future ("they will call him"), the Coptic passive idiom. The draft takes the plain passive carrying and names the construction.

- No ⲡⲉϫⲉ ("said") after the opening speaker line — the saying runs straight from ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉ into the relative clause, and the draft follows that shape.

# Logion 107

- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0364–s0367)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉⲧⲙⲛⲧⲣⲣⲟ ⲉⲥⲧⲛⲧⲱⲛ ⲉⲩⲣⲱⲙⲉ ⲛϣⲱⲥ ⲉⲩⲛⲧⲁϥ ⲙⲙⲁⲩ ⲛϣⲉ ⲛⲉⲥⲟⲟⲩ ⲁⲟⲩⲁ ⲛϩⲏⲧⲟⲩ ⲥⲱⲣⲙ ⲉⲣⲉⲡⲛⲟϭ ⲡⲉ ⲁϥⲕⲱ ⲙⲡⲥⲧⲉⲯⲓⲧ ⲁϥϣⲓⲛⲉ ⲛⲥⲁⲡⲓⲟⲩⲁ · ϣⲁⲛⲧⲉϥϩⲉ ⲉⲣⲟϥ ⲛⲧⲁⲣⲉϥϩⲓⲥⲉ ⲡⲉϫⲁϥ ⲙⲡⲉⲥⲟⲟⲩ ϫⲉϯⲟⲩⲟϣⲕ ⲡⲁⲣⲁⲡⲥⲧⲉⲯⲓⲧ

## English draft

Jesus said: "The kingdom is like a shepherd who had a hundred sheep. One of them went astray — the biggest one. He left the ninety-nine and sought the one, until he found it. When he had toiled, he said to the sheep: 'I want you more than the ninety-nine.'"

## Notes

- "the kingdom" renders ⲧⲙⲛⲧⲣⲣⲟ as the plain floor, uniform across the lane.
- "went astray" renders ⲥⲱⲣⲙ; the clause ⲉⲣⲉⲡⲛⲟϭ ⲡⲉ ("being the big one") is read as a circumstantial qualifier on the straying one — manuscript writes ⲉ where the edition expands ⲉⲣⲉ, noted honestly; no invented Greek.
- "the ninety-nine" renders ⲡⲥⲧⲉⲯⲓⲧ twice, kept uniform; "the one" renders ⲡⲓⲟⲩⲁ (the definite one, of the hundred).
- "When he had toiled" renders ⲛⲧⲁⲣⲉϥϩⲓⲥⲉ — the ⲛⲧⲁⲣⲉϥ- "when" clause with ϩⲓⲥⲉ "toil," the knowing-word kept uniform with Logion 58 ("toiled"). Fork named: ϩⲓⲥⲉ spans toil, labor, suffer; "toiled" is the plain floor here.
- "I want you" renders ⲟⲩⲟϣⲕ (ⲟⲩⲱϣⲕ) — want/desire; the draft follows the Coptic (G4 ruling 2026-10-11).
- "the sheep" renders ⲙⲡⲉⲥⲟⲟⲩ — manuscript reads ⲉⲥⲟ[[ⲩ]]ⲟⲩ with a corrected ⲩ (visible scribal correction); the normalized edition reads ⲉⲥⲟⲟⲩ, and the draft follows the normalized reading, with the correction flag on the page.
- No Greek printed or invented; Coptic alone, so stated.
- No doublets in this slice to merge.
- No parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).
- G1: checked against base sentences s0364–s0367 (chapter division ch.107, four sentences); manuscript-layer flags noted (ⲧⲛ̄ⲧⲱ(ⲛ), ⲉⲥⲟ[[ⲩ]]ⲟⲩ); no modern translation consulted or quoted.

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉⲡⲉⲧⲛⲁⲥⲱ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϩⲛⲧⲁⲧⲁⲡⲣⲟ ϥⲛⲁϣⲱⲡⲉ ⲛⲧⲁϩⲉ ⲁⲛⲟⲕ ϩⲱ ϯⲛⲁϣⲱⲡⲉ ⲉⲛⲧⲟϥ ⲡⲉ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛⲉⲧϩⲏⲡ · ⲛⲁⲟⲩⲱⲛϩ ⲉⲣⲟϥ

- "I myself will become him" renders ⲁⲛⲟⲕ ϩⲱ ϯⲛⲁϣⲱⲡⲉ ⲉⲛⲧⲟϥ ⲡⲉ — the doubled ⲁⲛⲟⲕ … ⲛⲧⲟϥ opposition kept on purpose: the saying's two directions (you → me, me → him) are its own shape, not collapsed into one.
- "the hidden things" renders ⲛⲉⲧϩⲏⲡ — the hidden ones/things, kept distinct from the reveal-verb: ⲛⲁⲟⲩⲱⲛϩ ⲉⲣⲟϥ "will be revealed to him" (ⲟⲩⲱⲛϩ kept uniform with Logion 5's reveal-word).
- Manuscript writes ⲏⲡ for the normalized ϩⲏⲡ; the draft follows the normalized edition and the flag is on the page.

# Logion 109

- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0370–s0374)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉⲧⲙⲛⲧⲣⲣⲟ ⲉⲥⲧⲛⲧⲱⲛ ⲉⲩⲣⲱⲙⲉ ⲉⲩⲛⲧⲁϥ ⲙⲙⲁⲩ ϩⲛⲧⲉϥⲥⲱϣⲉ ⲛⲟⲩⲁϩⲟ ⲉϥϩⲏⲡ ⲉϥⲟ ⲛⲁⲧⲥⲟⲟⲩⲛ ⲉⲣⲟϥ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲙⲙⲛⲛⲥⲁ ⲧⲣⲉϥⲙⲟⲩ ⲁϥⲕⲁⲁϥ ⲙⲡⲉϥϣⲏⲣⲉ ⲛⲉⲡϣⲏⲣⲉ ⲥⲟⲟⲩⲛ ⲁⲛ ⲁϥϥⲓⲧⲥⲱϣⲉ ⲉⲧⲙⲙⲁⲩ ⲁϥⲧⲁⲁⲥ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲉⲛⲧⲁϥⲧⲟⲟⲩⲥ ⲁϥⲉⲓ ⲉϥⲥⲕⲁⲓ ⲁϥϩⲉ ⲁⲡⲉϩⲟ ⲁϥⲁⲣⲭⲉⲓ ⲛϯϩⲟⲙⲧ ⲉⲧⲙⲏⲥⲉ ⲛⲛⲉⲧⲟⲩⲟϣⲟⲩ

## English draft

Jesus said: "The kingdom is like a man who had a treasure hidden in his field, and he did not know of it. And after he died, he left it to his son. The son did not know; he took the field and sold it. And the one who bought it came digging, and he found the treasure, and he began to lend at interest to those who wished."

## Notes

- "a treasure" renders ⲟⲩⲁϩⲟ; "hidden" renders the circumstantial ⲉϥϩⲏⲡ — the treasure being hidden, kept as written, not moved.
- "and he did not know of it" renders ⲉϥⲟ ⲛⲁⲧⲥⲟⲟⲩⲛ ⲉⲣⲟϥ — he being not knowing of it; the knowing-word ⲥⲟⲟⲩⲛ kept uniform across the lane.
- "after he died" renders ⲙⲙⲛⲛⲥⲁ ⲧⲣⲉϥⲙⲟⲩ — the ⲙⲛⲛⲥⲁ + causative infinitive "after his dying."
- "left it" renders ⲕⲁⲁϥ — the ⲕⲱ word (leave/bequeath) kept uniform with Logion 107's ⲕⲱ ("he left the ninety-nine").
- "lend at interest" renders ϯϩⲟⲙⲧ (give interest) — the plain carrying; the draft does not moralize the clause either way. ⲉⲧⲙⲏⲥⲉ ⲛⲛⲉⲧⲟⲩⲟϣⲟⲩ — "in loan to those who wish" (ⲟⲩⲟϣ kept uniform with the want-word).
- Heavy manuscript damage in this logion, all carried as the edition's restorations — [ⲙ]ⲙ̣ⲁⲩ, ϩ̣ⲏ̣[ⲡ, ⲙ̄[ⲙⲛ̄ⲛⲥⲁ / ⲧ]ⲣⲉ, [ϣⲏⲣⲉ / ⲛⲉ], [ⲥ / ⲉⲃⲟ]ⲗ̣, [ⲉⲛ]ⲧ, [ϥ / ϩ]ⲉ, [ⲛ / ⲉⲧ]. The draft follows the normalized edition (nothing invented beyond it); the flags stay on the page so the reader can see the text's wound-marks.
- No Greek printed or invented; Coptic alone, so stated.
- No doublets in this slice to merge.
- No parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).
- G1: checked against base sentences s0370–s0374 (chapter division ch.109, five sentences); manuscript-layer brackets/dots verified in the orig layer this run; no modern translation consulted or quoted.

# Logion 110

- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentence s0375)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉⲡⲉⲛⲧⲁϥϭⲓⲛⲉ ⲙⲡⲕⲟⲥⲙⲟⲥ ⲛϥⲣⲣⲙⲙⲁⲟ ⲙⲁⲣⲉϥⲁⲣⲛⲁ ⲙⲡⲕⲟⲥⲙⲟⲥ

## English draft

Jesus said: "Whoever has found the world and become rich — let him deny the world."

## Notes

- "found the world" renders ⲡⲉⲛⲧⲁϥϭⲓⲛⲉ ⲙⲡⲕⲟⲥⲙⲟⲥ (ϭⲓⲛⲉ̣ — the manuscript dots the letter; the normalized edition reads it plainly, and the draft follows, with the dot flagged here).
- "become rich" renders ⲛϥⲣⲣⲙⲙⲁⲟ — conjunctive "and become rich," the rich-root ⲣⲙⲙⲁⲟ echoing Logion 81's ⲣⲙⲙⲁⲟ and Logion 85's ⲙⲛⲧⲣⲙⲙⲁⲟ, kept uniform; footnoted, never merged.
- Scribal slip noted: the manuscript writes ⲛϩⲣⲣⲙⲙⲁⲟ (ⲛ̄ + ϩ + ⲣⲣⲙⲙⲁⲟ) where the normalized edition reads ⲛϥⲣⲣⲙⲙⲁⲟ (ⲛϥ- conjunctive); the draft follows the normalized edition's correction and the flag is on the page. A supplied ⲙ ([ⲙ̄]) in the same word is likewise the edition's, not invented.
- "let him deny the world" renders ⲙⲁⲣⲉϥⲁⲣⲛⲁ ⲙⲡⲕⲟⲥⲙⲟⲥ — the jussive ⲙⲁⲣⲉϥ- ("let him"); ⲁⲣⲛⲁ kept uniform with Logion 81's deny-word.
- The hard two-beat shape of the saying (find + become rich, then deny) is kept as written — the draft does not explain the tension away.
- No Greek printed or invented; Coptic alone, so stated.
- No doublets in this slice to merge.
- No parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).
- G1: checked against base sentence s0375 (chapter division ch.110, single sentence); manuscript-layer flags (ϭⲓⲛⲉ̣, [ⲙ̄], ⲛϩⲣⲣⲙⲙⲁⲟ) verified in the orig layer this run; no modern translation consulted or quoted.

# Logion 111

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0376–s0378)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉⲙⲡⲏⲩⲉ ⲛⲁϭⲱⲗ · ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲕⲁϩ ⲙⲡⲉⲧⲛⲙⲧⲟ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ

ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲉⲧⲟⲛϩ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϩⲛⲡⲉⲧⲟⲛϩ ϥⲛⲁⲛⲁⲩ ⲁⲛ ⲉⲙⲟⲩ

ⲟⲩⲭ ϩⲟⲧⲓ ⲉ ⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲱ ⲙⲙⲟⲥ ϫⲉⲡⲉⲧⲛⲁϩⲉ ⲉⲣⲟϥ ⲟⲩⲁⲁϥ ⲡⲕⲟⲥⲙⲟⲥ ⲙⲡϣⲁ ⲙⲙⲟϥ ⲁⲛ

## English draft

Jesus said: "The heavens will be rolled up, and the earth will pass away. And the living from the living will not see death." Does not Jesus say: 'Whoever finds himself — the world is not worthy of him'?

## Notes

- "rolled up" renders ϫⲱⲗ (ϫⲱⲗ — fold/roll up, as a scroll; fork: "folded together" named, not chosen).
- "pass away" renders ⲙⲡⲉⲧⲛⲙⲧⲟ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ (ⲙⲧⲟ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ — come to an end/pass away).
- "the living from the living" renders ⲡⲉⲧⲟⲛϩ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϩⲛⲡⲉⲧⲟⲛϩ — the articular relative is singular ("the living one from the living one"); the draft keeps the plain floor and the fork is on the page, not smoothed away.
- "will not see death" renders ϥⲛⲁⲛⲁⲩ ⲁⲛ ⲉⲙⲟⲩ — a different verb (ⲛⲁⲩ "see") from the "taste death" formula kept uniform at Logia 1/18/19/85; kept distinct, never harmonized.
- ⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ placement fork: the name sits inside the rhetorical question ("ⲟⲩⲭ ϩⲟⲧⲓ ⲉ ⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲱ ⲙⲙⲟⲥ"). The draft reads it as the embedded saying's speaker-tag ("Jesus:"); the vocative reading ("Is it not fitting to say, O Jesus, ...") is the named fork. Either way the saying's words are untouched.
- ⲡⲉⲧⲛⲁϩⲉ ⲉⲣⲟϥ: "whoever will find" (futurum); ⲟⲩⲁⲁϥ = reflexive "himself" kept.
- "not worthy of him" renders ⲙⲡϣⲁ ⲙⲙⲟϥ ⲁⲛ — the ⲙⲡϣⲁ construction, kept uniform with Logion 114 below.
- Manuscript layer: verse 1's ⲁ̣ⲩ̣ⲱ is dotted in the manuscript and ⲉⲃⲟⲗ⳿ carries the punctuation mark; the normalized edition reads both plainly and the draft follows, with the flags on the page.
- Find-the-world echo: the closing line answers Logion 110 ("whoever has found the world") across the gap — footnoted, never merged.
- No Greek printed or invented; Coptic alone, so stated.
- No parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).
- G1: checked against base sentences s0376–s0378 (chapter division ch.111, three sentences); quoted Coptic verified token-for-token against the base normalized text this run; manuscript-layer flags verified in the orig layer; no modern translation consulted or quoted.
- RULING 2026-10-12 (Gemini audit Flag 2, verified): the rhetorical frame ⲟⲩⲭ ϩⲟⲧⲓ ⲉ ⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲱ ⲙⲙⲟⲥ is now rendered 'Does not Jesus say: ...?' — the earlier draft embedded a speaker tag ('Jesus:') inside the quotation; the logion's own words are unchanged.

# Logion 112

- **Witness status:** Coptic-only
- **Parallels:** PENDING ROBERT
- **State:** DRAFTED (G1 self-audit done — see notes)

## Greek

No Greek witness — Coptic alone.

## Coptic (Coptic Scriptorium `thomas.gospel` v6.2.0, CC BY 4.0; base sentences s0379–s0380)

ⲡⲉϫⲉⲓⲏⲥⲟⲩⲥ ϫⲉⲟⲩⲟⲉⲓ ⲛⲧⲥⲁⲣⲝ ⲧⲁⲓ ⲉⲧⲟϣⲉ ⲛⲧⲯⲩⲭⲏ

ⲟⲩⲟⲉⲓ ⲛⲧⲯⲩⲭⲏ ⲧⲁⲓ ⲉⲧⲟϣⲉ ⲛⲧⲥⲁⲣⲝ

## English draft

Jesus said: "Woe to the flesh that depends on the soul; woe to the soul that depends on the flesh."

## Notes

- "Woe" renders ⲟⲩⲟⲉⲓ — kept uniform with Logion 102.
- "flesh" renders ⲧⲥⲁⲣⲝ (σάρξ) — kept distinct from ⲡⲥⲱⲙⲁ "body" (σῶμα), per the Logion 29 discipline (slice 6).
- "soul" renders ⲧⲯⲩⲭⲏ (ψυχή) — uniform with Logia 25, 28, 59.
- "depends on" renders ⲟϣⲉ (hang on / depend on) — kept uniform with Logion 87's ⲁϣⲉ.
- The two mirror halves are kept in the manuscript's order (flesh-first), and the chiasmus is left to stand on its own — the draft does not resolve which side is the greater woe.
- No Greek printed or invented; Coptic alone, so stated.
- No doublets in this slice to merge.
- No parallel citations added (PENDING ROBERT).
- G1: checked against base sentences s0379–s0380 (chapter division ch.112, two sentences); quoted Coptic verified token-for-token against the base normalized text this run; no modern translation consulted or quoted.
